```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/e2e/demo_e2e_full_jtd_portfolio_mf_chi_streaming.py
"""
End-to-End Mixed JTD Credit Engine Demo.

Exercises the full JTD model set in one portfolio (forced-default, independent
and stress-regime Bernoulli, beta-binomial, single-factor Gaussian and Student-t
copulas, and the multi-factor Gaussian and Student-t copulas) through the
bundle ingest path:
    Raw DataFrames -> JtdIngestBundle -> Compilation -> Factor Generator
    -> Streaming Engine (batched, no full (N, S)) -> Metrics

"""

from __future__ import annotations

import time

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.aggregate.summary import (
    streaming_model_tail_var_es,
    summarize_streaming_jtd_loss,
)
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.mixed import MixedCreditFactorGenerator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.compile import compile_jtd_portfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.jtd.streaming import run_jtd_streaming_from_generator
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditMasters,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.ingest import (
    compile_credit_factor_universe,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.ingest import compile_obligor_master
from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_df, print_success_message

# ============================================================
# 1. SETUP & CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================

NUM_OBLIGORS = 1_000
NUM_SCENARIOS = 100_000

# Distribution of models
N_FORCED_DEFAULT = int(0.05 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Forced Default
_OBLIGORS_REMAINING = NUM_OBLIGORS - N_FORCED_DEFAULT

# Distribute the remaining across 5 models
_FACTOR = 1 / 7
N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # Independent Bernoulli
N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # Stress Regime
N_BETA_BINOMIAL = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # Beta Binomial
N_GAUSS = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # SF Gaussian Copula
N_T_COP = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # SF Student-t Copula
N_MF_GAUSSIAN = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # Multi-Factor Gaussian Copula
N_MF_STUDENTS_T = int(_FACTOR * _OBLIGORS_REMAINING)  # Multi-Factor Student's T Copula

_ASSINGNED = (
    N_FORCED_DEFAULT
    + N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
    + N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
    + N_BETA_BINOMIAL
    + N_GAUSS
    + N_T_COP
    + N_MF_GAUSSIAN
    + N_MF_STUDENTS_T
)

# Correct rounding remainders if any (not strictly needed for 1000, but good practice)
if _ASSINGNED != NUM_OBLIGORS:
    N_BETA_BINOMIAL += NUM_OBLIGORS - _ASSINGNED

GLOBAL_T_COP_NU = 5.0  # Degrees of freedom for the common T-Copula chi-square factor

# Systemic sector factors the multi-factor obligors load on (independent normals
# in the universe; any cross-sector correlation is absorbed into the loadings).
MF_SECTOR_FACTORS = ("SECTOR_ENERGY", "SECTOR_FINANCIALS", "SECTOR_TECH")

MF_CHI_SECTOR_NUS = {
    "CHI_SECTOR_ENERGY": 5.0,
    "CHI_SECTOR_FINANCIALS": 5.0,
    "CHI_SECTOR_TECH": 5.0,
}


def _plot_portfolio_losses(
    portfolio_losses: np.ndarray,
    expected_loss: float,
    credit_var_99: float,
    worst_loss: float,
    credit_es_99: float,
) -> None:
    """Plots the aggregated portfolio loss distribution and risk metrics."""
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    import matplotlib.ticker as ticker

    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 6))

    losses_m = portfolio_losses / 1e6
    el_m = expected_loss / 1e6
    credit_var_99_m = credit_var_99 / 1e6
    worst_m = worst_loss / 1e6

    ax.hist(
        losses_m,
        bins=150,
        color="#3a7ca5",
        edgecolor="#294d69",
        alpha=0.8,
        density=True,
    )

    ax.axvline(
        el_m,
        color="#2ca02c",
        linestyle="dashed",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"Expected Loss (EL): ${el_m:,.1f}M",
    )
    ax.axvline(
        credit_var_99_m,
        color="#d62728",
        linestyle="solid",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit VaR: ${credit_var_99_m:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.axvline(
        credit_es_99 / 1e6,
        color="orange",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit ES: ${credit_es_99 / 1e6:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.set_title("E2E JTD Portfolio Loss Distribution", fontsize=14, pad=15)
    ax.set_xlabel("Simulated Portfolio Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Density", fontsize=12)

    ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}M"))
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.15)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=11)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


def _plot_model_distributions(model_losses: dict) -> None:
    """Plots the loss distributions of the individual JTD models on separate axes."""
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    import matplotlib.ticker as ticker

    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    # Grid sized to the model count (4 per row).
    n_models = len(model_losses)
    ncols = 4
    nrows = (n_models + ncols - 1) // ncols
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(
        nrows=nrows, ncols=ncols, figsize=(6 * ncols, 5 * nrows), layout="constrained"
    )
    fig.suptitle("Component Loss Distributions by Model", fontsize=18)

    axes = axes.flatten()
    for ax in axes[n_models:]:
        ax.set_visible(False)

    for ax, (model_name, losses) in zip(axes, model_losses.items()):
        losses_m = losses / 1e6

        # Plot the histogram (gracefully handling zero-variance/no-loss edge cases)
        ax.hist(
            losses_m,
            bins=100 if np.var(losses_m) > 0 else 10,
            color="#3a7ca5",
            edgecolor="#294d69",
            alpha=0.8,
            density=True,
        )

        # Calculate metrics for the lines
        el_m = losses_m.mean()
        var_99_m = np.quantile(losses_m, 0.99)
        es_99_m = (
            losses_m[losses_m >= var_99_m].mean()
            if np.any(losses_m >= var_99_m)
            else 0.0
        )

        # Draw risk metric lines
        ax.axvline(
            el_m,
            color="#2ca02c",
            linestyle="dashed",
            linewidth=2.5,
            label=f"EL: ${el_m:,.1f}M",
        )
        ax.axvline(
            var_99_m,
            color="#d62728",
            linestyle="solid",
            linewidth=2.5,
            label=f"99% VaR: ${var_99_m:,.1f}M",
        )
        ax.axvline(
            es_99_m,
            color="orange",
            linestyle="solid",
            linewidth=2.5,
            label=f"99% ES: ${es_99_m:,.1f}M",
        )

        ax.set_title(model_name, fontsize=14, pad=10)
        ax.set_xlabel("Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=10)
        ax.set_ylabel("Density", fontsize=10)
        ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}M"))

        ax.grid(True, alpha=0.15)
        ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=10)

    # Call standard tight_layout first to handle the internal subplot labels
    plt.tight_layout()

    # Then explicitly carve out 10% of the top of the figure for the suptitle,
    # and add a bit of breathing room between the rows/columns.
    fig.subplots_adjust(top=0.90, hspace=0.3, wspace=0.2)

    plt.show()


def run_demo() -> None:
    print_calling_file()
    print("--- S-TIER COPULA JTD ENGINE E2E DEMO ---")
    print(f"Obligors:  {NUM_OBLIGORS}")
    print(f"Scenarios: {NUM_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print(
        f"Model Mix: \n"
        f" - {N_FORCED_DEFAULT} Forced-Default \n"
        f" - {N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI} Independent Bernoulli \n"
        f" - {N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI} Stress Regime Bernoulli \n"
        f" - {N_BETA_BINOMIAL} Beta Binomial \n"
        f" - {N_GAUSS} Single-Factor Gaussian \n"
        f" - {N_T_COP} Single-Factor Student-t \n"
        f" - {N_MF_GAUSSIAN} Multi-Factor Gaussian \n"
        f" - {N_MF_STUDENTS_T} Multi-Factor Student's T"
    )
    print("-" * 41 + "\n")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # ============================================================
    # 2. INGEST: OBLIGOR MASTER
    # ============================================================
    print("[1/5] Building & Compiling Obligor Master...")

    obligor_uids = np.arange(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    rng.shuffle(obligor_uids)  # Shuffle to prove the compiler sorts it

    # Define models early so we can isolate the Non-Forced-Defaults
    models = (
        [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_GAUSS
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_T_COP
        + [JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL.name] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_MF_GAUSSIAN
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_MF_STUDENTS_T
    )
    models_array = np.array(models)
    non_fd_mask = models_array != JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name

    # --- OBLIGOR PDS --- #

    # Base PDs (Individual random generation, kept for forced defaults)
    _obligor_pds = rng.lognormal(mean=np.log(0.02), sigma=10.0, size=NUM_OBLIGORS)
    _obligor_pds = np.clip(_obligor_pds, 0.0001, 0.50)

    # Define a concentration parameter.
    kappa = 100.0

    # Calculate vectorized alpha and beta arrays per obligor
    _alphas = _obligor_pds * kappa
    _betas = (1.0 - _obligor_pds) * kappa

    # Draw completely smooth, continuously overdispersed probabilities
    _obligor_pds = rng.beta(_alphas, _betas)

    # --- OBLIGOR LGDS --- #
    _obligor_lgds = rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75, NUM_OBLIGORS)

    # --- APPLY HOMOGENEOUS NON-FORCED DEFAULT OVERRIDE --- #
    # Generate exactly ONE safe, strictly positive random PD and LGD
    shared_pd = rng.uniform(0.01, 0.05)  # e.g., a random PD between 1% and 5%
    shared_lgd = rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75)

    # Apply the single PD and LGD to everyone who is NOT a forced default
    _obligor_pds[non_fd_mask] = shared_pd
    _obligor_lgds[non_fd_mask] = shared_lgd

    obligor_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,
            "pd": _obligor_pds,
            "lgd": _obligor_lgds,
            "rating_id": 0,
            "sector_id": 0,
            "country_id": 0,
            "parent_uid": None,  # Ultimates
        }
    )

    obligor_master = compile_obligor_master(obligor_df)

    # ============================================================
    # 3. INGEST: JTD PORTFOLIO
    # ============================================================
    print("[2/5] Building & Compiling JTD Portfolio...")

    models = (
        [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_GAUSS
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_T_COP
        + [JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL.name] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_MF_GAUSSIAN
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_MF_STUDENTS_T
    )

    factor_names = (
        ["GLOBAL_CREDIT"]
        + [f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"]
        + [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"]
        + ["GLOBAL_BETA_PD"]
        + list(MF_SECTOR_FACTORS)
        + list(MF_CHI_SECTOR_NUS.keys())
    )

    # --- CALIBRATE THE BETA BINOMIAL PARAMETERS ---
    _obligor_mean_pd = float(shared_pd)

    # Guardrail 1: Define the absolute mathematical ceiling for variance
    max_theoretical_var = _obligor_mean_pd * (1.0 - _obligor_mean_pd)

    # Guardrail 2: Inject synthetic variance to prevent divide-by-zero.
    # Because all non-forced PDs are identical, actual variance is 0.0.
    # We set variance to 2% of the maximum limit to create moderate contagion.
    _obligor_var_pd = 0.02 * max_theoretical_var

    total = (max_theoretical_var / _obligor_var_pd) - 1
    alpha_mom = _obligor_mean_pd * total
    beta_mom = (1.0 - _obligor_mean_pd) * total

    # Guardrail 3: Prevent alpha < 1.0 which causes unstable U-shaped density spikes
    if alpha_mom < 1.0:
        alpha_mom = 1.5
        beta_mom = alpha_mom * ((1.0 - _obligor_mean_pd) / _obligor_mean_pd)

    print(
        f"\nCalibrated Beta-Binomial parameters to match shared obligor PD ({100 * _obligor_mean_pd:.2f}%):"
        f"\n- alpha: {alpha_mom:>8.2f}"
        f"\n- beta:  {beta_mom:>8.2f}"
    )

    factor_universe_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "factor_id": range(len(factor_names)),
            "factor_name": factor_names,
            "factor_kind": [
                "SYSTEMIC"
                if (n == "GLOBAL_CREDIT" or n.startswith("SECTOR_"))
                else "CHI"
                if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_"))
                else "UNIFORM"
                if n.startswith("U_")
                else "STRESS_INDICATOR"
                if n == "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"
                else "BETA_PD"
                if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD"
                else "IDIOSYNCRATIC"
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            "nu": [
                GLOBAL_T_COP_NU if n == "GLOBAL_CHI" else MF_CHI_SECTOR_NUS.get(n)
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            # Use the new shape parameters we locked in earlier to avoid beta naming collision
            "alpha": [
                alpha_mom if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD" else None for n in factor_names
            ],
            "beta": [beta_mom if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD" else None for n in factor_names],
        }
    )
    universe = compile_credit_factor_universe(factor_universe_df)

    jtd_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,  # Unsorted, matches the raw frame
            "instrument": models,
            "factor_loading": rng.uniform(0.1, 0.5, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "systemic_factor": "GLOBAL_CREDIT",  # Broadcast to all
            "idiosyncratic_factor": [
                f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)
            ],
            "uniform_factor": [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)],
            "stress_regime_factor": "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME",
            "stress_pd_multiplier": rng.uniform(1.25, 3.0, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "beta_factor": "GLOBAL_BETA_PD",  # Will broadcast. Non-Beta-Binomial builders safely ignore it.
        }
    )

    # Multi-factor Student-t obligors: each gets the sector chi-square factor for
    # the sector it loads on (per-sector tail dependence), with nu reconciling to
    # that chi factor's reference nu in the universe.
    _mft_sectors = [
        MF_SECTOR_FACTORS[i % len(MF_SECTOR_FACTORS)] for i in range(N_MF_STUDENTS_T)
    ]
    _mft_chi = ["CHI_" + sector for sector in _mft_sectors]
    _mft_nu = [MF_CHI_SECTOR_NUS[chi] for chi in _mft_chi]

    # Add T-Copula specific columns
    chi_factors = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
        + [None] * N_MF_GAUSSIAN
        + _mft_chi
    )
    nus = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + [GLOBAL_T_COP_NU] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
        + [None] * N_MF_GAUSSIAN
        + _mft_nu
    )

    jtd_df["chi_squared_factor"] = chi_factors
    jtd_df["nu"] = nus

    # --- MULTI-FACTOR LOADINGS (long format, one row per obligor x systemic) ---
    # Every MF obligor loads on GLOBAL_CREDIT plus one sector; sum beta^2 < 1
    # (room for the idiosyncratic residual), with the sector assigned round-robin.
    mf_uids = jtd_df.loc[
        jtd_df["instrument"] == JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN.name, "uid"
    ].to_numpy()
    _n_mf = mf_uids.shape[0]
    _global_betas = rng.uniform(0.20, 0.40, _n_mf)
    _sector_betas = rng.uniform(0.30, 0.60, _n_mf)
    _sectors = np.array(
        [MF_SECTOR_FACTORS[i % len(MF_SECTOR_FACTORS)] for i in range(_n_mf)],
        dtype=object,
    )
    mf_loadings_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "obligor_uid": np.concatenate([mf_uids, mf_uids]),
            "factor_name": np.concatenate(
                [np.array(["GLOBAL_CREDIT"] * _n_mf, dtype=object), _sectors]
            ),
            "factor_loading": np.concatenate([_global_betas, _sector_betas]),
        }
    )

    # Multi-factor Student-t obligors load the same way (GLOBAL_CREDIT + one
    # sector); their sector here must match the one driving their chi factor.
    mft_uids = jtd_df.loc[
        jtd_df["instrument"] == JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T.name, "uid"
    ].to_numpy()
    _n_mft = mft_uids.shape[0]
    _mft_global_betas = rng.uniform(0.20, 0.40, _n_mft)
    _mft_sector_betas = rng.uniform(0.30, 0.60, _n_mft)
    _mft_sectors_arr = np.array(_mft_sectors, dtype=object)
    mft_loadings_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "obligor_uid": np.concatenate([mft_uids, mft_uids]),
            "factor_name": np.concatenate(
                [np.array(["GLOBAL_CREDIT"] * _n_mft, dtype=object), _mft_sectors_arr]
            ),
            "factor_loading": np.concatenate([_mft_global_betas, _mft_sector_betas]),
        }
    )
    mf_loadings_df = pd.concat([mf_loadings_df, mft_loadings_df], ignore_index=True)

    print_df(
        jtd_df.head(n=10),
        title="Sample (head) JTD Portfolio DataFrame (Unsorted)",
    )
    print_df(
        jtd_df.tail(n=10),
        title="Sample (tail) JTD Portfolio DataFrame (Unsorted)",
    )

    bundle = JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(
        jtd_df, mf_loadings_df=mf_loadings_df
    )
    _masters = CompiledCreditMasters(
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
        factor_universe=universe,
    )
    compiled_portfolio = compile_jtd_portfolio(
        bundle=bundle,
        masters=_masters,
    )

    compiled_portfolio.summary()

    # ============================================================
    # 4. EAD PROVIDER (STREAMED PER BATCH, ALIGNED TO COMPILED PORTFOLIO)
    # ============================================================
    print("[3/5] Preparing streamed Exposure provider...")

    # Per-obligor base exposure is fixed; the scenario dispersion is drawn per
    # batch so EAD never materialises as a full (N, S) matrix. The batch RNG is
    # seeded from the scenario offset, keeping the run deterministic for a given
    # batch size (each scenario is drawn exactly once — batches partition them).
    base_ead = rng.uniform(1e6, 10e6, (NUM_OBLIGORS, 1))

    def ead_provider(scenario_offset: int, batch_size: int) -> np.ndarray:
        batch_rng = np.random.default_rng(RNG_SEED + 9_000_000 + scenario_offset)
        noise = batch_rng.lognormal(
            mean=0.0, sigma=0.1, size=(NUM_OBLIGORS, batch_size)
        )
        return base_ead * noise

    # ============================================================
    # 5. FACTOR GENERATOR (streamed; no full (F, S) matrix)
    # ============================================================
    print("[4/5] Building Credit Factor generator...")

    factor_generator = MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe(
        universe,
        total_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
        seed=RNG_SEED,
        stress_p_by_uix={int(u): 0.05 for u in universe.stress_indicator_uix},
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 6. STREAMING ENGINE EXECUTION
    # ============================================================
    print("[5/5] Executing `run_jtd_streaming_from_generator`...")

    start_time = time.perf_counter()

    streaming_result = run_jtd_streaming_from_generator(
        jtd_portfolio=compiled_portfolio,
        factor_generator=factor_generator,
        ead_provider=ead_provider,
        batch_size=min(NUM_SCENARIOS, 25_000),
        collect_by_model=True,
    )

    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time
    print(
        f"Streaming engine accumulated {NUM_OBLIGORS * NUM_SCENARIOS:,} "
        f"evaluations in {elapsed:.4f}s without materialising (N, S)"
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 7. METRICS & ANALYSIS
    # ============================================================
    print("\n--- Portfolio Credit Risk Metrics ---")

    portfolio_losses = streaming_result.portfolio_loss

    summary = summarize_streaming_jtd_loss(result=streaming_result, alphas=(0.99,))
    expected_loss = summary.expected_loss
    credit_var_99 = summary.var[0.99]
    credit_es_99 = summary.es[0.99]

    print(f"Expected Loss (EL):  ${expected_loss:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.0%):  ${credit_var_99:,.2f}")
    print(f"Worst Scenario Loss: ${np.max(portfolio_losses):,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit ES (99.0%): ${credit_es_99:,.2f}")

    # 8. PLOTS
    _plot_portfolio_losses(
        portfolio_losses,
        expected_loss,
        credit_var_99,
        np.max(portfolio_losses),
        credit_es_99,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # ATTRIBUTION (streaming retains portfolio + per-model aggregates only)
    # ============================================================
    # Per-obligor attribution (top contributors) and default-count statistics
    # need the full (N, S) loss matrix, which the streaming path deliberately
    # never holds. Use `run_jtd` when obligor-level detail is required; here we
    # attribute at the model level from the collected (n_models, S) breakdown.

    print("\n--- Per-Model Tail Attribution (99%, standalone) ---")
    per_model_tail = streaming_model_tail_var_es(result=streaming_result, alpha=0.99)
    ranked = sorted(per_model_tail.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1][1], reverse=True)
    for rank, (model_name, (m_var, m_es)) in enumerate(ranked, start=1):
        print(f"{rank:2d}. {model_name:<26} VaR: ${m_var:>15,.2f}  ES: ${m_es:>15,.2f}")
    print(
        "\n(Per-obligor VaR/ES attribution and default-count statistics require "
        "the materialising `run_jtd` path — they are not available when streaming.)"
    )

    # ============================================================
    # MODEL COMPARISON: FORCED-DEFAULT VS OTHERS
    # ============================================================

    # Per-model per-scenario loss comes straight from the streaming breakdown
    # (rows aligned to streaming_result.model_order); no (N, S) masking needed.
    _row_of = {mid: i for i, mid in enumerate(streaming_result.model_order)}
    n_scen = portfolio_losses.shape[0]

    def _model_losses(model: JtdModel) -> np.ndarray:
        row = _row_of.get(int(model))
        if row is None:
            return np.zeros(n_scen)
        return streaming_result.model_loss[row]

    forced_default_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT)
    independent_bernoulli_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI)
    stress_regime_bernoulli_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI)
    beta_binomial_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL)
    gaussian_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN)
    t_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T)
    mf_gaussian_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN)
    mf_students_t_losses = _model_losses(JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T)

    def _calc_metrics(losses):
        el = losses.mean()
        var = np.quantile(losses, 0.99)
        es = losses[losses >= var].mean()
        return el, var, es

    fd_el, fd_var, fd_es = _calc_metrics(forced_default_losses)
    ib_el, ib_var, ib_es = _calc_metrics(independent_bernoulli_losses)
    srb_el, srb_var, srb_es = _calc_metrics(stress_regime_bernoulli_losses)
    bb_el, bb_var, bb_es = _calc_metrics(beta_binomial_losses)
    gauss_el, gauss_var, gauss_es = _calc_metrics(gaussian_losses)
    t_el, t_var, t_es = _calc_metrics(t_losses)
    mfg_el, mfg_var, mfg_es = _calc_metrics(mf_gaussian_losses)
    mft_el, mft_var, mft_es = _calc_metrics(mf_students_t_losses)

    print("\n" + "=" * 91)
    print(f"{'MODEL COMPARISON SUMMARY':^91}")
    print("=" * 91)
    print(
        f"{'Model Type':<26} | {'Expected Loss (EL)':>18} | {'99% Credit VaR':>18} | {'99% Credit ES':>18}"
    )
    print("-" * 91)
    print(
        f"{'Forced-Default':<26} | ${fd_el:>17,.2f} | ${fd_var:>17,.2f} | ${fd_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Independent Bernoulli':<26} | ${ib_el:>17,.2f} | ${ib_var:>17,.2f} | ${ib_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Stress Regime Bernoulli':<26} | ${srb_el:>17,.2f} | ${srb_var:>17,.2f} | ${srb_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Beta Binomial':<26} | ${bb_el:>17,.2f} | ${bb_var:>17,.2f} | ${bb_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Gaussian Copula':<26} | ${gauss_el:>17,.2f} | ${gauss_var:>17,.2f} | ${gauss_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Student-t Copula':<26} | ${t_el:>17,.2f} | ${t_var:>17,.2f} | ${t_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Multi-Factor Gaussian':<26} | ${mfg_el:>17,.2f} | ${mfg_var:>17,.2f} | ${mfg_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Multi-Factor Student-t':<26} | ${mft_el:>17,.2f} | ${mft_var:>17,.2f} | ${mft_es:>17,.2f}"
    )

    print("=" * 91)

    # --- PLOT DISTRIBUTIONS PER MODEL ---
    model_losses_dict = {
        "Forced-Default": forced_default_losses,
        "Independent Bernoulli": independent_bernoulli_losses,
        "Stress Regime Bernoulli": stress_regime_bernoulli_losses,
        "Beta Binomial": beta_binomial_losses,
        "Gaussian Copula": gaussian_losses,
        "Student-t Copula": t_losses,
        "Multi-Factor Gaussian": mf_gaussian_losses,
        "Multi-Factor Student-t": mf_students_t_losses,
    }
    _plot_model_distributions(model_losses_dict)

    print_success_message()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```
</file>

```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/evaluators/demo_sf_copula_gaussian.py
"""Demonstration of the S-Tier Gaussian Copula JTD Evaluator with synthetic data."""

import time
from dataclasses import dataclass

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# ============================================================
# IMPORT PRODUCTION MODULES
# ============================================================
from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_gaussian import SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.sf_copula_gaussian import (
    evaluate_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd,
)

# --- 1. Scale Parameters ---
M_OBLIGORS = 100  # 100 Counterparties
S_SCENARIOS = 5_000  # 50,000 Monte Carlo Paths

# 1 Systemic Factor + M Idiosyncratic Factors
F_FACTORS = 1 + M_OBLIGORS


# Mocking the state container since it sits outside the provided context
@dataclass
class MockCreditFactorMatrix:
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]


def run_demo() -> None:
    print("--- S-TIER GAUSSIAN COPULA EVALUATOR DEMO ---\n")
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # --- 2. Allocate & Generate Data (The Input) ---
    print(f"Allocating Memory for {M_OBLIGORS} Obligors x {S_SCENARIOS} Scenarios...")

    # EAD Matrix: (M, S). Simulating constant $1M to $5M exposures for simplicity
    ead_base = rng.uniform(1e6, 5e6, (M_OBLIGORS, 1))
    ead = (
        np.broadcast_to(ead_base, (M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS))
        .astype(np.float64)
        .copy(order="C")
    )

    # Latent Factors: (F, S). Standard Normal Draws.
    latent_factors = rng.standard_normal((F_FACTORS, S_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64)

    # Instantiate the real production container
    factor_matrix = CreditFactorMatrix(latent_factors=latent_factors)
    # ...

    # Obligor Parameters
    obligor_uids = np.arange(1000, 1000 + M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int64)
    lgd = rng.uniform(0.4, 0.6, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)  # 40% to 60% LGD
    beta = rng.uniform(0.3, 0.7, M_OBLIGORS).astype(
        np.float64
    )  # Correlation to systemic factor

    # Thresholds: ~1% to ~5% Probability of Default mapped to inverse normal CDF
    # norm.ppf(0.01) = -2.33, norm.ppf(0.05) = -1.64
    thresholds = rng.uniform(-2.5, -1.6, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)

    # Uix (Pointers mapping obligors to factor matrix rows)
    sys_uix = np.empty(
        M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int32
    )  # All point to row 0 (The Market Factor)
    idio_uix = np.arange(1, 1 + M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int32)  # Point to rows 1 to M

    # Assemble the JTD Block
    block = SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd,
        thresholds=thresholds,
        factor_loading=beta,
        systemic_uix=sys_uix,
        idiosyncratic_uix=idio_uix,
    )

    print("\n--- Block Structure ---")
    block.print_block(max_items=5)

    # Pre-allocate contiguous output memory
    out_loss = np.empty((M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64)

    # --- 3. The Evaluator Call (JIT Boundary) ---
    print("Evaluating Jump-To-Default Losses...")
    start_time = time.perf_counter()

    evaluate_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
        block=block,
        ead=ead,
        credit_factors=factor_matrix,
        out=out_loss,
    )

    print(f"Evaluator completed in {time.perf_counter() - start_time:.4f}s\n")

    # --- 4. Reduction & Metrics (The Output) ---
    # Collapse the (M, S) obligor losses into an (S,) array of Portfolio Losses
    portfolio_losses = np.sum(out_loss, axis=0)

    expected_loss = np.mean(portfolio_losses)
    cvar_99 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.99)
    cvar_999 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.999)

    print("--- Portfolio Credit Risk Metrics ---")
    print(f"Expected Loss (EL):  ${expected_loss:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.0%):  ${cvar_99:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.9%):  ${cvar_999:,.2f}")
    print(f"Worst Scenario Loss: ${np.max(portfolio_losses):,.2f}")

    # --- 5. Visualization ---
    plot_copula_results(portfolio_losses, expected_loss, cvar_99)


def plot_copula_results(
    portfolio_losses: np.ndarray, el: float, cvar_99: float
) -> None:
    """Plots the right-tailed loss distribution characteristic of copula models."""
    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 6))

    # Plot histogram of losses (ignoring zero-loss scenarios for clarity if desired,
    # but for portfolio level, we plot all)
    counts, bins, patches = ax.hist(
        portfolio_losses / 1e6,  # Convert to Millions for readable axis
        bins=100,
        color="#1f77b4",
        edgecolor="black",
        alpha=0.8,
    )

    # Add vertical lines for Risk Metrics
    ax.axvline(
        el / 1e6,
        color="#2ca02c",
        linestyle="dashed",
        linewidth=2,
        label=f"Expected Loss (${el / 1e6:.1f}M)",
    )
    ax.axvline(
        cvar_99 / 1e6,
        color="#d62728",
        linestyle="solid",
        linewidth=2,
        label=f"99% Credit VaR (${cvar_99 / 1e6:.1f}M)",
    )

    ax.set_title(
        "Portfolio Loss Distribution (Single-Factor Gaussian Copula)",
        fontsize=14,
        pad=15,
    )
    ax.set_xlabel("Simulated Portfolio Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Frequency (Scenarios)", fontsize=12)
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.2)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=11)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\credit\jtd\evaluators\demo_sf_copula_students_t.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/evaluators/demo_sf_copula_students_t.py
"""Demonstration of the S-Tier Student-t Copula JTD Evaluator with synthetic data."""

import time

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

# ============================================================
# IMPORT PRODUCTION MODULES
# ============================================================
from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_students_t import SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.sf_copula_students_t import (
    evaluate_sf_students_t_copula_jtd,
)

# --- 1. Scale Parameters ---
M_OBLIGORS = 100  # 100 Counterparties
S_SCENARIOS = 50_000  # 50,000 Monte Carlo Paths

# 1 Systemic Factor + M Idiosyncratic Factors + M Chi-Squared Factors
F_FACTORS = 1 + (2 * M_OBLIGORS)


def run_demo() -> None:
    print("--- S-TIER STUDENT-T COPULA EVALUATOR DEMO ---\n")
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # --- 2. Allocate & Generate Data (The Input) ---
    print(f"Allocating Memory for {M_OBLIGORS} Obligors x {S_SCENARIOS} Scenarios...")

    # EAD Matrix: (M, S). Simulating constant $1M to $5M exposures
    ead_base = rng.uniform(1e6, 5e6, (M_OBLIGORS, 1))
    ead = (
        np.broadcast_to(ead_base, (M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS))
        .astype(np.float64)
        .copy(order="C")
    )

    # Obligor Parameters
    obligor_uids = np.arange(1000, 1000 + M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int64)
    lgd = rng.uniform(0.4, 0.6, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)
    beta = rng.uniform(0.3, 0.7, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)

    # Degrees of Freedom (nu): Lower values (e.g., 4 to 6) create extremely fat tails
    nu = rng.uniform(4.0, 8.0, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)

    # Thresholds: Mapped to inverse CDF (approximate -2.5 to -1.6)
    thresholds = rng.uniform(-2.5, -1.6, M_OBLIGORS).astype(np.float64)

    # --- Latent Factor Generation (The Mathematical Setup) ---
    # 1. Systemic Factor (Z ~ N(0,1))
    Z = rng.standard_normal((1, S_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64)

    # 2. Idiosyncratic Factors (Eps ~ N(0,1))
    eps = rng.standard_normal((M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64)

    # 3. Chi-Squared Factors (W ~ Chi^2(nu))
    # Note: We pass the obligor-specific `nu` array to generate df-matched Chi-sq draws
    W = rng.chisquare(df=nu[:, None], size=(M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS))

    # CRITICAL: The kernel expects 1 / sqrt(W) precomputed to avoid inner-loop division
    inv_sqrt_w = (1.0 / np.sqrt(W)).astype(np.float64)

    # Stack into a single C-contiguous factor matrix
    latent_factors = np.vstack([Z, eps, inv_sqrt_w]).astype(np.float64).copy(order="C")
    factor_matrix = CreditFactorMatrix(latent_factors=latent_factors)

    # --- Index Pointers (UIX) ---
    sys_uix = np.empty(M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int32)
    idio_uix = np.arange(1, 1 + M_OBLIGORS, dtype=np.int32)
    chi_uix = np.arange(1 + M_OBLIGORS, 1 + (2 * M_OBLIGORS), dtype=np.int32)

    # Assemble the JTD Block
    block = SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd,
        thresholds=thresholds,
        factor_loading=beta,
        sqrt_nu=np.sqrt(nu),
        systemic_uix=sys_uix,
        idiosyncratic_uix=idio_uix,
        chi_squared_uix=chi_uix,
    )

    print("\n--- Block Structure ---")
    block.print_block(max_items=5)

    # Pre-allocate contiguous output memory
    out_loss = np.empty((M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64)

    # --- 3. The Evaluator Call (JIT Boundary) ---
    print("Evaluating Jump-To-Default Losses (Student-t Copula)...")
    start_time = time.perf_counter()

    evaluate_sf_students_t_copula_jtd(
        block=block,
        ead=ead,
        credit_factors=factor_matrix,
        out=out_loss,
    )

    print(f"Evaluator completed in {time.perf_counter() - start_time:.4f}s\n")

    # --- 4. Reduction & Metrics (The Output) ---
    portfolio_losses = np.sum(out_loss, axis=0)

    expected_loss = np.mean(portfolio_losses)
    cvar_99 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.99)
    cvar_999 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.999)

    print("--- Portfolio Credit Risk Metrics ---")
    print(f"Expected Loss (EL):  ${expected_loss:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.0%):  ${cvar_99:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.9%):  ${cvar_999:,.2f}")
    print(f"Worst Scenario Loss: ${np.max(portfolio_losses):,.2f}")

    # --- 5. Visualization ---
    plot_copula_results(portfolio_losses, expected_loss, cvar_99)


def plot_copula_results(
    portfolio_losses: np.ndarray, el: float, cvar_99: float
) -> None:
    """Plots the right-tailed loss distribution characteristic of copula models."""
    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 6))

    counts, bins, patches = ax.hist(
        portfolio_losses / 1e6,  # Convert to Millions for readable axis
        bins=100,
        color="#8c564b",  # Distinct brown/red color for Student-t
        edgecolor="black",
        alpha=0.8,
    )

    ax.axvline(
        el / 1e6,
        color="#2ca02c",
        linestyle="dashed",
        linewidth=2,
        label=f"Expected Loss (${el / 1e6:.1f}M)",
    )
    ax.axvline(
        cvar_99 / 1e6,
        color="#d62728",
        linestyle="solid",
        linewidth=2,
        label=f"99% Credit VaR (${cvar_99 / 1e6:.1f}M)",
    )

    ax.set_title(
        "Portfolio Loss Distribution (Single-Factor Student-t Copula)",
        fontsize=14,
        pad=15,
    )
    ax.set_xlabel("Simulated Portfolio Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Frequency (Scenarios)", fontsize=12)
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.2)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=11)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\credit\jtd\kernels\demo_copula_throughput.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/kernels/demo_copula_throughput.py

import time

import numpy as np

from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED

# ============================================================
# IMPORT PRODUCTION KERNELS
# ============================================================
from riskquant.credit.jtd.kernels.sf_copula_gaussian import (
    kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.kernels.sf_copula_students_t import (
    kernel_sf_students_t_copula_jtd,
)
from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_success_message

# ============================================================
# BENCHMARK RUNNER
# ============================================================

M_OBLIGORS = 2_500
S_SCENARIOS = 200_000

NUM_SYS_FACTORS = 100
NUM_IDIO_FACTORS = M_OBLIGORS
NUM_CHI_FACTORS = M_OBLIGORS

F_FACTORS = NUM_SYS_FACTORS + NUM_IDIO_FACTORS + NUM_CHI_FACTORS

TOTAL_EVALS = M_OBLIGORS * S_SCENARIOS


def _rng():
    """Fixed seed for reproducibility."""
    return np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)


def run_benchmark():
    print_calling_file()

    # ============================================================
    # SCALE PARAMETERS
    # ============================================================

    print("--- S-TIER COPULA JTD KERNELS BENCHMARK ---")
    print(f"Obligors:     {M_OBLIGORS:,}")
    print(f"Scenarios:    {S_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print(f"Latent Pool:  {F_FACTORS:,} factors total")
    print(
        f"Total Evals:  {TOTAL_EVALS:,} Default Simulations "
        f"({TOTAL_EVALS / 1_000_000_000:.2f} Billion Evals)"
    )
    print("-" * 46)

    # ============================================================
    # MEMORY ALLOCATION
    # ============================================================

    print("Allocating engine memory...")
    print("-" * 46)

    out = np.empty(
        (M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS),
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    rng = _rng()

    # ============================================================
    # EXPOSURE PATHS
    # ============================================================

    ead = rng.uniform(
        1e6,
        10e6,
        (M_OBLIGORS, S_SCENARIOS),
    ).astype(np.float64)

    # ============================================================
    # LATENT FACTOR POOL
    # ============================================================
    #
    # Layout:
    #
    #   [ Systematic Gaussian Factors ]
    #   [ Idiosyncratic Gaussian Factors ]
    #   [ Student-T Scaling Factors ]
    #
    # IMPORTANT
    # ---------
    # The production Student-T kernel expects rows referenced
    # by chi_uix to contain:
    #
    #     inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)
    #
    # and NOT raw chi-square draws.
    #
    # This mirrors the production architecture where the
    # expensive square-root transformation is performed once
    # during factor generation rather than inside the kernel.
    #

    systematic_factors = rng.standard_normal((NUM_SYS_FACTORS, S_SCENARIOS)).astype(
        np.float64
    )

    idiosyncratic_factors = rng.standard_normal((NUM_IDIO_FACTORS, S_SCENARIOS)).astype(
        np.float64
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # Student-T scaling factors
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    #
    # For throughput benchmarking we do not require an exact
    # chi-square process. We only need positive values that
    # satisfy the production kernel contract:
    #
    #     inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)
    #
    # where W > 0.
    #

    chi_proxy = (
        np.abs(rng.standard_normal((NUM_CHI_FACTORS, S_SCENARIOS))).astype(np.float64)
        + 0.1
    )

    inv_sqrt_w = 1.0 / np.sqrt(chi_proxy)

    latent_factors = np.vstack(
        (
            systematic_factors,
            idiosyncratic_factors,
            inv_sqrt_w,
        )
    )

    # ============================================================
    # OBLIGOR PARAMETERS
    # ============================================================

    lgd = rng.uniform(
        0.2,
        0.8,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.float64)

    factor_loading = rng.uniform(
        0.1,
        0.9,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.float64)

    thresholds = rng.uniform(
        -3.0,
        -1.0,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.float64)

    # Obligor-specific Student-T degrees of freedom.
    nu = rng.uniform(
        3.0,
        10.0,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.float64)
    sqrt_nu = np.sqrt(nu)

    # ============================================================
    # FACTOR INDEX MAPPINGS
    # ============================================================

    sys_start = 0
    idio_start = NUM_SYS_FACTORS
    chi_start = NUM_SYS_FACTORS + NUM_IDIO_FACTORS

    sys_uix = rng.integers(
        sys_start,
        idio_start,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.int32)

    idio_uix = rng.integers(
        idio_start,
        chi_start,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.int32)

    chi_uix = rng.integers(
        chi_start,
        F_FACTORS,
        M_OBLIGORS,
    ).astype(np.int32)

    # ============================================================
    # VALIDATE MEMORY LAYOUT
    # ============================================================

    assert out.flags.c_contiguous
    assert ead.flags.c_contiguous
    assert latent_factors.flags.c_contiguous

    runs = 10

    # ============================================================
    # GAUSSIAN COPULA BENCHMARK
    # ============================================================

    print("\n[1] Warming up Gaussian Copula JIT...")

    start_compile = time.perf_counter()

    kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
        out=out[:10, :],
        latent_factors=latent_factors,
        ead=ead[:10, :],
        lgd=lgd[:10],
        factor_loading=factor_loading[:10],
        thresholds=thresholds[:10],
        sys_uix=sys_uix[:10],
        idio_uix=idio_uix[:10],
    )

    print(f"Compilation finished in {time.perf_counter() - start_compile:.4f}s")
    print("-" * 46)

    print(f"Executing {TOTAL_EVALS:,} Gaussian JTD Simulations...")

    total_time_gaussian = 0.0

    for r in range(runs):
        start_time = time.perf_counter()

        kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
            out=out,
            latent_factors=latent_factors,
            ead=ead,
            lgd=lgd,
            factor_loading=factor_loading,
            thresholds=thresholds,
            sys_uix=sys_uix,
            idio_uix=idio_uix,
        )

        run_time = time.perf_counter() - start_time
        total_time_gaussian += run_time

        print(f"  Run {r + 1}: {run_time:.4f} seconds")

    avg_time_gauss = total_time_gaussian / runs
    tput_gauss = TOTAL_EVALS / avg_time_gauss

    # Approximate bytes touched per evaluation.
    bw_gauss = (TOTAL_EVALS * 32 / avg_time_gauss) / (1024**3)

    print("-" * 46)
    print(f"Gaussian Avg Time:  {avg_time_gauss:.4f} seconds")
    print(f"Gaussian Throughput:{tput_gauss / 1_000_000_000:.2f} Billion Evals/sec")
    print(f"Gaussian Traffic:   {bw_gauss:.2f} GB/s")
    print("-" * 46)

    # ============================================================
    # STUDENT-T COPULA BENCHMARK
    # ============================================================

    # out.fill(0.0)

    print("\n[2] Warming up Student-T Copula JIT...")

    start_compile = time.perf_counter()

    kernel_sf_students_t_copula_jtd(
        out=out[:10, :],
        latent_factors=latent_factors,
        ead=ead[:10, :],
        lgd=lgd[:10],
        factor_loading=factor_loading[:10],
        thresholds=thresholds[:10],
        sqrt_nu=sqrt_nu[:10],
        sys_uix=sys_uix[:10],
        idio_uix=idio_uix[:10],
        chi_uix=chi_uix[:10],
    )

    print(f"Compilation finished in {time.perf_counter() - start_compile:.4f}s")
    print("-" * 46)

    print(f"Executing {TOTAL_EVALS:,} Student-T JTD Simulations...")

    total_time_t = 0.0

    for r in range(runs):
        start_time = time.perf_counter()

        kernel_sf_students_t_copula_jtd(
            out=out,
            latent_factors=latent_factors,
            ead=ead,
            lgd=lgd,
            factor_loading=factor_loading,
            thresholds=thresholds,
            sqrt_nu=sqrt_nu,
            sys_uix=sys_uix,
            idio_uix=idio_uix,
            chi_uix=chi_uix,
        )

        run_time = time.perf_counter() - start_time
        total_time_t += run_time

        print(f"  Run {r + 1}: {run_time:.4f} seconds")

    avg_time_t = total_time_t / runs
    tput_t = TOTAL_EVALS / avg_time_t

    # Approximate bytes touched per evaluation.
    bw_t = (TOTAL_EVALS * 40 / avg_time_t) / (1024**3)

    print("-" * 46)
    print(f"Student-T Avg Time:  {avg_time_t:.4f} seconds")
    print(f"Student-T Throughput:{tput_t / 1_000_000_000:.2f} Billion Evals/sec")
    print(f"Student-T Traffic:   {bw_t:.2f} GB/s")
    print("-" * 46)

    print_success_message()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_benchmark()
```

```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/validation/demo_validate_asset_correlation.py
"""
Engine Asset Correlation Validation Demo.

This script does NOT test theoretical math in a vacuum. It pushes raw DataFrames
through the actual `riskquant` compilation pipeline to prove that the
Struct-of-Arrays (SoA) memory blocks, UIX pointer resolution, and
CreditFactorMatrix structures correctly recover the theoretical correlations.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import build_credit_factor_matrix
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.mixed import MixedCreditFactorGenerator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.compile import compile_jtd_portfolio
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditMasters,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.ingest import (
    compile_credit_factor_universe,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.ingest import compile_obligor_master
from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_success_message

NUM_OBLIGORS = 100
NUM_SCENARIOS = 1_000_000
GLOBAL_T_COP_NU = 5.0


def run_demo() -> None:
    print_calling_file()
    print("--- S-TIER ENGINE CORRELATION VALIDATION ---")
    print(f"Obligors:  {NUM_OBLIGORS}")
    print(f"Scenarios: {NUM_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print()

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # ============================================================
    # 1. INGEST & COMPILE (Using Actual Codebase)
    # ============================================================

    # 1A. Obligor Master
    obligor_uids = np.arange(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    obligor_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,
            "pd": 0.10,
            "lgd": 0.60,
            "rating_id": 0,
            "sector_id": 0,
            "country_id": 0,
            "parent_uid": None,
        }
    )
    obligor_master = compile_obligor_master(obligor_df)

    # 1B. JTD Portfolio
    input_betas = rng.uniform(0.10, 0.50, NUM_OBLIGORS)
    factor_names = ["GLOBAL_CREDIT"] + [f"IDIO_{i}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
    # Compile the factor-universe MASTER from the generated factor list. At
    # validation scale this authoring table is generated (one idiosyncratic
    # factor per obligor) rather than the minimal canonical fixture, but it
    # flows through the SAME master contract: name -> UIX resolution and chi-dof
    # reconciliation now read from `universe`, never an ad-hoc {name: i} dict.
    factor_universe_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "factor_id": range(len(factor_names)),
            "factor_name": factor_names,
            "factor_kind": [
                "SYSTEMIC"
                if n == "GLOBAL_CREDIT"
                else "CHI"
                if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_"))
                else "UNIFORM"
                if n.startswith("U_")
                else "STRESS_INDICATOR"
                if n == "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"
                else "IDIOSYNCRATIC"
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            "nu": [
                GLOBAL_T_COP_NU if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_")) else None
                for n in factor_names
            ],
        }
    )
    universe = compile_credit_factor_universe(factor_universe_df)

    jtd_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name,
            "factor_loading": input_betas,
            "systemic_factor": "GLOBAL_CREDIT",
            "idiosyncratic_factor": [f"IDIO_{i}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)],
        }
    )

    # THIS IS THE CRITICAL TEST: Does the compiler correctly map the strings
    # to UIX integers and align the betas?
    _jtd_bundle = JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(jtd_df)
    _masters = CompiledCreditMasters(
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
        factor_universe=universe,
    )
    compiled_portfolio = compile_jtd_portfolio(
        bundle=_jtd_bundle,
        masters=_masters,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 2. GENERATE FACTOR MATRIX
    # ============================================================
    # === Synthesize the credit factor matrix from the universe master ===
    # The mixed generator derives its standard-normal / chi / uniform / stress
    # columns from `universe`, so every row carries the correct marginal and
    # cannot disagree with the compiled portfolio about which columns are chi
    # (or at what nu). This replaces the hand-rolled per-block buffer fill.
    factor_generator = MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe(
        universe,
        total_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
        seed=RNG_SEED,
    )
    credit_factors = build_credit_factor_matrix(
        factor_generator.iter_batches(batch_size=min(NUM_SCENARIOS, 25_000)),
        num_factors=universe.n_factors,
        num_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 3. EXTRACT LATENT VALUES VIA COMPILED BLOCK POINTERS
    # ============================================================

    # We grab the exact SoA block the engine uses
    gauss_block = compiled_portfolio.sf_gaussian_copula_jtd_block

    if gauss_block is None:
        raise ValueError("Gaussian block failed to compile.")

    # We extract the memory rows using the UIX indices resolved by the compiler
    factors = credit_factors.latent_factors

    z_rows = factors[gauss_block.systemic_uix]  # Shape: (100, 1_000_000)
    eps_rows = factors[gauss_block.idiosyncratic_uix]  # Shape: (100, 1_000_000)

    # We grab the compiled betas and broadcast them for scenario multiplication
    compiled_betas = gauss_block.factor_loading[:, None]

    # Reconstruct the continuous latent variables exactly as the kernel does
    sqrt_1mb = np.sqrt(1.0 - compiled_betas**2)
    asset_values = compiled_betas * z_rows + sqrt_1mb * eps_rows

    # ============================================================
    # 4. VALIDATE COMPILED CORRELATIONS
    # ============================================================

    # For validation, we need the 1D global Z vector.
    # Since all obligors share "GLOBAL_CREDIT", z_rows[0] is the systemic vector.
    global_z = z_rows[0]

    empirical_market_corr = np.empty(NUM_OBLIGORS)
    for i in range(NUM_OBLIGORS):
        empirical_market_corr[i] = np.corrcoef(asset_values[i], global_z)[0, 1]

    # We compare against the compiled betas, proving the memory aligned perfectly
    flat_betas = gauss_block.factor_loading
    market_corr_error = np.abs(empirical_market_corr - flat_betas)

    print("--- 1. Compiled Asset-to-Market Correlation ---")
    print(f"Mean Absolute Error: {market_corr_error.mean():.6f}")
    print(f"Max Absolute Error:  {market_corr_error.max():.6f}")
    print()

    # ============================================================
    # 5. VALIDATE CROSS-ASSET CORRELATION
    # ============================================================
    rng_pairs = np.random.default_rng(RNG_SEED)
    num_pairs = 1000

    theoretical_corr = np.empty(num_pairs)
    empirical_corr = np.empty(num_pairs)

    for k in range(num_pairs):
        i, j = rng_pairs.choice(NUM_OBLIGORS, size=2, replace=False)

        theoretical_corr[k] = flat_betas[i] * flat_betas[j]
        empirical_corr[k] = np.corrcoef(asset_values[i], asset_values[j])[0, 1]

    pair_error = np.abs(empirical_corr - theoretical_corr)

    print("--- 2. Compiled Asset-to-Asset Correlation ---")
    print(f"Mean Absolute Error: {pair_error.mean():.6f}")
    print(f"Max Absolute Error:  {pair_error.max():.6f}")
    print()

    print_success_message()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\credit\jtd\validation\demo_validate_beta_binomial_jtd_engine.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/validation/demo_validate_beta_binomial_jtd_engine.py
"""
Beta-Binomial JTD Engine Validation Demo.

Validates the BETA_BINOMIAL model end to end: a single shared per-scenario
default probability P_s ~ Beta(alpha, beta) drives conditionally-independent
obligor defaults (u_i < P_s). The checks confirm (1) the empirical mean default
rate matches the theoretical E[P_s] = alpha / (alpha + beta), and (2) the
realized default rate is strongly overdispersed versus an independent-Bernoulli
book of the same size — the defining signature of the common-shock model.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import time

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import build_credit_factor_matrix
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.mixed import MixedCreditFactorGenerator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.engine import run_jtd
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.compile import compile_jtd_portfolio
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditMasters,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.ingest import (
    compile_credit_factor_universe,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.ingest import compile_obligor_master
from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_df, print_success_message

# ============================================================
# 1. SETUP & CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================

NUM_OBLIGORS = 1_000
NUM_SCENARIOS = 100_000

# Distribution of models
N_FORCED_DEFAULT = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Forced-Default (Stress/Defaulted)
N_GAUSS = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Gaussian Copula
N_T_COP = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Student-t Copula
N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Independent Bernoulli
N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Stress Regime
N_BETA_BINOMIAL = int(1.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Beta Binomial

_ASSINGNED = (
    N_FORCED_DEFAULT
    + N_GAUSS
    + N_T_COP
    + N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
    + N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
    + N_BETA_BINOMIAL
)

# Correct rounding remainders if any (not strictly needed for 1000, but good practice)
if _ASSINGNED != NUM_OBLIGORS:
    N_BETA_BINOMIAL += NUM_OBLIGORS - _ASSINGNED

GLOBAL_T_COP_NU = 5.0  # Degrees of freedom for the common T-Copula chi-square factor


def _plot_portfolio_losses(
    portfolio_losses: np.ndarray,
    expected_loss: float,
    credit_var_99: float,
    worst_loss: float,
    credit_es_99: float,
) -> None:
    """Plots the aggregated portfolio loss distribution and risk metrics."""
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    import matplotlib.ticker as ticker

    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 6))

    losses_m = portfolio_losses / 1e6
    el_m = expected_loss / 1e6
    credit_var_99_m = credit_var_99 / 1e6
    # worst_m = worst_loss / 1e6

    ax.hist(
        losses_m,
        bins=150,
        color="#3a7ca5",
        edgecolor="#294d69",
        alpha=0.8,
        density=True,
    )

    ax.axvline(
        el_m,
        color="#2ca02c",
        linestyle="dashed",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"Expected Loss (EL): ${el_m:,.1f}M",
    )
    ax.axvline(
        credit_var_99_m,
        color="#d62728",
        linestyle="solid",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit VaR: ${credit_var_99_m:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.axvline(
        credit_es_99 / 1e6,
        color="orange",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit ES: ${credit_es_99 / 1e6:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.set_title("E2E JTD Portfolio Loss Distribution", fontsize=14, pad=15)
    ax.set_xlabel("Simulated Portfolio Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Density", fontsize=12)

    ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}M"))
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.15)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=11)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


def run_demo() -> None:
    print_calling_file()
    print("--- BETA-BINOMIAL JTD ENGINE VALIDATION DEMO ---")
    print(f"Obligors:  {NUM_OBLIGORS}")
    print(f"Scenarios: {NUM_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print(
        f"Model Mix: \n"
        f" - {N_FORCED_DEFAULT} Forced-Default \n"
        f" - {N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI} Independent Bernoulli \n"
        f" - {N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI} Stress Regime Bernoulli \n"
        f" - {N_BETA_BINOMIAL} Beta Binomial \n"
        f" - {N_GAUSS} Gaussian \n"
        f" - {N_T_COP} Student-t "
    )
    print("-" * 41 + "\n")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # ============================================================
    # 2. INGEST: OBLIGOR MASTER
    # ============================================================
    print("[1/5] Building & Compiling Obligor Master...")

    obligor_uids = np.arange(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    rng.shuffle(obligor_uids)  # Shuffle to prove the compiler sorts it

    # --- OBLIGOR PDS --- #

    # Base PDs
    # Realistic synthetic PD generation for 1000 names
    _obligor_pds = rng.lognormal(mean=np.log(0.02), sigma=10.0, size=NUM_OBLIGORS)
    _obligor_pds = np.clip(_obligor_pds, 0.0001, 0.50)

    # Define a concentration parameter. Lower = more overdispersion, higher = tighter.
    # kappa = 100.0 introduces highly realistic credit-spread overdispersion
    kappa = 100.0

    # Calculate vectorized alpha and beta arrays per obligor
    _alphas = _obligor_pds * kappa
    _betas = (1.0 - _obligor_pds) * kappa

    # Draw completely smooth, continuously overdispersed probabilities
    _obligor_pds = rng.beta(_alphas, _betas)

    # --- OBLIGOR LGDS --- #

    _obligor_lgds = rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75, NUM_OBLIGORS)

    obligor_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,
            "pd": _obligor_pds,
            "lgd": _obligor_lgds,
            "rating_id": 0,
            "sector_id": 0,
            "country_id": 0,
            "parent_uid": None,  # Ultimates
        }
    )

    obligor_master = compile_obligor_master(obligor_df)

    # ============================================================
    # 3. INGEST: JTD PORTFOLIO
    # ============================================================
    print("[2/5] Building & Compiling JTD Portfolio...")

    models = (
        [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_GAUSS
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_T_COP
        + [JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL.name] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
    )

    factor_names = (
        ["GLOBAL_CREDIT"]
        + [f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"]
        + [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"]
        + ["GLOBAL_BETA_PD"]
    )

    # --- CALIBRATE THE BETA BINOMIAL PARAMETERS ---
    _obligor_mean_pd = np.mean(_obligor_pds)
    _obligor_var_pd = np.var(_obligor_pds)

    # Guardrail 1: Prevent negative/zero 'total' values if cross-sectional variance is too high
    max_theoretical_var = _obligor_mean_pd * (1.0 - _obligor_mean_pd)
    if _obligor_var_pd >= max_theoretical_var:
        # Cap the variance at a safe 75% of the absolute mathematical maximum
        _obligor_var_pd = 0.75 * max_theoretical_var

    total = (_obligor_mean_pd * (1 - _obligor_mean_pd) / _obligor_var_pd) - 1
    alpha_mom = _obligor_mean_pd * total
    beta_mom = (1 - _obligor_mean_pd) * total

    # Guardrail 2: Prevent alpha < 1.0 which causes unstable U-shaped density spikes at the boundaries
    if alpha_mom < 1.0:
        alpha_mom = 1.5
        beta_mom = alpha_mom * ((1.0 - _obligor_mean_pd) / _obligor_mean_pd)

    print(
        f"\nCalibrated Beta-Binomial parameters to match obligor mean PD ({100 * _obligor_mean_pd:.2f}%):"
        f"\n- alpha: {alpha_mom:>8.2f}"
        f"\n- beta:  {beta_mom:>8.2f}"
    )

    factor_universe_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "factor_id": range(len(factor_names)),
            "factor_name": factor_names,
            "factor_kind": [
                "SYSTEMIC"
                if n == "GLOBAL_CREDIT"
                else "CHI"
                if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_"))
                else "UNIFORM"
                if n.startswith("U_")
                else "STRESS_INDICATOR"
                if n == "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"
                else "BETA_PD"
                if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD"
                else "IDIOSYNCRATIC"
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            "nu": [
                GLOBAL_T_COP_NU if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_")) else None
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            # Use the new shape parameters we locked in earlier to avoid beta naming collision
            "alpha": [
                alpha_mom if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD" else None for n in factor_names
            ],
            "beta": [beta_mom if n == "GLOBAL_BETA_PD" else None for n in factor_names],
        }
    )
    universe = compile_credit_factor_universe(factor_universe_df)

    jtd_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,  # Unsorted, matches the raw frame
            "instrument": models,
            "factor_loading": rng.uniform(0.1, 0.5, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "systemic_factor": "GLOBAL_CREDIT",
            "idiosyncratic_factor": [
                f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)
            ],
            "uniform_factor": [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)],
            "stress_regime_factor": "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME",
            "stress_pd_multiplier": rng.uniform(1.25, 3.0, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "beta_factor": "GLOBAL_BETA_PD",  # Will broadcast. Non-Beta-Binomial builders safely ignore it.
        }
    )

    # Add T-Copula specific columns
    chi_factors = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
    )
    nus = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + [GLOBAL_T_COP_NU] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_BETA_BINOMIAL
    )

    jtd_df["chi_squared_factor"] = chi_factors
    jtd_df["nu"] = nus

    print_df(
        jtd_df.head(n=10),
        title="Sample (head) JTD Portfolio DataFrame (Unsorted)",
    )
    print_df(
        jtd_df.tail(n=10),
        title="Sample (tail) JTD Portfolio DataFrame (Unsorted)",
    )

    _jtd_bundle = JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(jtd_df)
    _masters = CompiledCreditMasters(
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
        factor_universe=universe,
    )
    compiled_portfolio = compile_jtd_portfolio(
        bundle=_jtd_bundle,
        masters=_masters,
    )

    compiled_portfolio.summary()

    # ============================================================
    # 4. SYNTHESIZE EAD (ALIGNED TO COMPILED PORTFOLIO)
    # ============================================================
    print("[3/5] Generating Exposure Matrix...")

    ead_by_obligor = np.empty(
        (NUM_OBLIGORS, NUM_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    base_ead = rng.uniform(1e6, 10e6, (NUM_OBLIGORS, 1))
    ead_by_obligor[:] = base_ead  # Broadcast across scenarios

    ead_by_obligor *= rng.lognormal(
        mean=0.0, sigma=0.1, size=(NUM_OBLIGORS, NUM_SCENARIOS)
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 5. SYNTHESIZE FACTOR MATRIX
    # ============================================================
    print("[4/5] Generating Credit Factor Matrix...")

    factor_generator = MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe(
        universe,
        total_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
        seed=RNG_SEED,
        stress_p_by_uix={int(u): 0.05 for u in universe.stress_indicator_uix},
    )
    factor_matrix = build_credit_factor_matrix(
        factor_generator.iter_batches(batch_size=min(NUM_SCENARIOS, 25_000)),
        num_factors=universe.n_factors,
        num_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 6. ENGINE EXECUTION
    # ============================================================
    print("[5/5] Executing `run_jtd` Engine...")

    start_time = time.perf_counter()

    jtd_result = run_jtd(
        jtd_portfolio=compiled_portfolio,
        ead_by_obligor=ead_by_obligor,
        credit_factors=factor_matrix,
    )

    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time
    print(
        f"Engine completed {NUM_OBLIGORS * NUM_SCENARIOS:,} "
        f"evaluations in {elapsed:.4f}s"
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 7. METRICS & ANALYSIS
    # ============================================================
    print("\n--- Portfolio Credit Risk Metrics ---")

    portfolio_losses = np.sum(jtd_result.loss, axis=0)

    expected_loss = np.mean(portfolio_losses)
    credit_var_99 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.99)
    credit_es_99 = portfolio_losses[portfolio_losses >= credit_var_99].mean()

    print(f"Expected Loss (EL):  ${expected_loss:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.0%):  ${credit_var_99:,.2f}")
    print(f"Worst Scenario Loss: ${np.max(portfolio_losses):,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit ES (99.0%): ${credit_es_99:,.2f}")

    # 8. PLOTS
    _plot_portfolio_losses(
        portfolio_losses,
        expected_loss,
        credit_var_99,
        np.max(portfolio_losses),
        credit_es_99,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # DEFAULT STATS
    # ============================================================

    default_indicators = jtd_result.loss > 0.0
    defaults_per_scenario = default_indicators.sum(axis=0)

    print("\n--- Default Statistics ---")
    print(f"Average Defaults: {defaults_per_scenario.mean():.2f}")
    print(f"99% Default Count: {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.99):.0f}")
    print(f"Max Defaults:      {defaults_per_scenario.max():.0f}")

    print("\n--- Default Clustering Statistics ---")
    print(f"Average Defaults:      {defaults_per_scenario.mean():.2f}")
    print(f"95% Default Count:     {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.95):.0f}")
    print(f"99% Default Count:     {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.99):.0f}")
    print(f"99.9% Default Count:   {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.999):.0f}")
    print(f"Max Defaults:          {defaults_per_scenario.max():.0f}")

    # ============================================================
    # VaR Attribution
    # ============================================================

    var_idx = np.argmin(np.abs(portfolio_losses - credit_var_99))
    obligor_contributions = jtd_result.loss[:, var_idx]
    sorted_idx = np.argsort(obligor_contributions)[::-1]

    top_n = 10
    print(f"\n--- Top {top_n} Contributors to 99% Credit VaR ---")
    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_idx[:top_n], start=1):
        uid = compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids[idx]
        contrib = obligor_contributions[idx]
        print(f"{rank:2d}. Obligor {uid:<4d} Contribution: ${contrib:,.2f}")

    print(f"\nTotal Portfolio Loss in VaR Scenario: ${portfolio_losses[var_idx]:,.2f}")

    # ============================================================
    # Expected Shortfall (99%) Attribution
    # ============================================================

    tail_mask = portfolio_losses >= credit_var_99
    obligor_contributions = jtd_result.loss[:, tail_mask].mean(axis=1)
    sorted_idx = np.argsort(obligor_contributions)[::-1]

    top_n = 10
    print(f"\n--- Top {top_n} Contributors to 99% Credit Expected Shortfall ---")
    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_idx[:top_n], start=1):
        uid = compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids[idx]
        contrib = obligor_contributions[idx]
        print(f"{rank:2d}. Obligor {uid:<4d} Contribution: ${contrib:,.2f}")

    credit_es_99 = portfolio_losses[tail_mask].mean()
    print(f"\n99% Credit Expected Shortfall: ${credit_es_99:,.2f}")

    top10_share = (
        obligor_contributions[sorted_idx[:10]].sum() / obligor_contributions.sum()
    )
    print(f"Top 10 names explain {100 * top10_share:.1f}% of Credit Expected Shortfall")

    # ============================================================
    # BETA-BINOMIAL VALIDATION
    # ============================================================
    # Theoretical common-PD mean: E[P_s] = alpha / (alpha + beta).
    theoretical_mean_pd = alpha_mom / (alpha_mom + beta_mom)

    # Per-scenario realized default rate across the (100% beta-binomial) book.
    default_indicators = jtd_result.loss > 0.0
    default_rate_per_scenario = default_indicators.mean(axis=0)
    empirical_mean_pd = float(default_rate_per_scenario.mean())

    # Signature of the common-shock model: the default RATE is itself stochastic
    # (it IS P_s), so its cross-scenario variance is large. An independent-
    # Bernoulli book of the same size would pin the rate near its mean, with
    # variance ~ p(1 - p) / N.
    independent_rate_var = (
        theoretical_mean_pd * (1.0 - theoretical_mean_pd) / NUM_OBLIGORS
    )
    realized_rate_var = float(default_rate_per_scenario.var())

    print("\n--- Beta-Binomial Validation ---")
    print(f"Theoretical mean PD  E[P_s] = a/(a+b): {theoretical_mean_pd:.4%}")
    print(f"Empirical mean default rate:           {empirical_mean_pd:.4%}")
    print(f"Realized default-rate variance:        {realized_rate_var:.3e}")
    print(f"Independent-Bernoulli baseline var:    {independent_rate_var:.3e}")
    print(
        f"Overdispersion ratio:                  "
        f"{realized_rate_var / independent_rate_var:,.1f}x"
    )

    print_success_message()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```
</file>
