<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\credit\jtd\validation\demo_validate_stress_regime_bernoulli_engine.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/credit/jtd/validation/demo_validate_stress_regime_bernoulli_engine.py
"""
End-to-End Validation of the Stress Regime Bernoulli JTD Engine.

This script validates the Stress Regime Bernoulli block of the Jump-to-Default
(JTD) credit engine.

Stress Regime Bernoulli obligors are modeled as conditionally independent
Bernoulli default events whose probabilities of default are amplified when a
shared stress regime is active. The stress indicator introduces portfolio-wide
default clustering by simultaneously increasing default probabilities across
multiple obligors during adverse scenarios.

The purpose of this validation is to demonstrate that the engine correctly
applies stress-dependent default probabilities and reproduces the expected loss
and default characteristics implied by the stress regime specification.

Pipeline
--------
Raw DataFrames
    -> Compilation
    -> Portfolio Assembly
    -> JTD Engine
    -> Scenario Loss Distribution
    -> Validation Metrics

===============================================================================
MATHEMATICAL MODEL
===============================================================================

Let S_s denote a binary stress indicator for scenario s:

    S_s ∈ {0, 1}

where

    S_s = 1  => stress regime active
    S_s = 0  => normal regime

and

    P(S_s = 1) = p_stress

For obligor i with baseline probability of default PD_i and stress multiplier
m_i, the scenario-specific probability of default is

    PD_{i,s} =
        PD_i                      if S_s = 0
        min(PD_i * m_i, 1.0)      if S_s = 1

Conditional on the stress regime realization, defaults are independent:

    D_{i,s} | S_s ~ Bernoulli(PD_{i,s})

The portfolio loss in scenario s is

    L_s = Σ_i D_{i,s} * LGD_i * EAD_i

where

    D_{i,s} : default indicator
    LGD_i   : loss given default
    EAD_i   : exposure at default

Although obligors remain conditionally independent given the stress regime,
they become unconditionally dependent because they share the same stress
indicator. This creates default clustering and increases portfolio tail risk.

===============================================================================
THEORETICAL RESULTS
===============================================================================

Expected Default Probability:

    E[PD_i]
        = (1 - p_stress) * PD_i
        + p_stress * min(PD_i * m_i, 1.0)

Expected Loss:

    EL
        = Σ_i E[PD_i] * LGD_i * EAD_i

Default Dependence:

Because obligors share a common stress indicator,

    Cov(D_i, D_j) > 0

whenever both obligors are sensitive to the stress regime.

Unlike the Independent Bernoulli model, default dependence arises through the
shared regime state rather than through latent-factor copula structures.

Tail Risk:

Large portfolio losses occur disproportionately during stress-regime scenarios,
producing a heavier-tailed loss distribution than the Independent Bernoulli
model.

===============================================================================
EXPECTED VALIDATION PROPERTIES
===============================================================================

The simulation should demonstrate:

1. Stress-regime activation frequency converges to the configured
   stress probability.

2. Marginal default frequencies converge to their theoretical stressed
   expectations.

3. Simulated expected loss converges to the theoretical expected loss.

4. Default frequencies increase materially during stress-regime scenarios.

5. Positive pairwise default dependence emerges despite conditional
   independence within each regime.

6. Portfolio tail-loss metrics exceed those produced by an equivalent
   Independent Bernoulli portfolio.

7. Empirical loss moments converge to theoretical values within Monte Carlo
   sampling error.

===============================================================================
RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER JTD BLOCKS
===============================================================================

The Stress Regime Bernoulli block extends the Independent Bernoulli model by
introducing a shared stress indicator that simultaneously affects multiple
obligors.

Unlike Gaussian Copula and Student-t Copula models, dependence is generated
through explicit regime switching rather than latent asset-value factors.

Compared with the Independent Bernoulli model, Stress Regime Bernoulli
produces:

    - Positive default dependence
    - Increased default clustering
    - Higher portfolio tail risk
    - Regime-dependent loss distributions

while remaining computationally simple and easily interpretable.

===============================================================================
REFERENCES
===============================================================================

1. McNeil, A. J., Frey, R., & Embrechts, P. (2005).
   Quantitative Risk Management: Concepts, Techniques and Tools.
   Princeton University Press.

2. Bluhm, C., Overbeck, L., & Wagner, C. (2016).
   Introduction to Credit Risk Modeling (2nd Edition).
   Chapman & Hall / CRC.

3. Hamilton, J. D. (1989).
   A New Approach to the Economic Analysis of Nonstationary Time Series and
   the Business Cycle.
   Econometrica, 57(2), 357-384.

4. Gordy, M. B. (2003).
   A Risk-Factor Model Foundation for Ratings-Based Bank Capital Rules.
   Journal of Financial Intermediation, 12(3), 199-232.

"""

from __future__ import annotations

import time

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import RNG_SEED
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import build_credit_factor_matrix
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.mixed import MixedCreditFactorGenerator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.engine import run_jtd
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.compile import compile_jtd_portfolio
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditMasters,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.ingest import (
    compile_credit_factor_universe,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.ingest import compile_obligor_master
from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_df, print_success_message

# ============================================================
# 1. SETUP & CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================

NUM_OBLIGORS = 1_000
NUM_SCENARIOS = 100_000

# Distribution of models
N_GAUSS = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Gaussian Copula
N_T_COP = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Student-t Copula
N_FORCED_DEFAULT = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Forced-Default (Stress/Defaulted)
N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI = int(0.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Independent Bernoulli
N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI = int(1.0 * NUM_OBLIGORS)  # Stress Regime Bernoulli

GLOBAL_T_COP_NU = 5.0  # Degrees of freedom for the common T-Copula chi-square factor


def _plot_portfolio_losses(
    portfolio_losses: np.ndarray,
    expected_loss: float,
    credit_var_99: float,
    worst_loss: float,
    credit_es_99: float,
) -> None:
    """Plots the aggregated portfolio loss distribution and risk metrics."""
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    import matplotlib.ticker as ticker

    plt.style.use("dark_background")
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 6))

    losses_m = portfolio_losses / 1e6
    el_m = expected_loss / 1e6
    credit_var_99_m = credit_var_99 / 1e6
    worst_m = worst_loss / 1e6

    ax.hist(
        losses_m,
        bins=150,
        color="#3a7ca5",
        edgecolor="#294d69",
        alpha=0.8,
        density=True,
    )

    ax.axvline(
        el_m,
        color="#2ca02c",
        linestyle="dashed",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"Expected Loss (EL): ${el_m:,.1f}M",
    )
    ax.axvline(
        credit_var_99_m,
        color="#d62728",
        linestyle="solid",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit VaR: ${credit_var_99_m:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.axvline(
        credit_es_99 / 1e6,
        color="orange",
        linewidth=2.5,
        label=f"99% Credit ES: ${credit_es_99 / 1e6:,.1f}M",
    )

    ax.set_title("E2E JTD Portfolio Loss Distribution", fontsize=14, pad=15)
    ax.set_xlabel("Simulated Portfolio Loss ($ Millions)", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Density", fontsize=12)

    ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}M"))
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.15)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=11)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


def run_demo() -> None:
    print_calling_file()
    print("--- STRESS REGIME BERNOULLI JTD ENGINE E2E DEMO ---")
    print(f"Obligors:  {NUM_OBLIGORS}")
    print(f"Scenarios: {NUM_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print("-" * 34 + "\n")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed=RNG_SEED)

    # ============================================================
    # 2. INGEST: OBLIGOR MASTER
    # ============================================================
    print("[1/5] Building & Compiling Obligor Master...")

    # DATA EXAMPLE (Obligor Master):
    # | uid | pd   | lgd  | rating_id | parent_uid |
    # |-----|------|------|-----------|------------|
    # | 10  | 0.02 | 0.60 | 0         | None       |
    #
    # PD (0.02) = 2% chance of default this year.
    # LGD (0.60) = If default occurs, we lose 60% of our exposure (recover 40%).

    obligor_uids = np.arange(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    rng.shuffle(obligor_uids)  # Shuffle to prove the compiler sorts it

    obligor_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,
            "pd": rng.uniform(0.001, 0.05, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "lgd": rng.uniform(0.4, 0.8, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "rating_id": 0,
            "sector_id": 0,
            "country_id": 0,
            "parent_uid": None,  # Ultimates
        }
    )

    # The master is compiled into SoA. UIDs are strictly sorted ascending
    # to allow for lightning-fast `np.searchsorted` joins later.
    obligor_master = compile_obligor_master(obligor_df)

    # ============================================================
    # 3. INGEST: JTD PORTFOLIO
    # ============================================================
    print("[2/5] Building & Compiling JTD Portfolio...")

    # DATA EXAMPLE (JTD Portfolio):
    # | uid | instrument         | beta | systemic_factor | idiosyncratic_factor | nu  |
    # |-----|--------------------|------|-----------------|----------------------|-----|
    # | 10  | COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN | 0.45 | GLOBAL_CREDIT   | IDIO_10              | NaN |
    # | 11  | COPULA_SF_T        | 0.60 | GLOBAL_CREDIT   | IDIO_11              | 5.0 |
    #
    # Beta (0.45): The obligor's latent asset value is constructed as:
    #              A = 0.45 * Z + sqrt(1 - 0.45^2) * eps
    #
    #              The systematic market factor contributes 0.45 as a loading
    #              coefficient, corresponding to an asset correlation of
    #              rho = beta^2 = 20.25%.
    #
    #              The remaining 79.75% of variance is driven by the
    #              idiosyncratic factor (eps).

    models = (
        [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name] * N_GAUSS
        + [JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name] * N_T_COP
        + [JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
    )

    # We need UIX (Universe Index) pointers for all latent factors.
    # 1 Global + 300 Idio + 150 Chi = 451 total factors.
    factor_names = (
        ["GLOBAL_CREDIT"]
        + [f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"]
        + [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)]
        + ["GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"]
    )
    # Compile the factor-universe MASTER from the generated factor list. At
    # validation scale this authoring table is generated (one idiosyncratic
    # factor per obligor) rather than the minimal canonical fixture, but it
    # flows through the SAME master contract: name -> UIX resolution and chi-dof
    # reconciliation now read from `universe`, never an ad-hoc {name: i} dict.
    factor_universe_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "factor_id": range(len(factor_names)),
            "factor_name": factor_names,
            "factor_kind": [
                "SYSTEMIC"
                if n == "GLOBAL_CREDIT"
                else "CHI"
                if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_"))
                else "UNIFORM"
                if n.startswith("U_")
                else "STRESS_INDICATOR"
                if n == "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME"
                else "IDIOSYNCRATIC"
                for n in factor_names
            ],
            "nu": [
                GLOBAL_T_COP_NU if (n == "GLOBAL_CHI" or n.startswith("CHI_")) else None
                for n in factor_names
            ],
        }
    )
    universe = compile_credit_factor_universe(factor_universe_df)

    jtd_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": obligor_uids,  # Unsorted, matches the raw frame
            "instrument": models,
            "beta": rng.uniform(0.1, 0.5, NUM_OBLIGORS),
            "systemic_factor": "GLOBAL_CREDIT",
            "idiosyncratic_factor": [
                f"IDIO_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)
            ],
            "uniform_factor": [f"U_{i:03d}" for i in range(1, NUM_OBLIGORS + 1)],
            "stress_regime_factor": "GLOBAL_STRESS_REGIME",
            "stress_pd_multiplier": rng.uniform(1.25, 3.0, NUM_OBLIGORS),
        }
    )

    # Add T-Copula specific columns (pandas allows NaNs for non-applicable rows)
    chi_factors = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + ["GLOBAL_CHI"] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
    )
    nus = (
        [None] * N_GAUSS
        + [GLOBAL_T_COP_NU] * N_T_COP
        + [None] * N_FORCED_DEFAULT
        + [None] * N_INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI
        + [None] * N_STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI
    )

    jtd_df["chi_squared_factor"] = chi_factors
    jtd_df["nu"] = nus

    print_df(
        jtd_df.sample(n=25, random_state=RNG_SEED),
        title="Sample JTD Portfolio DataFrame (Unsorted)",
    )

    # Compile the portfolio! This strips strings, maps UIXs, joins PD/LGD,
    # computes thresholds via ndtri(PD), precomputes sqrt(nu), and builds
    # the C-contiguous Struct-of-Arrays (SoA) blocks.
    _jtd_bundle = JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(jtd_df)
    _masters = CompiledCreditMasters(
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
        factor_universe=universe,
    )
    compiled_portfolio = compile_jtd_portfolio(
        bundle=_jtd_bundle,
        masters=_masters,
    )

    compiled_portfolio.summary()

    # ============================================================
    # 4. SYNTHESIZE EAD (ALIGNED TO COMPILED PORTFOLIO)
    # ============================================================
    print("[3/5] Generating Exposure Matrix...")

    # EAD (Exposure At Default) is the total dollar amount at risk at the exact
    # moment of default. It changes per scenario because it's driven by market
    # risk (e.g., if a counterparty defaults when FX rates move against them,
    # our exposure is higher).
    #
    # DOD INVARIANT: The EAD matrix rows MUST align perfectly with the
    # `compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids` array, because the engine dispatches
    # contiguous memory slices based on the block offsets.

    ead_by_obligor = np.empty(
        (NUM_OBLIGORS, NUM_SCENARIOS), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    # Synthesize base $1M - $10M exposures
    base_ead = rng.uniform(1e6, 10e6, (NUM_OBLIGORS, 1))
    ead_by_obligor[:] = base_ead  # Broadcast across scenarios

    # Introduce stochastic noise to simulate market-driven exposure paths
    ead_by_obligor *= rng.lognormal(
        mean=0.0, sigma=0.1, size=(NUM_OBLIGORS, NUM_SCENARIOS)
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 5. SYNTHESIZE FACTOR MATRIX
    # ============================================================
    print("[4/5] Generating Credit Factor Matrix...")

    # === Synthesize the credit factor matrix from the universe master ===
    # The mixed generator derives its standard-normal / chi / uniform / stress
    # columns from `universe`, so every row carries the correct marginal and
    # cannot disagree with the compiled portfolio about which columns are chi
    # (or at what nu). This replaces the hand-rolled per-block buffer fill.
    factor_generator = MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe(
        universe,
        total_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
        seed=RNG_SEED,
        stress_p_by_uix={int(u): 0.05 for u in universe.stress_indicator_uix},
    )
    factor_matrix = build_credit_factor_matrix(
        factor_generator.iter_batches(batch_size=min(NUM_SCENARIOS, 25_000)),
        num_factors=universe.n_factors,
        num_scenarios=NUM_SCENARIOS,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 6. ENGINE EXECUTION
    # ============================================================
    print("[5/5] Executing `run_jtd` Engine...")

    start_time = time.perf_counter()

    # The engine walks through `compiled_portfolio.blocks` (Gaussian -> T-Copula -> Forced-Default).
    # It slices the `ead_by_obligor` and passes it to the highly-optimized `njit` kernels.
    # Because memory is C-contiguous, the CPU cache pre-fetches the scenario vectors,
    # resulting in near bare-metal hardware limits.
    jtd_result = run_jtd(
        jtd_portfolio=compiled_portfolio,
        ead_by_obligor=ead_by_obligor,
        credit_factors=factor_matrix,
    )

    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time
    print(
        f"Engine completed {NUM_OBLIGORS * NUM_SCENARIOS:,} "
        f"evaluations in {elapsed:.4f}s"
    )

    # ============================================================
    # 7. METRICS & ANALYSIS
    # ============================================================
    print("\n--- Portfolio Credit Risk Metrics ---")

    # jtd_result.loss is shape (NUM_OBLIGORS, NUM_SCENARIOS)
    # We sum down the obligor axis (axis=0) to get the total portfolio loss per scenario.
    portfolio_losses = np.sum(jtd_result.loss, axis=0)

    # Expected Loss (EL) is the mathematical mean of all Monte Carlo outcomes.
    expected_loss = np.mean(portfolio_losses)

    # Credit VaR (Value at Risk) 99.0% represents the loss threshold that is only
    # breached in the worst 1% of scenarios (driven heavily by the T-Copula W factor).
    credit_var_99 = np.quantile(portfolio_losses, 0.99)

    # Credit Expected Shortfall (ES) 99.0% is the average loss across all scenarios that breach the 99% VaR threshold.
    credit_es_99 = portfolio_losses[portfolio_losses >= credit_var_99].mean()

    print(f"Expected Loss (EL):  ${expected_loss:,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit VaR (99.0%):  ${credit_var_99:,.2f}")
    print(f"Worst Scenario Loss: ${np.max(portfolio_losses):,.2f}")
    print(f"Credit ES (99.0%): ${credit_es_99:,.2f}")

    # 8. PLOTS
    _plot_portfolio_losses(
        portfolio_losses,
        expected_loss,
        credit_var_99,
        np.max(portfolio_losses),
        credit_es_99,
    )

    # ============================================================
    # Empirical PD Validation
    # ============================================================

    # A key sanity check for any copula default engine is verifying that the
    # simulated default frequencies converge to the input PDs. Since default
    # thresholds are constructed via:
    #
    #     t_i = Phi^{-1}(PD_i)
    #
    # the unconditional probability of:
    #
    #     A_i < t_i
    #
    # should equal the input PD as the number of Monte Carlo scenarios grows.

    print("\n--- Empirical PD Validation ---")

    # A default occurs whenever a positive loss is realized.
    default_indicators = jtd_result.loss > 0.0

    # Empirical default frequency for each obligor.
    empirical_pd = default_indicators.mean(axis=1)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Align empirical PDs and target PDs by UID.
    #
    # Never assume row ordering is identical between:
    #   - jtd_result.loss
    #   - compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids
    #   - obligor_master
    #
    # Explicitly joining by UID avoids subtle validation bugs.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    validation_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids,
            "empirical_pd": empirical_pd,
        }
    )

    validation_df = validation_df.merge(
        pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "uid": obligor_master.uid,
                "target_pd": obligor_master.pd,
            }
        ),
        on="uid",
        how="inner",
    )

    validation_df["abs_error"] = np.abs(
        validation_df["empirical_pd"] - validation_df["target_pd"]
    )

    print(f"Mean Input PD:      {validation_df['target_pd'].mean():.4%}")

    print(f"Mean Empirical PD:  {validation_df['empirical_pd'].mean():.4%}")

    print(f"Mean Absolute Error:{validation_df['abs_error'].mean():.4%}")

    print(f"Max Absolute Error: {validation_df['abs_error'].max():.4%}")

    # ============================================================
    # Largest Deviations
    # ============================================================

    top_n = 10

    worst_df = validation_df.sort_values(
        "abs_error",
        ascending=False,
    ).head(top_n)

    print(f"\n--- Top {top_n} Largest PD Deviations ---")

    for rank, row in enumerate(
        worst_df.itertuples(index=False),
        start=1,
    ):
        print(
            f"{rank:2d}. "
            f"Obligor {row.uid:<4d} "
            f"Target={row.target_pd:>7.3%} "
            f"Empirical={row.empirical_pd:>7.3%} "
            f"Error={row.abs_error:>7.3%}"
        )

    # ============================================================
    # DEFAULT STATS
    # ============================================================

    default_indicators = jtd_result.loss > 0.0

    defaults_per_scenario = default_indicators.sum(axis=0)

    print("\n--- Default Statistics ---")
    print(f"Average Defaults: {defaults_per_scenario.mean():.2f}")
    print(f"99% Default Count: {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.99):.0f}")
    print(f"Max Defaults:      {defaults_per_scenario.max():.0f}")

    print("\n--- Default Clustering Statistics ---")
    print(f"Average Defaults:      {defaults_per_scenario.mean():.2f}")
    print(f"95% Default Count:     {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.95):.0f}")
    print(f"99% Default Count:     {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.99):.0f}")
    print(f"99.9% Default Count:   {np.quantile(defaults_per_scenario, 0.999):.0f}")
    print(f"Max Defaults:          {defaults_per_scenario.max():.0f}")

    # ============================================================
    # VaR Attribution
    # ============================================================

    # Find the scenario closest to the 99% Credit VaR.
    # This identifies the tail scenario responsible for the VaR loss.
    var_idx = np.argmin(np.abs(portfolio_losses - credit_var_99))

    # Loss contribution of each obligor in the VaR scenario.
    obligor_contributions = jtd_result.loss[:, var_idx]

    # Sort descending by contribution.
    sorted_idx = np.argsort(obligor_contributions)[::-1]

    top_n = 10

    print(f"\n--- Top {top_n} Contributors to 99% Credit VaR ---")

    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_idx[:top_n], start=1):
        uid = compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids[idx]
        contrib = obligor_contributions[idx]

        print(f"{rank:2d}. Obligor {uid:<4d} Contribution: ${contrib:,.2f}")

    print(f"\nTotal Portfolio Loss in VaR Scenario: ${portfolio_losses[var_idx]:,.2f}")

    # ============================================================
    # Expected Shortfall (99%) Attribution
    # ============================================================

    # Select all portfolio loss scenarios that exceed the 99% Credit VaR.
    # This defines the 1% tail of the loss distribution.
    tail_mask = portfolio_losses >= credit_var_99

    # Average loss contribution of each obligor across all tail scenarios.
    # This produces a stable Expected Shortfall-style attribution.
    obligor_contributions = jtd_result.loss[:, tail_mask].mean(axis=1)

    # Sort obligors by contribution to tail losses.
    sorted_idx = np.argsort(obligor_contributions)[::-1]

    top_n = 10

    print(f"\n--- Top {top_n} Contributors to 99% Credit Expected Shortfall ---")

    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_idx[:top_n], start=1):
        uid = compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids[idx]
        contrib = obligor_contributions[idx]

        print(f"{rank:2d}. Obligor {uid:<4d} Contribution: ${contrib:,.2f}")

    # Average portfolio loss across all tail scenarios.
    credit_es_99 = portfolio_losses[tail_mask].mean()

    print(f"\n99% Credit Expected Shortfall: ${credit_es_99:,.2f}")

    # --- TAIL CONCENTRATION ANALYSIS ---

    top10_share = (
        obligor_contributions[sorted_idx[:10]].sum() / obligor_contributions.sum()
    )

    print(f"Top 10 names explain {100 * top10_share:.1f}% of Credit Expected Shortfall")

    # ============================================================
    # MODEL COMPARISON: FORCED-DEFAULT VS GAUSSIAN VS T-COPULA
    # ============================================================

    # 1. Create a fast lookup series from the raw dataframe
    model_series = jtd_df.set_index("uid")["instrument"]

    # 2. Align the model labels to the exact memory order of the loss matrix
    aligned_models = model_series.loc[
        compiled_portfolio.obligor_uids.tolist()
    ].to_numpy()

    # 3. Generate the boolean masks
    forced_default_mask = aligned_models == JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name
    independent_bernoulli_mask = aligned_models == JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name
    stress_regime_bernoulli_mask = (
        aligned_models == JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name
    )
    gaussian_mask = aligned_models == JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name
    t_mask = aligned_models == JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name

    # 4. Slice the 2D loss matrix and sum across obligors
    forced_default_losses = jtd_result.loss[forced_default_mask].sum(axis=0)
    independent_bernoulli_losses = jtd_result.loss[independent_bernoulli_mask].sum(
        axis=0
    )
    stress_regime_bernoulli_losses = jtd_result.loss[stress_regime_bernoulli_mask].sum(
        axis=0
    )
    gaussian_losses = jtd_result.loss[gaussian_mask].sum(axis=0)
    t_losses = jtd_result.loss[t_mask].sum(axis=0)

    # 5. Calculate Metrics
    forced_default_el = forced_default_losses.mean()
    forced_default_var = np.quantile(forced_default_losses, 0.99)
    forced_default_es = forced_default_losses[
        forced_default_losses >= forced_default_var
    ].mean()

    independent_bernoulli_el = independent_bernoulli_losses.mean()
    independent_bernoulli_var = np.quantile(independent_bernoulli_losses, 0.99)
    independent_bernoulli_es = independent_bernoulli_losses[
        independent_bernoulli_losses >= independent_bernoulli_var
    ].mean()

    stress_regime_bernoulli_el = stress_regime_bernoulli_losses.mean()
    stress_regime_bernoulli_var = np.quantile(stress_regime_bernoulli_losses, 0.99)
    stress_regime_bernoulli_es = stress_regime_bernoulli_losses[
        stress_regime_bernoulli_losses >= stress_regime_bernoulli_var
    ].mean()

    gauss_el = gaussian_losses.mean()
    gauss_var = np.quantile(gaussian_losses, 0.99)
    gauss_es = gaussian_losses[gaussian_losses >= gauss_var].mean()

    t_el = t_losses.mean()
    t_var = np.quantile(t_losses, 0.99)
    t_es = t_losses[t_losses >= t_var].mean()

    # 6. Print Formatted Table
    print("\n" + "=" * 85)
    print(f"{'MODEL COMPARISON SUMMARY':^85}")
    print("=" * 91)
    print(
        f"{'Model Type':<26} | {'Expected Loss (EL)':>18} | {'99% Credit VaR':>18} | {'99% Credit ES':>18}"
    )
    print("-" * 91)
    print(
        f"{'Forced-Default':<26} | ${forced_default_el:>17,.2f} | ${forced_default_var:>17,.2f} | ${forced_default_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Independent Bernoulli':<26} | ${independent_bernoulli_el:>17,.2f} | ${independent_bernoulli_var:>17,.2f} | ${independent_bernoulli_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Stress Regime Bernoulli':<26} | ${stress_regime_bernoulli_el:>17,.2f} | ${stress_regime_bernoulli_var:>17,.2f} | ${stress_regime_bernoulli_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Gaussian Copula':<26} | ${gauss_el:>17,.2f} | ${gauss_var:>17,.2f} | ${gauss_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print(
        f"{'Student-t Copula':<26} | ${t_el:>17,.2f} | ${t_var:>17,.2f} | ${t_es:>17,.2f}"
    )
    print("=" * 91)

    # ============================================================
    # END
    # ============================================================

    print_success_message()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_demo()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\_arrays.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/_arrays.py
"""
Cross-cutting NumPy array primitives shared across packages.

Top-level private module (mirrors `_njit.py`) so leaf consumers in
`proxy_resolver`, `simlab`, `portfolio`, `market_data`, and `credit` can import
these without a layering inversion.

No domain imports — pure array algebra; domain sentinels (e.g., `INVALID_UIX`
or `NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32`) are passed in by callers rather than imported here.

Contents
--------
unique_first_seen                        : dtype-preserving first-seen dedup + inverse.
searchsorted_membership                  : bool found-mask over a sorted-unique universe.
searchsorted_or_raise                    : STRICT INNER JOIN. Raises on any miss.
searchsorted_soft_join                   : SAFE LEFT JOIN. Probes existence, maps misses to a sentinel.
searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32 : UNSAFE LEFT JOIN. Bypasses probe, blindly maps `< 0` to a sentinel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
from numpy.typing import NDArray

# Centralized preview limit for error-message diagnostics to prevent
# massive stdout dumps on join failures.
_PREVIEW: Final[int] = 10


def _as_c_contig_f64(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)


def _as_c_contig_f32(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float32)


def _as_c_contig_i64(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int64)


def _as_c_contig_i32(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int32)


def _as_c_contig_i16(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int16]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int16)


def _as_c_contig_i8(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int8)


def _as_c_contig_bool(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.bool_]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.bool_)


def unique_first_seen(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray, npt.NDArray[np.int32]]:
    """
    First-seen dedup: dtype-preserving in the values, int32 in the inverse.

    `np.unique` sorts; we need first-seen order so shock-compute /
    proxy-resolution plans are deterministic across reruns of identical inputs.

    Returns
    -------
    unique_first_seen
        (K,) — unique values in first-seen order, same dtype as ``arr``.

    inverse_first_seen
        (N,) int32 — for each input position, its index in ``unique_first_seen``.
    """
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr[:0].copy(), np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)

    _, first_idx, inverse_sorted = np.unique(
        arr, return_index=True, return_inverse=True
    )
    # Reorder sorted-unique into first-seen order.
    order = np.argsort(first_idx, kind="mergesort")
    out_unique = arr[first_idx[order]]

    # Build sorted_idx -> first_seen_idx remap, then apply to the inverse.
    sorted_to_first_seen = np.empty(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    sorted_to_first_seen[order] = np.arange(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    inverse_first_seen = sorted_to_first_seen[inverse_sorted].astype(
        np.int32, copy=False
    )
    return out_unique, inverse_first_seen


# =========================================================================
# VECTORIZED JOIN PRIMITIVES
# =========================================================================
#
# `universe` MUST be sorted ascending and unique (caller's invariant).
# These do NOT validate that invariant; they trust it, exactly as the
# hand-rolled sites they replace did.


def searchsorted_membership(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    """
    EXISTENCE CHECK: Per-element membership of `raw` in sorted-unique `universe`.

    Returns a `(raw.size,)` bool mask, ``True`` where the value is present.
    No sentinel handling — callers that treat ``raw < 0`` as "not required"
    should mask those out before calling, or ignore the corresponding mask entries.
    """
    if raw.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.bool_)
    if universe.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(raw.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)

    idx = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    in_bounds = idx < universe.size
    found = np.zeros(raw.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)
    found[in_bounds] = universe[idx[in_bounds]] == raw[in_bounds]
    return found


def searchsorted_or_raise(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    ctx: str = "ids",
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """
    STRICT INNER JOIN: Locate every `raw` value in sorted-unique `universe`.
    Raises ValueError on any missing target.

    Returns the `(raw.size,)` int64 index array (the natural `np.searchsorted`
    dtype — callers gather with it, no downcast needed).
    """
    if raw.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)

    n = universe.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Universe is empty, {raw.shape[0]} target ID(s) cannot be resolved."
        )

    pos = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    matched = universe[pos] == raw

    if not matched.all():
        bad = np.unique(raw[~matched])[:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        n_bad = int((~matched).sum())
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {n_bad} ID(s) absent from the universe. First {len(bad)}: {bad}"
        )

    return pos


def searchsorted_soft_join(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    sentinel: np.int32,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    SAFE LEFT JOIN: Probes existence. Maps ANY missing `raw` value to `sentinel`.

    Use when absent target IDs are a valid, expected runtime case (e.g.,
    a netting set whose obligor is not modelled in the target portfolio).

    Returns `(raw.size,)` int32 indices. Caller filters via ``keep = pos >= 0``
    (assuming sentinel is negative).
    """
    n = universe.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.full(raw.shape[0], sentinel, dtype=np.int32)

    pos = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    hit = universe[pos] == raw
    return np.where(hit, pos, sentinel).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    sentinel: np.int32,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    UNSAFE LEFT JOIN: High-performance ID translation with sentinel preservation.

    `raw < 0` rows are explicitly treated as sentinels and mapped to `sentinel`.

    CRITICAL: All other IDs (>= 0) are BLINDLY ASSUMED present in `universe`.
    This primitive does NOT probe membership (no equality check, no bounds clip).
    If a missing positive ID is passed, it will silently map to the next-largest
    element's index. Only use this when upstream referential integrity is 100%
    guaranteed by prior validation phases.
    """
    uix = np.searchsorted(universe, raw).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    uix[raw < 0] = sentinel
    return uix
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/constants.py

"""
Common constants used across the riskquant codebase.

This module separates:
- typed sentinel values used in numeric payloads / arrays
- general configuration constants used by the portfolio engine
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date

import numpy as np

# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL SENTINELS
# ==============================================================================

# Universal sentinel for "no valid ID".
# Used across instrument_ids, underlying_ids, volatility_ids, and discount_curve_ids.
NO_ID = np.int32(-999_999)

# Universal sentinel for missing dates (settlement, delivery, expiry).
# Using -1 allows for clean `date_ord >= 0` masking.
NO_DATE_ORD32 = np.int32(-99_999)

# Specific sentinel for FX: No conversion required (Underlying CCY == Settlement CCY)
NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED = np.int32(-1)

# Invalid UIX sentinel for any universe index (*_uix fields).
# Used exclusively in the Execution Engine (blocks/compilers), never in Ingest.
INVALID_UIX = np.int32(-999)

# NOT IMPLEMENTED sentinels for fields expected to be populated but aren't yet.
NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL: np.int8 = np.int8(-99)

# ==============================================================================
# OPTION ENCODING
# ==============================================================================

# 1 for call options, -1 for put options, 0 for not-an-option.
# Allows for branchless payoff calculation: payoff * cp_mult
CALL_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(1)
PUT_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(-1)
NOT_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(0)

# ==============================================================================
# Date encoding
# ==============================================================================

DATE_EPOCH: date = date(1970, 1, 1)

# ==============================================================================
# CONFIGURATION CONSTANTS
# ==============================================================================

ROUND_DECIMALS_LEVEL = 8
ROUND_DECIMALS_QUANTITY = 8
ROUND_DECIMALS_STRIKE_PRICE = 8

POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE = 1e-8
PD_DUST_TOLERANCE = 1e-10

# ==============================================================================
# DEVELOPMENT-ONLY FALLBACK VALUES
# ==============================================================================

FALLBACK_MOCK_VOLATILITY_DO_NOT_SHIP: float = 0.50

# ==============================================================================
# EWMA Volatility Calculation Constants
# ==============================================================================

EWMA_DECAY_FACTOR: float = 0.94
MAX_EWMA_VOL_RATIO: float = 10.0
MIN_EWMA_VARIANCE: float = 1e-12

# ==============================================================================
# CREDIT RISK CONSTANTS
# ==============================================================================

NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR: np.int64 = np.int64(-1)
NO_RATING_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(-1)
NO_SECTOR_ID: np.int32 = np.int32(-1)
NO_COUNTRY_ID: np.int32 = np.int32(-1)

# Degrees-of-freedom sentinel for non-chi credit factors. nu is strictly
# positive for a chi-square mixing factor, so a negative value is an
# unambiguous "this factor carries no nu" marker (sentinel over NaN, per the
# core blocks contract).
NO_CHI_NU: np.float64 = np.float64(-1.0)

# Beta(alpha, beta) parameter sentinel for non-BETA_PD credit factors. Both
# shape parameters are strictly positive for a Beta factor, so a negative value
# is an unambiguous "this factor carries no alpha/beta" marker.
NO_BETA_PARAM: np.float64 = np.float64(-1.0)

# ==============================================================================
# RANDOM SEED FOR TESTING
# ==============================================================================

RNG_SEED: int = 42

# ==============================================================================
# DAYS PER YEAR FOR TTE CALCULATION
# ==============================================================================

DAYS_PER_YR_ACT_365_25: float = 365.25
TTE_FLOOR: float = 1e-9

# ==============================================================================
# IMPLIED VOL CALIBRATION CONSTANTS
# ==============================================================================

#: Failure sentinel written into the iv output for non-invertible option quotes.
#: Negative by construction, so the downstream contract ``success_mask = iv >= 0.0``
#: flags it as a failed solve. Imported by the Black-76 scalar solver and asserted
#: against by the CalibratedOptionQuotes master.
IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL = -1.0

#: Reliability floor for calibrated implied vols, expressed as vega / forward.
#: A solved iv reprices its target even when vega is ~0, but such a vol is
#: under-determined (deep ITM/OTM, ultra-short tenors). Quotes with
#: ``vega < MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD * fwd`` are gated out by
#: ``CalibratedOptionQuotes.reliable_mask``. Heuristic; tune per surface.
MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD: float = 1e-3
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/aggregate/loss_metrics.py
"""
Portfolio-loss risk metrics.

VaR and ES are computed by the shared per-row tail kernel
(`riskquant.scenario_metrics`), which operates on the PER-SCENARIO loss vector(s) — a
``(S,)`` row, or a ``(G, S)`` stack of group rows — never the full per-obligor
``(n, S)`` matrix. That ``(S,)`` total is the quantity a batched engine can
accumulate online, so the metric layer never assumes a materialised loss matrix.

Tail-convention bridge: the kernel reports the LOWER tail (worst = most negative,
the P&L convention). Credit loss is the opposite (worst = largest). The single
``_loss_tail_var_es`` adapter negates on the way in and out, so everything
public here is loss-oriented: larger loss is worse, VaR/ES are upper-tail.
``scenario_metrics``'s VaR is the order statistic at ``floor((1 - alpha) * S)`` and ES
the mean beyond it.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "expected_loss",
    "loss_var_es",
    "value_at_risk",
    "expected_shortfall",
    "tail_var_es_by_group",
    "loss_quantiles",
    "PortfolioLossDistribution",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.scenario_metrics.api.var_es import compute_var_es

_F64_1D = npt.NDArray[np.float64]
_F64_2D = npt.NDArray[np.float64]


def _loss_tail_var_es(
    *,
    loss_by_group: _F64_2D,
    alpha: float,
) -> tuple[_F64_1D, _F64_1D]:
    """Per-row upper-tail loss VaR/ES via the lower-tail kernel.

    Negate so the largest losses become the most-negative values the kernel
    treats as its tail, then negate the results back into loss space.

    Parameters
    ----------
    loss_by_group
        Shape (G, S) stack of group loss rows. G groups, S scenarios.
    alpha
        Confidence level, e.g. 0.99.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[_F64_1D, _F64_1D]
        VaR and ES for each group. Each shape (G,).
    """
    var_es_result = compute_var_es(-loss_by_group, confidence_level=alpha)
    return -var_es_result.var, -var_es_result.es


def expected_loss(portfolio_loss: _F64_1D) -> float:
    """Mean portfolio loss across scenarios (EL)."""
    return float(portfolio_loss.mean())


def loss_var_es(portfolio_loss: _F64_1D, alpha: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Upper-tail VaR and ES of a single ``(S,)`` portfolio loss vector."""
    var, es = _loss_tail_var_es(
        loss_by_group=portfolio_loss.reshape(1, -1), alpha=alpha
    )
    return float(var[0]), float(es[0])


def value_at_risk(portfolio_loss: _F64_1D, alpha: float) -> float:
    """Value-at-Risk at confidence ``alpha`` (upper-tail loss quantile)."""
    return loss_var_es(portfolio_loss, alpha)[0]


def expected_shortfall(portfolio_loss: _F64_1D, alpha: float) -> float:
    """Expected shortfall at ``alpha`` (mean loss beyond VaR)."""
    return loss_var_es(portfolio_loss, alpha)[1]


def tail_var_es_by_group(
    loss_by_group: _F64_2D, alpha: float
) -> tuple[_F64_1D, _F64_1D]:
    """Per-group upper-tail VaR/ES for a ``(G, S)`` stack of group loss rows.

    One parallel kernel pass over all groups — the basis for standalone
    per-segment (e.g. per-model) tail risk.
    """
    return _loss_tail_var_es(loss_by_group=loss_by_group, alpha=alpha)


def loss_quantiles(portfolio_loss: _F64_1D, alphas: _F64_1D) -> _F64_1D:
    """General loss quantiles at each level in ``alphas`` (interpolated)."""
    return np.ascontiguousarray(np.quantile(portfolio_loss, alphas), dtype=np.float64)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class PortfolioLossDistribution:
    """
    The portfolio loss distribution — a per-scenario total-loss vector with the
    standard risk metrics as accessors. The natural carrier for the ``(S,)``
    quantity the metric layer needs, whether collapsed from a resolved JTD loss
    or accumulated online.
    """

    portfolio_loss: _F64_1D  # (S,) total loss per scenario

    @property
    def n_scenarios(self) -> int:
        return int(self.portfolio_loss.shape[0])

    def expected_loss(self) -> float:
        return expected_loss(self.portfolio_loss)

    def var_es(self, alpha: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
        return loss_var_es(self.portfolio_loss, alpha)

    def value_at_risk(self, alpha: float) -> float:
        return value_at_risk(self.portfolio_loss, alpha)

    def expected_shortfall(self, alpha: float) -> float:
        return expected_shortfall(self.portfolio_loss, alpha)

    def quantiles(self, alphas: _F64_1D) -> _F64_1D:
        return loss_quantiles(self.portfolio_loss, alphas)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/aggregate/summary.py
"""
Resolve a JTD loss into the credit package's headline outputs.

`build_portfolio_loss_distribution` collapses the per-obligor ``(n, S)`` loss to
the per-scenario portfolio total. `summarize_jtd_loss` produces the headline risk
summary (EL, plus VaR/ES at a set of confidence levels) and — when the compiled
portfolio is supplied — the additive expected-loss contribution of each JTD
model. `model_tail_var_es` exposes per-model STANDALONE tail risk via one
parallel pass of the shared tail kernel over a per-model loss stack.

EL contributions are additive (sum to portfolio EL). Per-model VaR/ES are
standalone (a model's own tail) and do NOT sum to portfolio VaR/ES — additive
tail allocation (Euler) is a separate, deliberately deferred concern.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "build_portfolio_loss_distribution",
    "summarize_jtd_loss",
    "summarize_streaming_jtd_loss",
    "JtdRiskSummary",
    "model_tail_var_es",
    "streaming_model_tail_var_es",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.aggregate.loss_metrics import (
    PortfolioLossDistribution,
    expected_loss,
    loss_var_es,
    tail_var_es_by_group,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.jtd.results import JtdLossResult, StreamingJtdResult

_DEFAULT_ALPHAS: tuple[float, ...] = (0.9500, 0.9750, 0.9900, 0.9990)


def build_portfolio_loss_distribution(
    result: JtdLossResult,
) -> PortfolioLossDistribution:
    """Collapse the per-obligor ``(n, S)`` loss to the ``(S,)`` portfolio total."""
    portfolio_loss = result.loss.sum(axis=0)
    return PortfolioLossDistribution(portfolio_loss=portfolio_loss)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class JtdRiskSummary:
    """
    Headline portfolio risk numbers resolved from a JTD loss.

    ``var`` / ``es`` are keyed by confidence level. ``expected_loss_by_model`` is
    populated only when the compiled portfolio is provided; its values sum to
    ``expected_loss``.
    """

    n_scenarios: int
    expected_loss: float
    var: dict[float, float]
    es: dict[float, float]
    expected_loss_by_model: dict[str, float]


def _expected_loss_by_model(
    result: JtdLossResult, jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Per-model EL contribution via the portfolio's model bands (additive)."""
    by_model: dict[str, float] = {}
    for i in range(jtd_portfolio.total_blocks):
        block = jtd_portfolio.blocks[i]
        start = int(jtd_portfolio.block_offsets[i])
        end = start + block.num_obligors
        name = JtdModel(block.JTD_MODEL_ID).name
        contribution = float(result.loss[start:end, :].sum(axis=0).mean())
        by_model[name] = by_model.get(name, 0.0) + contribution
    return by_model


def _model_loss_stack(
    result: JtdLossResult, jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio
) -> tuple[list[str], npt.NDArray[np.float64]]:
    """Per-model portfolio loss rows: model names + a ``(n_models, S)`` stack."""
    names: list[str] = []
    rows: list[npt.NDArray[np.float64]] = []
    for i in range(jtd_portfolio.total_blocks):
        block = jtd_portfolio.blocks[i]
        start = int(jtd_portfolio.block_offsets[i])
        end = start + block.num_obligors
        names.append(JtdModel(block.JTD_MODEL_ID).name)
        rows.append(result.loss[start:end, :].sum(axis=0))
    return names, np.ascontiguousarray(np.stack(rows))


def summarize_jtd_loss(
    *,
    result: JtdLossResult,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio | None = None,
    alphas: tuple[float, ...] = _DEFAULT_ALPHAS,
) -> JtdRiskSummary:
    """
    Headline risk summary for a resolved JTD loss.

    Parameters
    ----------
    result
        Resolved per-obligor JTD loss.
    jtd_portfolio
        Optional compiled portfolio; when supplied, per-model expected-loss
        contributions are computed from its model bands.
    alphas
        Confidence levels for VaR and ES.
    """
    portfolio_loss = result.loss.sum(axis=0)
    var: dict[float, float] = {}
    es: dict[float, float] = {}
    for a in alphas:
        var[a], es[a] = loss_var_es(portfolio_loss, a)
    by_model = (
        _expected_loss_by_model(result, jtd_portfolio)
        if jtd_portfolio is not None
        else {}
    )
    return JtdRiskSummary(
        n_scenarios=int(portfolio_loss.shape[0]),
        expected_loss=expected_loss(portfolio_loss),
        var=var,
        es=es,
        expected_loss_by_model=by_model,
    )


def summarize_streaming_jtd_loss(
    *,
    result: StreamingJtdResult,
    alphas: tuple[float, ...] = _DEFAULT_ALPHAS,
) -> JtdRiskSummary:
    """
    Headline risk summary for a streamed JTD loss.

    Operates on the accumulated ``(S,)`` portfolio total directly (no per-obligor
    matrix). Per-model expected-loss contributions are filled when the streaming
    run collected the ``(n_models, S)`` breakdown (`collect_by_model=True`); they
    remain additive (sum to the portfolio EL).
    """
    portfolio_loss = result.portfolio_loss
    var: dict[float, float] = {}
    es: dict[float, float] = {}
    for a in alphas:
        var[a], es[a] = loss_var_es(portfolio_loss, a)

    by_model: dict[str, float] = {}
    if result.model_loss is not None:
        model_el = result.model_loss.mean(axis=1)
        for row, model_id in enumerate(result.model_order):
            name = JtdModel(model_id).name
            by_model[name] = by_model.get(name, 0.0) + float(model_el[row])

    return JtdRiskSummary(
        n_scenarios=int(portfolio_loss.shape[0]),
        expected_loss=expected_loss(portfolio_loss),
        var=var,
        es=es,
        expected_loss_by_model=by_model,
    )


def model_tail_var_es(
    *,
    result: JtdLossResult,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    alpha: float,
) -> dict[str, tuple[float, float]]:
    """Standalone per-model ``(VaR, ES)`` at ``alpha`` — one parallel kernel pass.

    These are each model's own tail risk; they do not sum to the portfolio's.
    """
    names, stack = _model_loss_stack(result, jtd_portfolio)
    var, es = tail_var_es_by_group(stack, alpha)
    return {name: (float(var[i]), float(es[i])) for i, name in enumerate(names)}


def streaming_model_tail_var_es(
    *,
    result: StreamingJtdResult,
    alpha: float,
) -> dict[str, tuple[float, float]]:
    """Standalone per-model ``(VaR, ES)`` at ``alpha`` from a streamed run.

    Requires the run to have collected the per-model breakdown
    (`collect_by_model=True`); one parallel kernel pass over the ``(n_models, S)``
    stack. As with the non-streaming variant these are each model's own tail and
    do not sum to the portfolio's.
    """
    if result.model_loss is None:
        raise ValueError(
            "streaming_model_tail_var_es requires a run with collect_by_model=True "
            "(result.model_loss is None)."
        )
    var, es = tail_var_es_by_group(result.model_loss, alpha)
    return {
        JtdModel(model_id).name: (float(var[i]), float(es[i]))
        for i, model_id in enumerate(result.model_order)
    }
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\compile.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/compile.py

"""
Exposure compiler: raw trade -> netting-set CSR views.

PURE PROJECTION KERNEL.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

HARD INVARIANTS (ENFORCED)

- Every trade MUST map to a netting set uid
- Every netting set uid MUST exist in the master
- No implicit singleton construction
- No partial joins
- No silent fallback logic

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

DESIGN

- Structure = CSR over netting sets
- Attributes = strict projection from master
- Join primitive = centralized (searchsorted_or_raise)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_raise
from riskquant.constants import NO_ID
from riskquant.credit.exposure.compiled import CompiledExposure
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView
from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster

# =====================================================================
# Validation
# =====================================================================


def _validate_inputs(
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    if trade_uids.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("trade_uids must be int64")

    if trade_netting_set_id.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("trade_netting_set_id must be int64")

    if trade_uids.ndim != 1 or trade_netting_set_id.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("inputs must be 1D")

    if trade_uids.shape[0] != trade_netting_set_id.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError("trade_uids and trade_netting_set_id must align")


def _validate_master(master: CompiledNettingSetMaster) -> None:
    if master.uid.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("netting_set_master.uid must be int64")

    if master.net_collateral.dtype != np.float64:
        raise TypeError("netting_set_master.net_collateral must be float64")

    if master.uid.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("netting_set_master.uid must be 1D")

    if master.uid.size >= 2 and not np.all(master.uid[:-1] < master.uid[1:]):
        raise ValueError("netting_set_master.uid must be strictly ascending and unique")


def _validate_no_missing_netting_sets(
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    if np.any(trade_netting_set_id == NO_ID):
        raise ValueError(
            "NO_ID detected. "
            "All trades must be assigned to a valid or singleton netting set upstream."
        )


# =====================================================================
# CSR construction (structure only)
# =====================================================================


def _build_csr(
    netting_set_per_row: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> tuple[
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # netting_set_uids
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # offsets
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # mtm_row_perm
]:
    """
    Deterministic grouping of trades by netting set.

    Guarantees:
    - netting_set_uids sorted ascending
    - stable ordering within each netting set
    """

    order = np.argsort(netting_set_per_row, kind="stable")

    mtm_row_perm = np.ascontiguousarray(order, dtype=np.int64)
    sorted_ns = netting_set_per_row[order]

    netting_set_uids, counts = np.unique(sorted_ns, return_counts=True)
    netting_set_uids = np.ascontiguousarray(netting_set_uids, dtype=np.int64)

    offsets = np.empty(netting_set_uids.size + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    offsets[0] = 0
    np.cumsum(counts, out=offsets[1:])

    return netting_set_uids, offsets, mtm_row_perm


# =====================================================================
# Attribute projection (STRICT)
# =====================================================================


def _project_netting_set_attributes(
    netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Strict projection from master.

    Invariant:
    - Every netting_set_uid MUST exist in master.uid
    """

    pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        raw=netting_set_uids,
        universe=master.uid,
        ctx="compile_exposure: netting_set_uids",
    )

    collateral = np.ascontiguousarray(
        master.net_collateral[pos],
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    return collateral


# =====================================================================
# Public API
# =====================================================================


def compile_exposure(
    *,
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> CompiledExposure:
    """
    Compile exposure into a Replacement Cost netting-set CSR view.

    This function is a PURE projection kernel:
    - no mutation
    - no inference
    - no fallback logic

    Upstream responsibilities:
    - assign singleton netting sets
    - ensure full master coverage
    """

    # ---- Validate inputs ----
    _validate_inputs(trade_uids, trade_netting_set_id)
    _validate_master(netting_set_master)
    _validate_no_missing_netting_sets(trade_netting_set_id)

    # ---- Build structure ----
    netting_set_uids, offsets, mtm_row_perm = _build_csr(trade_netting_set_id)

    # ---- Project attributes ----
    collateral = _project_netting_set_attributes(
        netting_set_uids,
        netting_set_master,
    )

    # ---- Construct view ----
    rc_view = RcNettingSetView(
        netting_set_uids=netting_set_uids,
        netting_set_offsets=offsets,
        mtm_row_perm=mtm_row_perm,
        collateral=collateral,
    )

    return CompiledExposure(replacement_cost=rc_view)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\compiled.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/compiled.py
"""
Compiled exposure container.

`CompiledExposure` is the EAD-layer analogue of `CompiledPortfolio`: an ordered
set of per-model netting-set views. Each populated view contributes one
contiguous row-band to the eventual (n_netting_sets x n_scenarios) EAD matrix;
`view_offsets[i]` is its first row. `netting_set_uids` aggregates every view's
uids into one flat int64 array in band order — the join key into the JTD layer.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TypeAlias

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView

ViewT: TypeAlias = RcNettingSetView  # | SaccrNettingSetView | ...  (future)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledExposure:
    """EAD-ready exposure: an ordered set of per-model netting-set views."""

    # VIEWS: One per exposure model. Each has its own netting set membership, collateral, etc.
    replacement_cost: RcNettingSetView | None = None

    # DERIVED FIELDS: Populated at init by __post_init__. These are the fields the engine dispatches on.
    _views: tuple[ViewT, ...] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _view_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_views: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_netting_sets: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        ordered: tuple[ViewT | None, ...] = (self.replacement_cost,)
        views: tuple[ViewT, ...] = tuple(v for v in ordered if v is not None)
        total_views = len(views)

        # 1. Calculate the total size needed
        total_ns = sum(v.num_netting_sets for v in views)

        # 2. Allocate uninitialized arrays (faster than zeros)
        view_offsets = np.empty(total_views, dtype=np.int64)
        netting_set_uids = np.empty(total_ns, dtype=np.int64)

        # 3. Populate both arrays in a single, clean pass
        offset = 0
        for i, v in enumerate(views):
            view_offsets[i] = offset
            n = v.num_netting_sets
            netting_set_uids[offset : offset + n] = v.netting_set_uids
            offset += n

        object.__setattr__(self, "_views", views)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_view_offsets", view_offsets)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_views", total_views)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_netting_sets", total_ns)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_netting_set_uids", netting_set_uids)

    @property
    def views(self) -> tuple[ViewT, ...]:
        return self._views

    @property
    def view_offsets(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._view_offsets

    @property
    def total_views(self) -> int:
        return self._total_views

    @property
    def total_netting_sets(self) -> int:
        return self._total_netting_sets

    @property
    def netting_set_uids(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._netting_set_uids

    def iter_views(self) -> Iterator[ViewT]:
        yield from self._views

    def summary(self) -> str:
        """Return a console-friendly overview of compiled exposure layout."""

        lines: list[str] = []

        width = 90

        lines.append("=" * width)
        lines.append(f"{'COMPILED EXPOSURE SUMMARY':^{width}}")
        lines.append("=" * width)

        lines.append(f"Total Views        : {self._total_views:,}")
        lines.append(f"Total Netting Sets : {self._total_netting_sets:,}")

        lines.append("-" * width)
        lines.append(f"{'View Type':<40} {'Netting Sets':>15} {'Offset':>15}")
        lines.append("-" * width)

        if self._total_views == 0:
            lines.append(f"{'Empty Exposure':^{width}}")
        else:
            for offset, view in zip(
                self._view_offsets,
                self._views,
                strict=True,
            ):
                lines.append(
                    f"{type(view).__name__:<40} "
                    f"{view.num_netting_sets:>15,} "
                    f"{offset:>15,}"
                )

        lines.append("=" * width)

        return "\n".join(lines)

    def print_view_details(self, max_items: int | None = 10) -> None:
        """Cascade into each view and print its raw SoA arrays."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{f'VIEW DETAILS (MAX {max_items} ITEMS PER VIEW)':^90}")
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
        for view in self._views:
            title = f"View: {type(view).__name__}"
            print("-" * len(title))
            print(title)
            print("-" * len(title))
            view.print(max_items=max_items)
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\engine.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/engine.py
"""
Exposure engine: model-id -> evaluator dispatch and the simulated exposure resolution loop.

`EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS` is the exposure-layer analogue of `valuation.engine.BLOCK_EVALUATORS`:
a frozen tuple indexed by `ExposureModel` id. `run_exposure` reads each view's
`EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID` class constant, indexes the table, and dispatches — no
`type()`, no isinstance ladder. Adding SA-CCR is: one enum member, one view,
one evaluator, one `_register` line. The import-time guard rejects any model
that ships unwired.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Final, Protocol, TypeVar, cast, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.compiled import CompiledExposure
from riskquant.credit.exposure.evaluators.replacement_cost import (
    evaluate_replacement_cost,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.models import (
    NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS,
    REPLACEMENT_COST_ID,
    ExposureModel,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.results import NettingSetExposurePaths
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.base import _NettingSetView

V_contra = TypeVar("V_contra", bound=_NettingSetView, contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class ExposureEvaluator(Protocol[V_contra]):
    """Structural contract for all exposure-model evaluators."""

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        view: V_contra,
        mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...


_EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS_MUT: list[ExposureEvaluator[Any] | None] = [
    None
] * NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS


def _register(model_id: int, evaluator: ExposureEvaluator[Any]) -> None:
    if not isinstance(evaluator, ExposureEvaluator):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Evaluator for model_id={model_id} does not satisfy the "
            f"ExposureEvaluator protocol: {evaluator!r}"
        )
    _EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS_MUT[model_id] = evaluator


_register(REPLACEMENT_COST_ID, evaluate_replacement_cost)

EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[ExposureEvaluator[Any], ...]] = cast(
    tuple[ExposureEvaluator[Any], ...], tuple(_EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS_MUT)
)

_unregistered = [
    ExposureModel(i).name for i, ev in enumerate(EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS) if ev is None
]
if _unregistered:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No evaluator registered for exposure models: {_unregistered}. "
        f"Register them in exposure/engine.py."
    )


def run_exposure(
    *,
    exposure: CompiledExposure,
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> NettingSetExposurePaths:
    """
    Resolve simulated exposure across all model views from a portfolio MtM matrix.

    Walks the compiled views in band order, dispatches each on its
    `EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID`, and writes into contiguous bands of one pre-allocated
    (n_netting_sets x n_scenarios) output. Mirror of `run_valuation`.
    """
    n_sets = exposure.total_netting_sets
    n_scenarios = mtm.shape[1]

    # Pre-allocated contiguous block for the evaluators to write into
    out_exposure: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (n_sets, n_scenarios), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    views = exposure.views
    offsets = exposure.view_offsets
    evaluators_table = EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS

    for i in range(exposure.total_views):
        view = views[i]
        start = int(offsets[i])
        end = start + view.num_netting_sets

        evaluator = evaluators_table[view.EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID]
        evaluator(view=view, mtm=mtm, out=out_exposure[start:end, :])

    return NettingSetExposurePaths(uid=exposure.netting_set_uids, exposure=out_exposure)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\evaluators\kernels\replacement_cost.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/evaluators/kernels/replacement_cost.py
"""
Replacement Cost (RC) exposure model: kernel.

    V  = sum of MtM over the netting set's member trades
    RC = max(V - C, 0)

`_kernel_replacement_cost` computes RC for all netting sets and scenarios
in one parallel pass. The kernel uses positional-or-keyword arguments
(NOT keyword-only): Numba's parsing of the `*,` marker is unreliable
across versions and inconsistent with every other JTD kernel in the
codebase. The evaluator wrapper materializes the SoA fields from the
view before crossing the JIT boundary.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import parallel_njit

_F64_2D = nb.float64[:, ::1]
_F64_1D = nb.float64[::1]
_I64_1D = nb.int64[::1]


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out
        _F64_2D,  # mtm
        _I64_1D,  # mtm_row_perm
        _I64_1D,  # ns_offsets
        _F64_1D,  # collateral
    )
)
def _kernel_replacement_cost(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_netting_sets, s_scenarios)
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M_total, s_scenarios)
    mtm_row_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (M_kept,)
    ns_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (n_netting_sets + 1,)
    collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_netting_sets,)
) -> None:
    n_net_sets, s_scenarios = out.shape
    for n in nb.prange(n_net_sets):
        start = ns_offsets[n]
        end = ns_offsets[n + 1]
        coll = collateral[n]
        out_row = out[n]
        if end > start:
            row0 = mtm[mtm_row_perm[start]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = row0[s]
            for i in range(start + 1, end):
                row = mtm[mtm_row_perm[i]]
                for s in range(s_scenarios):
                    out_row[s] += row[s]
        else:
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = 0.0
        # RC = max(V - C, 0).
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = max(out_row[s] - coll, 0.0)
```
</file>

# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/evaluators/replacement_cost.py
"""
Replacement Cost (RC) exposure model: evaluator.

    V  = sum of MtM over the netting set's member trades
    RC = max(V - C, 0)

`_kernel_replacement_cost` computes RC for all netting sets and scenarios
in one parallel pass.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.evaluators.kernels.replacement_cost import (
    _kernel_replacement_cost,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView


def evaluate_replacement_cost(
    *,
    view: RcNettingSetView,
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Unpack the view SoA fields and dispatch to the JIT kernel."""
    _kernel_replacement_cost(
        out=out,
        mtm=mtm,
        mtm_row_perm=view.mtm_row_perm,
        ns_offsets=view.netting_set_offsets,
        collateral=view.collateral,
    )

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/models.py
"""
Exposure-model dispatch identifiers.

`ExposureModel` is the exposure-layer analogue of `portfolio.instruments.Instrument`:
the single integer the exposure engine dispatches on. It indexes directly into
`EXPOSURE_EVALUATORS`. The enum holds only IMPLEMENTED models — it grows by one
member each time a model is wired, and the import-time guard in `engine.py`
fails loudly if a member ships without an evaluator.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final


class ExposureModel(IntEnum):
    """ExposureModel is the single integer dispatch key for the exposure engine."""

    # --- (0) Replacement Cost (RC) for netting sets with no margin agreements ---

    # RC(NS) = MAX(MTM(NS) - COLLATERAL(NS), 0)
    REPLACEMENT_COST = 0

    # --- (1) Replacement Cost with Margin (RCM) for netting sets with margin agreements ---


NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS: Final[int] = len(ExposureModel)

REPLACEMENT_COST_ID: Final[int] = ExposureModel.REPLACEMENT_COST.value
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/obligor_ead/align.py
"""
Obligor-EAD -> JTD portfolio alignment.

The obligor-grain analogue of `jtd.bridge.aggregate_ead_to_obligor`: that bridge
reindexes netting-set EAD onto a JTD portfolio's band order; this reindexes EAD
that is already at obligor grain. Same join tool (`searchsorted_or_raise`), same
target order (`jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`, which is band order — an internal
compaction artifact, not the caller's order).

The semantics here are STRICT in the opposite direction to the bridge: every
obligor the JTD portfolio models must have an EAD row (a modelled obligor with no
exposure is a gap in the inputs, not something to silently zero). Obligors
present in the EAD but absent from the portfolio are simply unused.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "align_obligor_ead_to_portfolio",
]

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_raise
from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.compiled import CompiledObligorEad


def align_obligor_ead_to_portfolio(
    *,
    obligor_ead: CompiledObligorEad,
    obligor_uids_target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Reindex obligor EAD onto a target obligor-uid order (band order).

    Parameters
    ----------
    obligor_ead
        Validated, uid-sorted obligor EAD; `ead` is ``(N,)`` or ``(N, S)``.
    obligor_uids_target
        Target obligor-uid order — typically `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`.

    Returns
    -------
    The EAD reindexed onto `obligor_uids_target`: ``(n_target,)`` for scalar EAD,
    ``(n_target, S)`` for scenario-varying. C-contiguous, ready to feed (after any
    broadcast) the bare-array engine core.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any target obligor has no EAD row (`searchsorted_or_raise`).
    """
    pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        universe=obligor_ead.uid,
        raw=obligor_uids_target,
        ctx="align_obligor_ead_to_portfolio: obligor uid(s)",
    )
    return np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_ead.ead[pos])
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/obligor_ead/compiled.py
"""
Obligor-grain exposure-at-default, keyed by uid.

`CompiledObligorEad` is the obligor-level analogue of the exposure engine's
netting-set EAD: exposure that already lives at obligor grain — a vendor feed, or
a desk's direct input — carrying its own uid key. That key is what lets it be
reindexed onto a JTD portfolio's band order by uid (a `searchsorted` join) rather
than by the positional convention a bare ``(N, S)`` array would force on the
caller. `uid` is ascending-sorted and unique: the structural invariant the join
relies on.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledObligorEad",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledObligorEad:
    """
    Per-obligor EAD with its uid join key.

    `ead` is either per-obligor scalar ``(N,)`` (scenario-independent, e.g. a desk
    popping in one exposure per name) or scenario-varying ``(N, S)`` (e.g. a
    vendor exposure feed); its rows align to `uid`. `uid` is ascending-sorted and
    unique. Validation of dtypes and values lives in `compile_obligor_ead`; this
    container enforces only the structural invariants the uid-join depends on.
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,) ascending sorted, unique — the join key
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,) or (N, S); rows aligned to uid

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.uid.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(
                f"CompiledObligorEad: uid must be 1-D, got {self.uid.ndim}-D."
            )
        if self.ead.ndim not in (1, 2):
            raise ValueError(
                f"CompiledObligorEad: ead must be 1-D (N,) or 2-D (N, S), got "
                f"{self.ead.ndim}-D."
            )
        if self.ead.shape[0] != self.uid.shape[0]:
            raise ValueError(
                f"CompiledObligorEad: ead rows {self.ead.shape[0]} != uid count "
                f"{self.uid.shape[0]}."
            )
        if self.uid.shape[0] > 1 and not bool((np.diff(self.uid) > 0).all()):
            raise ValueError(
                "CompiledObligorEad: uid must be ascending-sorted and unique "
                "(the searchsorted join key)."
            )

    @property
    def num_obligors(self) -> int:
        return self.uid.shape[0]

    @property
    def is_scenario_varying(self) -> bool:
        return self.ead.ndim == 2
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/obligor_ead/ingest.py
"""
Obligor-EAD ingest (the edge).

`compile_obligor_ead` is the perimeter where a human- or vendor-authored EAD
table becomes a validated, uid-sorted SoA container. It takes only the uid + EAD
columns, so the same single sheet a desk authors (uid, ead, pd, lgd, ...) can be
fed here for exposure and to `compile_obligor_master` for credit, each picking
the columns it owns. Fail-fast on anything that would corrupt the downstream
uid-join or the loss identity: missing columns, non-finite EAD, negative EAD,
duplicate uids.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "compile_obligor_ead",
]

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.compiled import CompiledObligorEad


def compile_obligor_ead(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    uid_col: str = "uid",
    ead_col: str = "ead",
) -> CompiledObligorEad:
    """
    Compile a uid + EAD table into a validated `CompiledObligorEad`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df
        Table carrying at least `uid_col` and `ead_col`; other columns are
        ignored, so the same sheet can also feed `compile_obligor_master`.
    uid_col, ead_col
        Column names for the obligor uid and its per-obligor exposure.

    Notes
    -----
    The rows are sorted by uid here, so callers need not pre-sort. Scenario-
    varying EAD is not authored through this scalar-table edge; build a
    `CompiledObligorEad` with an ``(N, S)`` `ead` directly for that.
    """
    for col in (uid_col, ead_col):
        if col not in df.columns:
            raise ValueError(f"compile_obligor_ead: missing required column '{col}'.")

    uid = np.ascontiguousarray(df[uid_col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))

    ead_raw = df[ead_col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(ead_raw).all():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_obligor_ead: non-finite value in EAD column '{ead_col}'."
        )
    if (ead_raw < 0.0).any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_obligor_ead: negative EAD in column '{ead_col}' "
            "(exposure-at-default must be >= 0)."
        )

    if uid.shape[0] != np.unique(uid).shape[0]:
        raise ValueError("compile_obligor_ead: duplicate obligor uid(s).")

    # Sort by uid so the container's join-key invariant holds regardless of the
    # authoring order.
    order = np.argsort(uid, kind="stable")
    uid_sorted = np.ascontiguousarray(uid[order])
    ead_sorted = np.ascontiguousarray(ead_raw[order])

    return CompiledObligorEad(uid=uid_sorted, ead=ead_sorted)
```
</file>
