```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/results.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class NettingSetExposurePaths:
    """
    NettingSetExposurePaths holds the exposure paths for a set of netting sets across
    multiple scenarios.

    NettingSetExposurePaths.exposure --> (n_netting_sets, n_scenarios) array of exposure values.

    Row i of every array describes the netting set identified by ``uid[i]``.
    ``uid`` is the join key into the downstream credit layer (the ``uid``
    column consumed by the deterministic / copula JTD ingest cases).
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,)     effective-netting-set identifier
    exposure: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N, S)   Exposure per netting set, per scenario

    def print(
        self,
        *,
        max_rows: int = 10,
        precision: int = 0,
        summary: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Pretty-print the simulated exposure result.

        Parameters
        ----------
        max_rows
            Maximum number of rows to display.
        precision
            Number of decimal places for floats.
        summary
            Whether to print global matrix summary statistics.
        """
        n_rows, n_scenarios = self.exposure.shape

        print(
            f"NettingSetExposurePaths: {n_rows} netting sets × {n_scenarios} scenarios"
        )

        display_rows = min(n_rows, max_rows)

        # Adjusted headers to accommodate the row-wise Expected Exposure
        print(f"\n{'UID':>12} | {'Expected Exposure (EE)':>22} | Simulated Paths")
        print("-" * 91)

        # Local print-options scope: never mutate numpy globals.
        with np.printoptions(precision=precision, suppress=True):
            for i in range(display_rows):
                uid_str = f"{self.uid[i]:>12_.0f}"

                # Expected Exposure: The mean of this specific netting set across all scenarios
                ee_str = f"{self.exposure[i].mean():>22_.{precision}f}"

                # The raw stochastic distribution paths
                exposure_str = np.array2string(
                    self.exposure[i],
                    separator=", ",
                    formatter={"float_kind": lambda x: f"{x:>18_.{precision}f}"},
                )

                print(f"{uid_str} | {ee_str} | {exposure_str}")

        if display_rows < n_rows:
            print(f"... ({n_rows - display_rows} more rows)")

        if summary:
            # Renamed to clarify these are global matrix stats, not counterparty metrics
            print("\nGlobal matrix statistics (across all netting sets & scenarios):")
            print(f"  Mean : {self.exposure.mean():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Std  : {self.exposure.std():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Min  : {self.exposure.min():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Max  : {self.exposure.max():>18_.{precision}f}")
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\views\base.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/views/base.py
"""
Base class for compiled netting-set views.

`_NettingSetView` carries only what is universal to every EAD model: the CSR
band layout that groups portfolio-order MtM rows into contiguous per-netting-set
spans. All model-specific SoA arrays (collateral, notionals, supervisory
factors, ...) live on the concrete subclasses.

`EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID` is the integer the exposure engine dispatches on — it
indexes `EAD_EVALUATORS` directly. The `_UNSET` sentinel is rejected at
class-creation time, a compile-time guard against adding a model view without
wiring its id.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: int = -999


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _NettingSetView:
    """Universal CSR base for compiled netting-set views."""

    EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = _UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID

    # Effective-netting-set identifiers, one per band. dtype: int64
    netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,)

    # Exclusive prefix-sum band boundaries into the reordered MtM matrix. int64
    netting_set_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N + 1,)

    # Permutation: global portfolio MtM rows -> NS-contiguous order. int64
    mtm_row_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (M_view,)

    def __init_subclass__(cls, **kwargs: object) -> None:
        is_abstract_base = cls.__name__.startswith("_")
        if not is_abstract_base and (cls.EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID == _UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID):
            raise TypeError(
                f"Netting-set view '{cls.__name__}' must set EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID "
                f"(its ExposureModel id) for engine dispatch."
            )

    @property
    def num_netting_sets(self) -> int:
        return self.netting_set_uids.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_trades(self) -> int:
        return self.mtm_row_perm.shape[0]
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/views/replacement_cost.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.models import REPLACEMENT_COST_ID
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.base import _NettingSetView


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class RcNettingSetView(_NettingSetView):
    """Replacement Cost netting-set view: CSR grouping + net collateral."""

    EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = REPLACEMENT_COST_ID

    collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    def print(
        self,
        *,
        max_items: int | None = 10,
        precision: int = 2,
        show_collateral: bool = True,
        add_space_before_table: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Pretty-print the RcNettingSetView.

        Parameters
        ----------
        max_rows
            Maximum number of netting sets to display.
        precision
            Number of decimal places for floats.
        show_collateral
            Whether to display the collateral column.
        add_space_before_table
            Insert a blank line before the table header.
        """
        max_items = max_items if max_items is not None else 10
        n_sets = self.num_netting_sets
        n_trades = self.num_trades

        if add_space_before_table:
            print()

        print(f"RcNettingSetView: {n_sets} netting sets, {n_trades} trades")

        display_rows = min(n_sets, max_items)

        header = f"{'Netting Set UID':>16}"
        if show_collateral:
            header += f" | {'Collateral':>12}"
        header += f" | {'Netting Set Offsets':>20}"
        print()
        print(header)
        print("-" * 80)

        with np.printoptions(precision=precision, suppress=True):
            for i in range(display_rows):
                uid_str = f"{self.netting_set_uids[i]:>16_.0f}"

                collateral_str = ""
                if show_collateral:
                    collateral_str = f" | {self.collateral[i]:>12_.{precision}f}"

                offsets = self.netting_set_offsets[i : i + 2]
                offsets_str = f" | [{offsets[0]:>6}, {offsets[1]:>6})"

                print(f"{uid_str}{collateral_str}{offsets_str}")

            if display_rows < n_sets:
                print(f"... ({n_sets - display_rows} more netting sets)")

            print(f"\nSummary statistics (across {n_sets} netting sets):")
            print(f"  Total collateral: {self.collateral.sum():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Mean collateral : {self.collateral.mean():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Std collateral  : {self.collateral.std():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Min collateral  : {self.collateral.min():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Max collateral  : {self.collateral.max():>16_.{precision}f}")

            print(f"\nPermutation (first {min(10, n_trades)} rows):")
            print(f"  mtm_row_perm[:10] = {self.mtm_row_perm[:10]}")
            if n_trades > 10:
                print(f"  ... ({n_trades - 10} more)")
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/base.py
"""
Credit-factor scenario generation contract.

A credit-factor batch holds LATENT draws (systemic Z, idiosyncratic eps,
chi-square mixing W) — not market shocks. There is no base level, shift type,
or shock scale: latent factors are a different sample space, so this is a
credit-native protocol, deliberately NOT `simlab`'s `ScenarioGenerator`.

`build_credit_factor_matrix` assembles streamed batches into the C-contiguous
`(num_credit_factors, num_scenarios)` `CreditFactorMatrix` the JTD kernels read.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "build_credit_factor_matrix",
]

from collections.abc import Iterable, Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class CreditFactorBatch:
    """
    A streamed batch of latent credit-factor draws.

    `latent_factors` is `(batch_size, F)` — scenario-major, the streaming-
    friendly layout. `build_credit_factor_matrix` transposes it into the
    factor-major `CreditFactorMatrix`.
    """

    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (batch_size, F)
    scenario_offset: int
    scenario_count_total: int


class CreditFactorGenerator(Protocol):
    """Streaming-first credit-factor generation."""

    total_scenarios: int
    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]: ...


def build_credit_factor_matrix(
    batches: Iterable[CreditFactorBatch],
    *,
    num_factors: int,
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> CreditFactorMatrix:
    """
    Assemble streamed `(batch, F)` batches into a `(F, S)` C-contiguous matrix.

    The transpose happens once, at the edge, during assembly — the JTD kernels
    then index factor rows with the scenario axis contiguous.
    """

    # =====================================================================
    # DOD MEMORY LAYOUT: C-CONTIGUOUS
    # =====================================================================
    # We deliberately violate the generic "scenario arrays are F-order" rule.
    # The JTD kernels process obligor-by-obligor, loading factor rows via:
    #     z_row = latent_factors[uix]
    #
    # C-order guarantees `z_row` is contiguous. This allows the innermost
    # `for s in range(scenarios)` loop to execute as straight-line SIMD
    # over unit-stride memory. F-order would result in strided reads.
    # =====================================================================
    latent: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (num_factors, num_scenarios),
        dtype=np.float64,
        order="C",
    )
    for batch in batches:
        off = batch.scenario_offset
        bs = batch.latent_factors.shape[0]
        latent[:, off : off + bs] = batch.latent_factors.T

    return CreditFactorMatrix(latent_factors=latent)
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\mixed.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/mixed.py
r"""Mixed credit-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

The general-case generator: fills ONE `CreditFactorMatrix` whose rows carry
different marginals, so a portfolio spanning multiple JTD model families
(copula + Bernoulli + stress-regime) draws from a single coherent factor pool.

`GaussianCopulaGenerator`, `IndependentUniformGenerator` and `TCopulaGenerator`
are special cases of this one — kept separate because their single-marginal
fill is one contiguous draw with no per-column dispatch. Reach for this
generator only when a matrix must hold more than one marginal at once.

Row marginals
-------------
Every column not listed below is an independent standard normal (systemic Z,
idiosyncratic eps — the copula factor rows). The listed columns are overwritten:

    uniform_columns        : U(0, 1)                 — independent-Bernoulli rows
    chi_columns            : 1 / sqrt(W), W ~ chi^2(nu)
                             — t-copula mixing rows (same precompute as
                             `TCopulaGenerator`; the kernel multiplies, never
                             takes a sqrt in the hot loop)
    indicator_columns      : {0.0, 1.0}, P(=1) = p   — stress-regime rows
    beta_columns           : P ~ Beta(alpha, beta)   — beta-binomial common-PD
                             rows (the per-scenario stochastic default
                             probability the kernel thresholds each obligor's
                             independent uniform against)

Dependence
----------
NONE is enforced here. Obligor-to-obligor correlation is built in the JTD
kernels from factor loadings against SHARED row indices (`A_i = beta_i * Z +
sqrt(1 - beta_i^2) * eps_i`; two obligors are correlated iff they reference the
same systemic row). Coupling a uniform row to a systemic factor would convert
the independent-Bernoulli model into a Gaussian copula — that is what
`COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN` is for. The one correlation that legitimately belongs at
the generator level — covariance AMONG systemic factors in a multi-factor
copula (a Cholesky over a designated systemic sub-block, with every other row
left independent and PIT-transformed off that core) — is deferred until
multi-factor copula support lands.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused buffer.

LAYOUT
------
- Main buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)`` so the partial-batch slice
  ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is C-contiguous — required by ``rng.standard_normal(out=
  ...)`` and ``rng.random(out=...)``.
- A 1D ``scratch`` buffer (length ``batch_size``) backs each non-normal column
  fill: the draw targets the contiguous scratch, then the strided write
  ``buffer[:bs, col] = scratch[:bs]`` lands it in the factor column. Filling a
  factor column directly would require a non-contiguous ``out=``, which numpy
  rejects. ``bool_scratch`` backs the indicator threshold the same way.
- Both scratch buffers are allocated once at setup and reused every batch — no
  heap allocation inside the streaming loop.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "MixedCreditFactorGenerator",
]

from collections.abc import Iterator, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
        CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
    )


def _empty_i32() -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)


def _empty_f64() -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MixedCreditFactorGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """Zero-allocation heterogeneous-marginal latent-factor generator.

    Parameters
    ----------
    available_factor_ids
        (F,) int32 — the full credit-factor universe. Its length sets the
        number of columns; column position == factor UIX.
    total_scenarios
        Number of scenarios to stream.
    uniform_columns
        (n_u,) int32 — factor columns filled with U(0, 1).
    chi_columns, chi_nu
        (n_c,) int32 / (n_c,) float64 — chi columns and their aligned degrees
        of freedom. Each column emits ``1 / sqrt(W)`` with ``W ~ chi^2(nu)``.
        ``nu`` MUST match the obligor ``nu`` of every t-copula obligor that
        references the column (the kernel's ``sqrt(nu_i / W)`` transform breaks
        the t-marginal otherwise).
    indicator_columns, indicator_p
        (n_i,) int32 / (n_i,) float64 — stress-regime columns and their aligned
        stress probabilities in [0, 1]. Each column emits {0.0, 1.0} with
        ``P(=1) = p``.
    seed
        Optional RNG seed.

    Columns not listed in any of the three column arrays are independent
    standard normals. The three column sets must be pairwise disjoint.
    """

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    uniform_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(default_factory=_empty_i32)
    chi_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(default_factory=_empty_i32)
    chi_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(default_factory=_empty_f64)
    indicator_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(default_factory=_empty_i32)
    indicator_p: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(default_factory=_empty_f64)
    beta_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(default_factory=_empty_i32)
    beta_alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(default_factory=_empty_f64)
    beta_beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(default_factory=_empty_f64)
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    @classmethod
    def from_universe(
        cls,
        universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
        *,
        total_scenarios: int,
        seed: int | None = None,
        stress_p_by_uix: Mapping[int, float] | None = None,
    ) -> MixedCreditFactorGenerator:
        """Build from the credit factor-universe master.

        Every structural column set — chi, uniform, stress-indicator — is
        derived from the universe's kind data, and the chi dof come from
        `chi_nu_vector`. This produces a matrix with the correct marginal on
        EVERY universe row, so it cannot disagree with what JTD ingest expects
        for any model family in the portfolio.

        `stress_p_by_uix` supplies the per-run stress probability for each
        STRESS_INDICATOR factor (keyed by UIX) — the one knob deliberately kept
        off the static universe. Required iff the universe declares any
        stress-indicator factors.
        """
        indicator_columns = universe.stress_indicator_uix
        if indicator_columns.size == 0:
            indicator_p = _empty_f64()
        else:
            if stress_p_by_uix is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    "from_universe: stress_p_by_uix is required — the universe "
                    f"declares STRESS_INDICATOR factor(s) at UIX "
                    f"{indicator_columns.tolist()}, and stress probability is a "
                    "per-run parameter, not a static universe attribute."
                )
            try:
                indicator_p = np.array(
                    [float(stress_p_by_uix[int(c)]) for c in indicator_columns],
                    dtype=np.float64,
                )
            except KeyError as missing:
                raise ValueError(
                    "from_universe: stress_p_by_uix has no entry for "
                    f"STRESS_INDICATOR UIX {missing}."
                ) from None

        return cls(
            available_factor_ids=universe.factor_ids,
            total_scenarios=total_scenarios,
            uniform_columns=universe.uniform_uix,
            chi_columns=universe.chi_uix,
            chi_nu=universe.chi_nu_vector,
            indicator_columns=indicator_columns,
            indicator_p=indicator_p,
            beta_columns=universe.beta_pd_uix,
            beta_alpha=universe.beta_alpha,
            beta_beta=universe.beta_beta,
            seed=seed,
        )

    def chi_nu_by_uix(self) -> dict[int, float]:
        """{chi factor UIX -> dof} — the reconciliation map JTD ingest checks
        obligor ``nu`` against. Pass the result as
        ``compile_jtd_portfolio_from_df(..., chi_nu_by_uix=...)``."""
        return dict(zip(self.chi_columns.tolist(), self.chi_nu.tolist(), strict=True))

    def _validate(self, n_factors: int) -> None:
        if self.chi_columns.shape != self.chi_nu.shape:
            raise ValueError("chi_columns and chi_nu must be aligned")
        if self.indicator_columns.shape != self.indicator_p.shape:
            raise ValueError("indicator_columns and indicator_p must be aligned")
        if not (
            self.beta_columns.shape == self.beta_alpha.shape == self.beta_beta.shape
        ):
            raise ValueError("beta_columns, beta_alpha and beta_beta must be aligned")
        if self.chi_nu.size and not np.all(self.chi_nu > 0.0):
            raise ValueError("chi_nu must be strictly positive")
        if self.indicator_p.size and not np.all(
            (self.indicator_p >= 0.0) & (self.indicator_p <= 1.0)
        ):
            raise ValueError("indicator_p must lie in [0, 1]")
        if self.beta_alpha.size and not np.all(
            (self.beta_alpha > 0.0) & (self.beta_beta > 0.0)
        ):
            raise ValueError("beta_alpha and beta_beta must be strictly positive")

        special = np.concatenate(
            (
                self.uniform_columns,
                self.chi_columns,
                self.indicator_columns,
                self.beta_columns,
            )
        )
        if special.size == 0:
            return
        if special.min() < 0 or special.max() >= n_factors:
            raise ValueError(
                f"factor column out of range [0, {n_factors}): "
                f"{special.min()}..{special.max()}"
            )
        if np.unique(special).size != special.size:
            raise ValueError(
                "a factor column is assigned more than one marginal "
                "(uniform / chi / indicator sets must be disjoint)"
            )

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        self._validate(n_factors)

        uniform_cols = self.uniform_columns
        chi_cols = self.chi_columns
        chi_shape_halved = self.chi_nu * 0.5  # chi^2(nu) = 2 * Gamma(nu/2)
        ind_cols = self.indicator_columns
        ind_p = self.indicator_p
        beta_cols = self.beta_columns
        beta_alpha = self.beta_alpha
        beta_beta = self.beta_beta

        n_uniform = int(uniform_cols.shape[0])
        n_chi = int(chi_cols.shape[0])
        n_ind = int(ind_cols.shape[0])
        n_beta = int(beta_cols.shape[0])

        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # Main buffer: C-order so [:bs, :] is C-contiguous for any bs.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")
        # Reused scratch (allocated once): contiguous `out=` target for the
        # per-column draws, plus a bool scratch for the indicator threshold.
        scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)
        scratch2 = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)
        bool_scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.bool_)

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            # Fill the whole batch with independent standard normals; the
            # normal (systemic / idiosyncratic) columns keep these draws.
            rng.standard_normal(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            # Overwrite uniform columns with U(0, 1).
            for c in range(n_uniform):
                s = scratch[:bs]
                rng.random(out=cast(np.ndarray, s))
                buffer[:bs, uniform_cols[c]] = s

            # Overwrite chi columns with 1 / sqrt(W), W ~ chi^2(nu).
            for c in range(n_chi):
                s = scratch[:bs]
                rng.standard_gamma(chi_shape_halved[c], out=cast(np.ndarray, s))
                s *= 2.0  # W ~ chi^2(nu)
                np.reciprocal(np.sqrt(s), out=s)  # store 1 / sqrt(W)
                buffer[:bs, chi_cols[c]] = s

            # Overwrite indicator columns with {0.0, 1.0}, P(=1) = p.
            for c in range(n_ind):
                s = scratch[:bs]
                rng.random(out=cast(np.ndarray, s))
                b = bool_scratch[:bs]
                np.less(s, ind_p[c], out=b)  # u < p  -> stressed
                buffer[:bs, ind_cols[c]] = b  # bool -> float64 on assignment

            # Overwrite beta columns with P ~ Beta(alpha, beta). Drawn via the
            # Gamma ratio Beta = G1 / (G1 + G2), G1 ~ Gamma(alpha), G2 ~
            # Gamma(beta): standard_gamma supports out=, so the whole fill stays
            # zero-allocation (rng.beta has no out= and would allocate). This is
            # the per-scenario stochastic common PD the beta-binomial kernel
            # thresholds each obligor's independent uniform against.
            for c in range(n_beta):
                g1 = scratch[:bs]
                g2 = scratch2[:bs]
                rng.standard_gamma(beta_alpha[c], out=cast(np.ndarray, g1))
                rng.standard_gamma(beta_beta[c], out=cast(np.ndarray, g2))
                np.add(g1, g2, out=g2)  # denom = G1 + G2
                np.divide(g1, g2, out=g1)  # G1 / (G1 + G2) ~ Beta(alpha, beta)
                buffer[:bs, beta_cols[c]] = g1

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\parametric_beta.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_beta.py
"""
Independent Beta(alpha, beta) latent-factor generator.

Emits independent Beta(alpha, beta) draws for the specified factor rows of the
credit-factor universe.

The primary use case is the Beta-Binomial JTD model, where a scenario-level
Beta random variable represents the default environment:

    P_s ~ Beta(alpha, beta)

and obligor defaults are then generated conditionally:

    D_i,s | P_s ~ Bernoulli(P_s)

This generator produces the scenario-level Beta draws only. Bernoulli default
realization remains the responsibility of the JTD engine.

CONTRACT
--------
Yielded latent-factor matrices are views into a reused C-contiguous buffer.
Consumers must treat them as ephemeral (read-only) or copy them.

LAYOUT
------
The buffer is C-order:

    (batch_size, n_factors)

The partial-batch slice:

    buffer[:bs, :]

remains C-contiguous and is therefore suitable for direct NumPy random-number
generation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "BetaGenerator",
]

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
        CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class BetaGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """
    Independent Beta(alpha, beta) factor generator.

    Each factor row i generates

        X_i,s ~ Beta(alpha_i, beta_i)

    independently across factors and scenarios.
    """

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    total_scenarios: int
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    @classmethod
    def from_universe(
        cls,
        universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
        *,
        total_scenarios: int,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> BetaGenerator:
        """
        Construct a Beta generator over the universe's BETA_PD factors only.

        NOTE: this is a standalone, single-marginal generator and yields a
        beta-ONLY sub-matrix (one row per BETA_PD factor, indexed 0..n_beta-1) —
        its rows do NOT align to full-universe UIX. For the JTD pipeline, where a
        ``BetaBinomialJtdBlock.beta_uix`` indexes the full ``(F, S)`` matrix, use
        ``MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe`` instead, which fills the
        beta rows in place at their universe UIX. This generator is kept as the
        single-marginal primitive (mirroring the Gaussian/Uniform generators).

        ``available_factor_ids``, ``alpha`` and ``beta`` are all the BETA_PD
        subset, aligned by construction.
        """
        return cls(
            available_factor_ids=universe.factor_ids[universe.beta_pd_uix],
            alpha=universe.beta_alpha,
            beta=universe.beta_beta,
            total_scenarios=total_scenarios,
            seed=seed,
        )

    def iter_batches(
        self,
        *,
        batch_size: int,
    ) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:

        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])

        buffer = np.empty(
            (batch_size, n_factors),
            dtype=np.float64,
            order="C",
        )
        # Reused scratch for the Gamma-ratio Beta draw (zero-allocation).
        g1_scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)
        g2_scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)

            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            # Beta(alpha, beta) = G1 / (G1 + G2) with G1 ~ Gamma(alpha),
            # G2 ~ Gamma(beta). standard_gamma supports out=, so no per-column
            # allocation (rng.beta has no out=).
            for j in range(n_factors):
                g1 = g1_scratch[:bs]
                g2 = g2_scratch[:bs]
                rng.standard_gamma(self.alpha[j], out=g1)
                rng.standard_gamma(self.beta[j], out=g2)
                np.add(g1, g2, out=g2)
                np.divide(g1, g2, out=g1)
                view[:, j] = g1

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )

            scenario_offset += bs
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_copula_gaussian.py
"""Gaussian-copula latent-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

Emits independent standard-normal draws for the full credit-factor universe.
All dependence — single-factor `beta` mixing or multi-factor CSR loadings —
is applied downstream in the JTD kernels, never here.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused C-contiguous
buffer; consumers treat them as ephemeral (read-only) or copy.

LAYOUT: the buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)``. The partial-
batch slice ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is therefore C-contiguous for any
``bs <= batch_size`` — required by ``rng.standard_normal(out=...)``,
which rejects non-contiguous output arrays. (An F-order layout would
break here on the final partial batch.)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "GaussianCopulaGenerator",
]


from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
        CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class GaussianCopulaGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """Zero-allocation independent standard-normal latent-factor generator."""

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    @classmethod
    def from_universe(
        cls,
        universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
        *,
        total_scenarios: int,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> GaussianCopulaGenerator:
        """Build from the credit factor-universe master (sizes the matrix to
        the universe's factor axis). Fills EVERY column with standard normal, so it is
        correct only for portfolios that read systemic / idiosyncratic columns. Use
        MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe for a matrix that also honours chi / uniform / indicator columns.
        """
        return cls(
            available_factor_ids=universe.factor_ids,
            total_scenarios=total_scenarios,
            seed=seed,
        )

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # C-order: buffer[:bs, :] is C-contiguous for every bs <= batch_size,
        # the precondition `rng.standard_normal(out=...)` requires.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            rng.standard_normal(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\parametric_copula_t.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_copula_t.py
"""Student-t-copula latent-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

Emits the RAW latent universe: standard-normal columns for systemic / idio
factors and chi-square columns for the mixing variables W. The Student-t
transform ``sqrt(nu_i / W)`` is applied ONCE, per obligor, inside
``kernel_sf_t_copula_jtd`` — never pre-applied here.

`nu_by_chi` is a per-chi-factor SoA array, not a hidden scalar default.
Chi-square draws use ``standard_gamma`` (which supports ``out=``);
``chisquare`` does not. There is no Cholesky here — systemic correlation
belongs to multi-factor loadings, and single-factor idiosyncratic draws
are independent by definition.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused buffer.

LAYOUT
------
- Main buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)`` so the partial-batch
  slice ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is C-contiguous — required by
  ``rng.standard_normal(out=...)``.
- A separate 1D ``chi_scratch`` buffer of length ``batch_size`` is reused
  for each chi-square fill. The scratch is contiguous; the strided write
  ``buffer[:bs, chi_cols[c]] = chi_scratch[:bs]`` happens after the gamma
  draw, NOT during it. Filling chi columns directly in the main C-order
  buffer would require a non-contiguous ``out=``, which numpy rejects.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "TCopulaGenerator",
]

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
        CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class TCopulaGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """
    Zero-allocation Student-t-copula latent-factor generator.

    `chi_factor_columns` lists the factor-axis positions that carry a
    chi-square mixing variable $W$; `nu_by_chi` is the degrees-of-freedom
    array aligned to it. Every other column is a standard-normal latent
    factor.

    **CRITICAL KERNEL OPTIMIZATION:**
    The columns corresponding to `chi_factor_columns` DO NOT emit raw $\\chi^2$ draws.
    Instead, they emit the precomputed inverse square root: $1/\\sqrt{W}$.
    This allows the downstream JTD kernel to evaluate the Student-t variable as
    $\\sqrt{\\nu} \\cdot (1/\\sqrt{W}) \\cdot Z$, entirely eliminating the `np.sqrt`
    and division operations from the engine's innermost scenario loop.
    """

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    chi_factor_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (n_chi,) positions in the F axis
    nu_by_chi: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (n_chi,) dof, aligned
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    @classmethod
    def from_universe(
        cls,
        universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
        *,
        total_scenarios: int,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> TCopulaGenerator:
        """Build from the credit factor-universe master.

        `chi_factor_columns` and `nu_by_chi` come straight from the universe
        (`chi_uix` / `chi_nu_vector`, aligned by construction), so the dof a chi
        column is drawn with cannot drift from the dof JTD ingest reconciles
        obligor `nu` against — both read the one master. Sizes the matrix to the
        universe's factor axis; non-chi columns are standard normals, correct
        for t-copula portfolios (which read systemic / idiosyncratic / chi).
        """
        return cls(
            available_factor_ids=universe.factor_ids,
            total_scenarios=total_scenarios,
            chi_factor_columns=universe.chi_uix,
            nu_by_chi=universe.chi_nu_vector,
            seed=seed,
        )

    def chi_nu_by_uix(self) -> dict[int, float]:
        """{chi factor UIX -> dof} — the reconciliation map JTD ingest checks
        obligor ``nu`` against. This generator is the source of truth for the
        dof each chi column is drawn with; pass the result as
        ``compile_jtd_portfolio_from_df(..., chi_nu_by_uix=...)``."""
        return dict(
            zip(
                self.chi_factor_columns.tolist(),
                self.nu_by_chi.tolist(),
                strict=True,
            )
        )

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")
        if self.chi_factor_columns.shape != self.nu_by_chi.shape:
            raise ValueError("chi_factor_columns and nu_by_chi must be aligned")
        if self.nu_by_chi.size and not np.all(self.nu_by_chi > 0.0):
            raise ValueError("nu_by_chi must be strictly positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        chi_cols = self.chi_factor_columns
        chi_shape_halved = self.nu_by_chi * 0.5  # chi^2(nu) = 2 * Gamma(nu/2)
        n_chi = int(chi_cols.shape[0])

        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # Main buffer: C-order so [:bs, :] is C-contiguous for any bs.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")
        # 1D scratch: contiguous, used as `out=` for the chi gamma draw.
        chi_scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            # Fill the whole batch with standard normals...
            rng.standard_normal(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            # ...then overwrite the chi columns with transformed chi-square draws.
            # Draw into a 1D contiguous scratch, then write across (the
            # destination is a strided 1D view, but the write itself is
            # not the `out=` target).

            for c in range(n_chi):
                chi_view = chi_scratch[:bs]
                rng.standard_gamma(chi_shape_halved[c], out=cast(np.ndarray, chi_view))
                chi_view *= 2.0  # W ~ chi^2(nu)

                # =====================================================================
                # MATHEMATICAL INVARIANT: PRECOMPUTED INVERSE SQUARE ROOT
                # We apply the 1/sqrt(W) transform in-place before writing to the
                # C-contiguous buffer. This ensures the downstream JTD kernel receives
                # the optimized factor and avoids calculating square roots in the hot path.
                # =====================================================================
                np.reciprocal(np.sqrt(chi_view), out=chi_view)

                buffer[:bs, chi_cols[c]] = chi_view

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_uniform.py
"""Independent Uniform(0, 1) latent-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

Emits independent U(0, 1) draws for the full credit-factor universe — the
factor source the Bernoulli-family JTD kernels consume (`u_row[s] < pd_i`).
This is the counterpart to `GaussianCopulaGenerator`: same streaming, zero-
allocation contract, different marginal.

There is no dependence here, by design and by definition. The independent-
Bernoulli model is independent; coupling a uniform row to a systemic factor
would make it a copula (use `COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN` instead). When a portfolio
spans both Bernoulli and copula blocks, use `MixedCreditFactorGenerator`,
which fills normal, uniform, chi and indicator rows in one matrix.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused C-contiguous
buffer; consumers treat them as ephemeral (read-only) or copy.

LAYOUT: the buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)``. The partial-batch
slice ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is therefore C-contiguous for any ``bs <=
batch_size`` — required by ``rng.random(out=...)``, which rejects non-
contiguous output arrays.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "IndependentUniformGenerator",
]

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
        CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class IndependentUniformGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """Zero-allocation independent Uniform(0, 1) latent-factor generator."""

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    @classmethod
    def from_universe(
        cls,
        universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
        *,
        total_scenarios: int,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> IndependentUniformGenerator:
        """Build from the credit factor-universe master (sizes the matrix to
        the universe's factor axis). Fills EVERY column with U(0, 1), so it is
        correct only for portfolios that read independent-Bernoulli (uniform) columns. Use
        MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe for a matrix that mixes uniform with normal / chi / indicator columns.
        """
        return cls(
            available_factor_ids=universe.factor_ids,
            total_scenarios=total_scenarios,
            seed=seed,
        )

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # C-order: buffer[:bs, :] is C-contiguous for every bs <= batch_size,
        # the precondition `rng.random(out=...)` requires.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            rng.random(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/state.py
"""
Latent-factor container for copula credit models.

`CreditFactorMatrix` is the credit-layer analogue of `MarketDataMatrix`: a
single C-contiguous `(num_credit_factors, num_scenarios)` array holding every
simulated latent driver — systemic Z, idiosyncratic eps, chi-squared mixing W —
in one integer-indexed universe. Copula blocks index it by `*_uix`.

It is deliberately separate from `MarketDataMatrix`: latent copula draws and
market prices are different sample spaces. Separation also lets the credit
Monte Carlo run an independent scenario count `S_credit != S_market`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CreditFactorMatrix",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CreditFactorMatrix:
    """
    Vectorized latent-factor container for copula JTD models.

    Dimension 0 is the credit-factor UIX space; dimension -1 is the scenario
    index. Z, eps and W share one UIX space, so a block's systemic/idio/chi
    indices all address the same array.
    """

    # (num_credit_factors, num_scenarios) — all latent drivers, scenario-major.
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    @property
    def num_credit_factors(self) -> int:
        return self.latent_factors.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_scenarios(self) -> int:
        return self.latent_factors.shape[1]
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/base.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID: int = -999


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _JtdBlock:
    """Model-agnostic base for compiled obligor-keyed JTD blocks."""

    # ===== Class contract =====

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = _UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID
    _ABSTRACT: ClassVar[bool] = True  # opt-in per class (not inherited)

    # ===== Core data (assumed valid) =====

    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # ===== Class construction guard =====

    def __init_subclass__(cls, **kwargs: object) -> None:
        # Explicit super() bypasses the @dataclass(slots=True) closure cell bug
        super(_JtdBlock, cls).__init_subclass__(**kwargs)

        # Only consider attributes defined *on this class*
        is_abstract = cls.__dict__.get("_ABSTRACT", False) or cls.__name__.startswith(
            "_"
        )

        if is_abstract:
            return

        if cls.JTD_MODEL_ID == _UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID:
            raise TypeError(f"JTD block '{cls.__name__}' must set JTD_MODEL_ID.")

    # ===== Lightweight API =====

    @property
    def num_obligors(self) -> int:
        return self.obligor_uids.shape[0]

    def print_block(self, max_items: int | None = 10) -> None:
        print(f"{type(self).__name__}:")
        for f in fields(self):
            v = getattr(self, f.name)
            if isinstance(v, np.ndarray):
                print(
                    f"  {f.name:<36} | shape: {v.shape!s:>10} | "
                    f"dtype: {v.dtype!s:>8} | values: {v[:max_items]}"
                )
            else:
                print(f"  {f.name:<36} {v}")
        print()
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/beta_binomial.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import BETA_BINOMIAL_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class BetaBinomialJtdBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """
    Beta-binomial default model.

    In this model, the default probability is stochastic and common
    across obligors.

    For scenario s:

        P_s ~ Beta(alpha, beta)

    For obligor i:

        D_i,s ~ Bernoulli(P_s)

    Loss:

        L_i,s = EAD_i,s * LGD_i * D_i,s
    """

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = BETA_BINOMIAL_ID

    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,)

    beta_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,)
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\blocks\independent_bernoulli.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/independent_bernoulli.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """
    Independent-default credit model.

    U_i,s ~ Uniform(0,1)

    default_i,s = 1(U_i,s < PD_i)

    loss_i,s = ead_i,s * lgd_i * default_i,s
    """

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI_ID

    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/mf_copula.py

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _MultiFactorCopulaBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """
    Multi-factor copula family base: K systemic loadings per obligor, CSR-packed.

    Each obligor i loads on a variable-length set of (independent, standard
    normal) systemic factors. The loadings are stored in compressed sparse row
    (CSR) form so obligors may reference different factor subsets with zero
    padding:

        loading_indptr[i] : loading_indptr[i + 1]

    slices the flat `loading_values` / `systemic_uix` arrays for obligor i.

    The latent variable is

        A_i = sum_j loading_values[j] * Z[systemic_uix[j]]  +  idio_weight[i] * eps_i

    over j in the obligor's CSR slice, with `idio_weight[i] = sqrt(1 - sum beta^2)`
    precomputed at ingest (the systemic factors are independent, so the systemic
    variance is the sum of squared loadings). Default iff A_i < thresholds[i].
    """

    _ABSTRACT: ClassVar[bool] = True

    # Threshold per obligor: A_i < t_i implies default. float64
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,)

    # CSR loading structure (ragged systemic loadings per obligor).
    loading_indptr: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N + 1,)
    loading_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (nnz,)
    systemic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (nnz,) index into CreditFactorMatrix

    idiosyncratic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,)
    idio_weight: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,) sqrt(1 - sum beta^2)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/mf_coplua_students_t.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula import _MultiFactorCopulaBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import COPULA_MF_T_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock(_MultiFactorCopulaBlock):
    """
    A_i = sqrt(nu_i / W) * (sum_k beta_ik * Z_k + sqrt(1 - sum beta^2) * eps_i) ;
    default if A_i < t_i.

    The multi-factor Gaussian latent (CSR loadings + idiosyncratic, from the
    shared `_MultiFactorCopulaBlock` base) is t-scaled by the shared chi-square
    mixing variable W — read from the latent matrix via `chi_squared_uix` — so a
    heavy market draw drags every obligor's latent down together (tail
    dependence). `thresholds` are Student-t quantiles of PD (with nu); the kernel,
    not the generator, owns the t-transform.
    """

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = COPULA_MF_T_ID

    chi_squared_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,) index into mixing variable W
    sqrt_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,) precomputed sqrt(degrees of freedom)
```
</file>

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula import _SingleFactorCopulaBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import COPULA_SF_T_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)

    A_i = sqrt(nu_i / W) * (beta_i * Z + sqrt(1 - beta_i^2) * eps_i).

    `nu` is a real per-obligor SoA field, not a hidden default argument. The
    chi-square mixing variable W is read from the latent matrix via
    `chi_squared_uix`; the kernel — not the generator — owns the t-transform.
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/stress_regime_bernoulli.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import (
    STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI_ID,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """
    Stress regime bernoulli credit model.

    Stress Regime Bernoulli
    -----------------------
    Stress_s ∈ {0,1}

    PD_i,s =
        PD_i                    if Stress_s = 0
        PD_i * shock_mult_i     if Stress_s = 1

    default_i,s = 1(U_i,s < PD_i,s)

    loss_i,s = ead_i,s * lgd_i * default_i,s

    """

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI_ID

    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,)

    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,)

    # regime_indicator_uix: (scalar) index into the latent factor matrix to
    # determine which factor is the stress regime indicator. The stress regime
    # indicator is a binary variable that indicates whether a given scenario is
    # in the stressed regime (1) or not (0).
    stress_regime_indicator_uix: np.int32  # (scalar)

    # stress_pd_multiplier: (N,) multiplier to apply to the PD in stressed scenarios,
    # e.g. 2.0 means PD doubles in stressed scenarios. Each obligor can have a different
    # stress PD multiplier, but the same multiplier is applied across all scenarios for
    # a given obligor.
    stress_pd_multiplier: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,)
```
</file>

```python
    StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,

    BetaBinomialJtdBlock
    | ForcedDefaultJtdBlock
    | IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock
    | MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
    | MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock
    | SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
    | SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock

    SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock | SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\bridge.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/bridge.py
"""
Exposure -> Credit bridge.

Aggregates per-netting-set EAD (the output of `run_exposure`) up to per-obligor
EAD, then reindexes the result onto a target obligor-uid order. Pure SoA joins
plus one CSR segment-sum kernel — no row loops in Python, no jagged arrays.

The two joins differ in semantics:

  1. netting-set uid -> obligor uid is a FAIL-FAST lookup: an EadResult row
     that points at an unknown netting set is a bug in the upstream pipeline.

  2. obligor_per_ns -> target obligor index is a SOFT lookup: a netting set
     whose obligor has no row in `obligor_uids_target` is dropped (e.g. when
     the JTD portfolio only models a subset of the obligor universe).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import (
    searchsorted_or_raise,
    searchsorted_soft_join,
)
from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    _I64_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)
from riskquant.constants import INVALID_UIX
from riskquant.credit.exposure.results import NettingSetExposurePaths
from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,
        _F64_2D,
        _I32_1D,
        _I64_1D,
    )
)
def _kernel_aggregate_ead(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_target, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_ns, S)
    ead_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (n_ns,)   ead row -> target band order
    target_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (n_target + 1,)
) -> None:
    n_target, s_scenarios = out.shape
    for g in nb.prange(n_target):
        start = target_offsets[g]
        end = target_offsets[g + 1]
        out_row = out[g]
        if end == start:
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = 0.0
            continue
        src0 = ead[ead_perm[start]]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = src0[s]
        for i in range(start + 1, end):
            src = ead[ead_perm[i]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] += src[s]


def aggregate_ead_to_obligor(
    *,
    ead_result: NettingSetExposurePaths,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
    obligor_uids_target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Aggregate per-netting-set EAD up to per-obligor EAD in target-row order.

    Parameters
    ----------
    ead_result
        Output of `run_exposure`; rows aligned to its `uid` (netting-set
        uid) array.
    netting_set_master
        Provides the netting-set uid -> obligor uid linkage. `uid`
        ascending sorted.
    obligor_uids_target
        Target obligor-uid order (typically `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`).
        Rows in the output align to this; obligors absent here are
        dropped from the aggregation.
    """
    # Strict: ns uid -> obligor uid.
    ns_pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        raw=ead_result.uid,
        universe=netting_set_master.uid,
        ctx="aggregate_ead_to_obligor: netting-set uid(s)",
    )
    obligor_per_ns = netting_set_master.obligor_uid[ns_pos]

    # Soft: target obligor index per ns; absences -> INVALID_UIX (-999).
    # We must explicitly probe membership here, because it is valid and
    # expected for some netting sets to belong to obligors not modeled
    # in the target JTD portfolio.
    target_idx = searchsorted_soft_join(
        raw=obligor_per_ns,
        universe=obligor_uids_target,
        sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
    )

    # Drop ns rows whose obligor isn't in the target universe.
    keep = target_idx >= 0
    target_idx = target_idx[keep]
    kept_ead_rows = np.flatnonzero(keep).astype(np.int32, copy=False)

    # CSR build: sort kept ns rows by target obligor, emit offsets.
    order = np.argsort(target_idx, kind="stable")
    ead_perm = np.ascontiguousarray(kept_ead_rows[order], dtype=np.int32)
    target_offsets = np.zeros(obligor_uids_target.shape[0] + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    counts = np.bincount(target_idx, minlength=obligor_uids_target.shape[0])
    np.cumsum(counts.astype(np.int64, copy=False), out=target_offsets[1:])

    s = ead_result.exposure.shape[1]
    out = np.empty((obligor_uids_target.shape[0], s), dtype=np.float64, order="C")
    _kernel_aggregate_ead(
        out=out,
        ead=ead_result.exposure,
        ead_perm=ead_perm,
        target_offsets=target_offsets,
    )
    return out
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\compiled.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/compiled.py
"""
Compiled JTD portfolio container.

`CompiledJtdPortfolio` is the credit-layer analogue of `CompiledExposure`: an
ordered set of per-model obligor blocks. Each populated block contributes one
contiguous row-band to the eventual (n_obligors x n_scenarios) JTD-loss matrix;
`block_offsets[i]` is its first row. `obligor_uids` aggregates every block's
uids into one flat int64 array in band order — the row order the engine's
`ead_by_obligor` argument must match.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledJtdPortfolio",
]

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.beta_binomial import (
    BetaBinomialJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.forced_default import ForcedDefaultJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.independent_bernoulli import (
    IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_gaussian import MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_students_t import (
    MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_gaussian import SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_students_t import (
    SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.stress_regime_bernoulli import (
    StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.type_alias import JtdBlockTypeUnion


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledJtdPortfolio:
    """JTD-ready credit portfolio: an ordered set of compiled obligor blocks."""

    # --- COMPILED BLOCKS ---

    # Non-copula blocks
    forced_default_jtd_block: ForcedDefaultJtdBlock | None = None
    independent_bernoulli_jtd_block: IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock | None = None
    beta_binomial_jtd_block: BetaBinomialJtdBlock | None = None
    stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd_block: StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock | None = None

    # SF Copula blocks
    sf_gaussian_copula_jtd_block: SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    sf_students_t_copula_jtd_block: SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock | None = None

    # MF Copula blocks
    mf_gaussian_copula_jtd_block: MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    mf_students_t_copula_jtd_block: MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock | None = None

    # Internal fields derived from the above and used by the engine.
    _blocks: tuple[JtdBlockTypeUnion, ...] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _block_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_blocks: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_obligors: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # Band order + slot names are owned by the model registry; getattr by
        # compiled_slot fails fast here if a spec names a slot this dataclass
        # does not declare.
        from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import JTD_MODEL_SPECS

        ordered: tuple[JtdBlockTypeUnion | None, ...] = tuple(
            getattr(self, spec.compiled_slot) for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS
        )
        blocks: tuple[JtdBlockTypeUnion, ...] = tuple(
            b for b in ordered if b is not None
        )
        total_blocks = len(blocks)

        # 1. Calculate the total size needed
        total_obligors = sum(b.num_obligors for b in blocks)

        # 2. Allocate uninitialized arrays (faster than zeros)
        block_offsets = np.empty(total_blocks, dtype=np.int64)
        obligor_uids = np.empty(total_obligors, dtype=np.int64)

        # 3. Populate both arrays in a single, clean pass
        offset = 0
        for i, b in enumerate(blocks):
            block_offsets[i] = offset
            n = b.num_obligors
            obligor_uids[offset : offset + n] = b.obligor_uids
            offset += n

        object.__setattr__(self, "_blocks", blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_block_offsets", block_offsets)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_blocks", total_blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_obligors", total_obligors)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_obligor_uids", obligor_uids)

    @property
    def blocks(self) -> tuple[JtdBlockTypeUnion, ...]:
        return self._blocks

    @property
    def block_offsets(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._block_offsets

    @property
    def total_blocks(self) -> int:
        return self._total_blocks

    @property
    def total_obligors(self) -> int:
        return self._total_obligors

    @property
    def obligor_uids(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._obligor_uids

    def iter_blocks(self) -> Iterator[JtdBlockTypeUnion]:
        yield from self._blocks

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of block layout and obligor counts."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{'COMPILED PORTFOLIO SUMMARY':^90}")
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"Total Obligors: {self._total_obligors:,}")
        print(f"Total Blocks: {self._total_blocks}")
        print("-" * 90)
        print(f"{'Block Type':<32} | {'Obligors':<10} | {'Offset'}")
        print("-" * 90)

        if self._total_blocks == 0:
            print(f"{'Empty Portfolio':^90}")
        else:
            for i, block in enumerate(self._blocks):
                print(
                    f"{type(block).__name__:<32} | "
                    f"{block.num_obligors:<10,} | {self._block_offsets[i]}"
                )
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")

    def print_block_details(self, max_items: int | None = 10) -> None:
        """Cascade into each block and print its raw SoA arrays."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{f'BLOCK DETAILS (MAX {max_items} ITEMS PER BLOCK)':^90}")
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
        for block in self._blocks:
            title = f"Block: {type(block).__name__}"
            print("-" * len(title))
            print(title)
            print("-" * len(title))
            block.print_block(max_items=max_items)
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/engine.py
"""
JTD engine: model-id -> evaluator dispatch and the loss-resolution loop.

The dispatch table is now owned by the registry: `JTD_EVALUATORS` is just
`registry.evaluator_table()`. Adding a model no longer touches this file — the
model's evaluator is wired the moment its `JtdModelSpec` is registered, and the
registry validates completeness/contiguity at import.

`run_jtd` walks the compiled obligor blocks in band order, dispatches each on its
`JTD_MODEL_ID`, and writes into contiguous bands of one pre-allocated
(n_obligors x n_scenarios) output.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "run_jtd",
    "run_jtd_for_obligor_ead",
]

from typing import Any, Final

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.align import (
    align_obligor_ead_to_portfolio,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.compiled import CompiledObligorEad
from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.base import JtdEvaluator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import evaluator_table
from riskquant.credit.jtd.results import JtdLossResult

JTD_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[JtdEvaluator[Any], ...]] = evaluator_table()


def run_jtd(
    *,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    ead_by_obligor: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
) -> JtdLossResult:
    """
    Resolve jump-to-default loss across all JTD models.

    Parameters
    ----------
    jtd_portfolio
        Compiled obligor blocks; `obligor_uids` defines the output row order.
    ead_by_obligor
        (n_obligors, S) EAD, rows aligned to `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids` —
        the output of `aggregate_ead_to_obligor`.
    credit_factors
        Latent-factor matrix; `num_scenarios` must match `ead_by_obligor`.
        Ignored by the forced-default model, which is scenario-independent.
    """
    n_obligors = jtd_portfolio.total_obligors
    n_scenarios = credit_factors.num_scenarios

    if ead_by_obligor.shape != (n_obligors, n_scenarios):
        raise ValueError(
            f"ead_by_obligor shape {ead_by_obligor.shape} != expected "
            f"{(n_obligors, n_scenarios)}."
        )

    loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (n_obligors, n_scenarios), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    blocks = jtd_portfolio.blocks
    offsets = jtd_portfolio.block_offsets
    evaluators_table = JTD_EVALUATORS

    for i in range(jtd_portfolio.total_blocks):
        block = blocks[i]
        start = int(offsets[i])
        end = start + block.num_obligors

        evaluator = evaluators_table[block.JTD_MODEL_ID]
        evaluator(
            block=block,
            ead=ead_by_obligor[start:end, :],
            credit_factors=credit_factors,
            out=loss[start:end, :],
        )

    return JtdLossResult(uid=jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids, loss=loss)


def run_jtd_for_obligor_ead(
    *,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    obligor_ead: CompiledObligorEad,
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
) -> JtdLossResult:
    """
    uid-safe entry point: resolve JTD loss from EAD keyed by obligor uid.

    The safe outer tier over `run_jtd`. `run_jtd` takes a bare ``(N, S)`` array
    that must already be in the portfolio's band order — correct for the in-
    pipeline caller (the exposure stage feeds it aligned), but a silent-miscalc
    footgun for an external caller who has EAD keyed by uid and no reason to know
    band order. This entry closes that gap: it reindexes `obligor_ead` onto
    `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids` by uid with a fail-fast join (every modelled
    obligor must have an EAD row), then runs the core. Scalar ``(N,)`` EAD is
    broadcast across the scenario axis; scenario-varying ``(N, S)`` EAD must match
    `credit_factors.num_scenarios`.
    """
    aligned = align_obligor_ead_to_portfolio(
        obligor_ead=obligor_ead,
        obligor_uids_target=jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids,
    )
    n_scenarios = credit_factors.num_scenarios

    if aligned.ndim == 1:
        # Scalar per-obligor EAD -> materialise (N, S) for the bare-array core.
        ead_by_obligor = np.ascontiguousarray(
            np.broadcast_to(aligned[:, None], (aligned.shape[0], n_scenarios))
        )
    else:
        if aligned.shape[1] != n_scenarios:
            raise ValueError(
                f"run_jtd_for_obligor_ead: scenario-varying EAD has "
                f"{aligned.shape[1]} scenarios, but credit_factors has "
                f"{n_scenarios}."
            )
        ead_by_obligor = aligned

    return run_jtd(
        jtd_portfolio=jtd_portfolio,
        ead_by_obligor=ead_by_obligor,
        credit_factors=credit_factors,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/evaluators/base.py
"""
JTD evaluator dispatch contract.

`JtdEvaluator` is deliberately NOT the valuation `Evaluator` protocol. A pricer
maps (block, market data) -> PV; a JTD evaluator maps (block, EAD, latent
factors) -> loss. Every evaluator — copula or deterministic — honors the same
`(n_obligors, S)` `out` contract so the engine table stays homogeneous.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Protocol, TypeVar, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
    from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock

B_contra = TypeVar("B_contra", bound="_JtdBlock", contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class JtdEvaluator(Protocol[B_contra]):
    """Structural contract for all JTD-model evaluators."""

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        block: B_contra,
        ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
        credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/evaluators/stress_regime_bernoulli.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.stress_regime_bernoulli import (
    StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.kernels.stress_regime_bernoulli import (
    kernel_stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd,
)


def evaluate_stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd(
    *,
    block: StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:

    # Extract the stress regime indicator (1 for stressed, 0 for not stressed) for
    # each scenario from the credit factors using the provided index from the block.
    stress_regime_indicator = credit_factors.latent_factors[
        block.stress_regime_indicator_uix
    ]

    kernel_stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd(
        out=out,
        latent_factors=credit_factors.latent_factors,
        stress_regime_indicator=stress_regime_indicator,
        ead=ead,
        lgd=block.lgd,
        pd=block.pd,
        uniform_uix=block.uniform_uix,
        stress_pd_multiplier=block.stress_pd_multiplier,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\ingest\_shared.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/_shared.py
"""
Shared low-level ingest primitives: factor-name -> UIX resolution, typed column
extraction, Gaussian / Student-t default thresholds, and the obligor-master
PD/LGD join. Used by the per-model builders (and, for resolution, indirectly via
the builders feeding the semantic validators). No model-specific logic here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd
from scipy.special import ndtri
from scipy.stats import t as scipy_student_t

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_raise
from riskquant.constants import PD_DUST_TOLERANCE
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit

_PREVIEW = 10


def _resolve_factor_uix(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.object_],
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:

    out = np.empty(names.shape, dtype=np.int32)
    mapping_get = factor_name_to_uix.get

    missing = set()
    sentinel = -999  # must not collide with valid UIX

    for i, name in enumerate(names):
        val = mapping_get(name, sentinel)
        if val == sentinel:
            missing.add(name)
        else:
            out[i] = val

    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown credit factor(s) for '{ctx}': {sorted(missing)}")

    return out


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    arr = np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))
    # `to_numpy` can hand back a read-only view of the column block, which
    # `ascontiguousarray` passes through unchanged. Numba kernels are typed
    # `float64[::1]` (writable) and reject `readonly` arrays, so guarantee a
    # writable buffer; copy only when the view is actually read-only.
    if not arr.flags.writeable:
        arr = arr.copy()
    return arr


def _gaussian_thresholds(pd_arr: npt.NDArray[np.float64]) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """t_i = Phi^{-1}(PD_i) with dust-clipping to keep ndtri finite."""
    out = np.empty_like(pd_arr)
    np.clip(pd_arr, PD_DUST_TOLERANCE, 1.0 - PD_DUST_TOLERANCE, out=out)
    return ndtri(out, out=out)


def _students_t_thresholds(
    pd_arr: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    t_i = F_t^{-1}(PD_i; nu_i)

    Ensures:
        P(T_i < t_i) = PD_i

    for Student-t latent variables.
    """
    clipped = np.clip(
        pd_arr,
        PD_DUST_TOLERANCE,
        1.0 - PD_DUST_TOLERANCE,
    )

    return np.asarray(
        scipy_student_t.ppf(clipped, df=nu),
        dtype=np.float64,
    )


def _join_pd_lgd_from_master(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray[np.float64], npt.NDArray[np.float64]]:
    """
    Look up PD and LGD on the obligor master in one ``searchsorted``.

    Delegates the actual lookup to the centralized
    ``_arrays.searchsorted_or_raise`` — every credit-package join goes
    through the same primitive, never inline searchsorted/clip/match.
    """
    pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        raw=obligor_uids,
        universe=obligor_master.uid,
        ctx="compile_jtd_portfolio: JTD obligor uid(s)",
    )
    pd_arr = np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_master.pd[pos], dtype=np.float64)
    lgd_arr = np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_master.lgd[pos], dtype=np.float64)
    return pd_arr, lgd_arr
```
</file>

```python
"""Per-model JTD block builder. Input is assumed structurally and semantically
valid (validated upstream in `validate.py`); builders only resolve and construct."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.beta_binomial import BetaBinomialJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest._shared import (
    _i64,
    _join_pd_lgd_from_master,
    _resolve_factor_uix,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit


def build_beta_binomial_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> BetaBinomialJtdBlock:
    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    # PD is stochastic (P_s ~ Beta drawn into the beta row); only LGD is joined.
    _, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)

        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd_arr,
        uniform_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["uniform_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="uniform_factor",
        ),
        beta_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["beta_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="beta_factor",
        ),
    )
```

```python
def build_forced_default_block(
```
