<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\ingest\builders\mf_gaussian.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/builders/mf_gaussian.py
"""Per-model JTD block builder. Input is assumed structurally and semantically
valid (validated upstream in `validate.py`): the loadings frame is present and
routable, every obligor is covered, no factor is duplicated, and sum-beta^2 <= 1.
This builder only packs CSR (in band order) and precomputes the idio weight."""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_gaussian import MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest._shared import (
    _f64,
    _gaussian_thresholds,
    _i64,
    _join_pd_lgd_from_master,
    _resolve_factor_uix,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit


def build_mf_gaussian_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
    mf_loadings_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock:
    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    n = obligor_uids.shape[0]
    pd_arr, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)

    idiosyncratic_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        df["idiosyncratic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
        factor_name_to_uix,
        ctx="idiosyncratic_factor",
    )

    # Band-order position of each obligor; filter the shared loadings frame to
    # this block's obligors (other obligors belong to other MF blocks).
    uid_to_pos = {int(uid): pos for pos, uid in enumerate(obligor_uids)}
    load_uids = mf_loadings_df["obligor_uid"].to_numpy()
    keep = np.array([int(u) in uid_to_pos for u in load_uids], dtype=bool)

    pos = np.array([uid_to_pos[int(u)] for u in load_uids[keep]], dtype=np.int64)
    loadings = _f64(mf_loadings_df.loc[keep], "factor_loading")
    sys_names = mf_loadings_df.loc[keep, "factor_name"].to_numpy(copy=False)

    # Stable sort by obligor position -> CSR-contiguous per obligor, band order.
    order = np.argsort(pos, kind="stable")
    pos_sorted = pos[order]
    loading_values = np.ascontiguousarray(loadings[order])
    systemic_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        sys_names[order], factor_name_to_uix, ctx="mf systemic factor_name"
    )

    counts = np.bincount(pos_sorted, minlength=n)
    loading_indptr = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    loading_indptr[0] = 0
    np.cumsum(counts, out=loading_indptr[1:])

    # Independent systemic factors -> systemic variance = sum of squared
    # loadings; idio weight is the residual (sum beta^2 <= 1 guaranteed upstream).
    sum_sq = np.add.reduceat(loading_values * loading_values, loading_indptr[:-1])
    idio_weight = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - sum_sq))

    return MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd_arr,
        thresholds=_gaussian_thresholds(pd_arr),
        loading_indptr=loading_indptr,
        loading_values=loading_values,
        systemic_uix=systemic_uix,
        idiosyncratic_uix=idiosyncratic_uix,
        idio_weight=np.ascontiguousarray(idio_weight),
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/builders/mf_students_t.py
"""Per-model JTD block builder. Input is assumed structurally and semantically
valid (validated upstream in `validate.py`): the loadings frame is present and
routable, every obligor covered, no factor duplicated, sum-beta^2 <= 1, and the
chi/nu reconciliation holds. This builder packs CSR (band order), precomputes the
idio weight, and sets Student-t thresholds (mirrors mf_gaussian + sf_students_t).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_students_t import MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest._shared import (
    _f64,
    _i64,
    _join_pd_lgd_from_master,
    _resolve_factor_uix,
    _students_t_thresholds,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit


def build_mf_students_t_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
    mf_loadings_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock:
    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    n = obligor_uids.shape[0]
    pd_arr, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)
    nu = _f64(df, "nu")

    idiosyncratic_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        df["idiosyncratic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
        factor_name_to_uix,
        ctx="idiosyncratic_factor",
    )
    chi_squared_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        df["chi_squared_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
        factor_name_to_uix,
        ctx="chi_squared_factor",
    )

    # Band-order position of each obligor; filter the shared loadings frame to
    # this block's obligors (others belong to the MF-gaussian block).
    uid_to_pos = {int(uid): pos for pos, uid in enumerate(obligor_uids)}
    load_uids = mf_loadings_df["obligor_uid"].to_numpy()
    keep = np.array([int(u) in uid_to_pos for u in load_uids], dtype=bool)

    pos = np.array([uid_to_pos[int(u)] for u in load_uids[keep]], dtype=np.int64)
    loadings = _f64(mf_loadings_df.loc[keep], "factor_loading")
    sys_names = mf_loadings_df.loc[keep, "factor_name"].to_numpy(copy=False)

    # Stable sort by obligor position -> CSR-contiguous per obligor, band order.
    order = np.argsort(pos, kind="stable")
    pos_sorted = pos[order]
    loading_values = np.ascontiguousarray(loadings[order])
    systemic_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        sys_names[order], factor_name_to_uix, ctx="mf systemic factor_name"
    )

    counts = np.bincount(pos_sorted, minlength=n)
    loading_indptr = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    loading_indptr[0] = 0
    np.cumsum(counts, out=loading_indptr[1:])

    # Independent systemic factors -> systemic variance = sum of squared loadings.
    sum_sq = np.add.reduceat(loading_values * loading_values, loading_indptr[:-1])
    idio_weight = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - sum_sq))

    return MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd_arr,
        thresholds=_students_t_thresholds(pd_arr, nu),
        loading_indptr=loading_indptr,
        loading_values=loading_values,
        systemic_uix=systemic_uix,
        idiosyncratic_uix=idiosyncratic_uix,
        idio_weight=np.ascontiguousarray(idio_weight),
        chi_squared_uix=chi_squared_uix,
        sqrt_nu=np.sqrt(nu),
    )
```

```python
"""Per-model JTD block builder. Input is assumed structurally and semantically
```

```python
    StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,

        df["stress_regime_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),

        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\ingest\bundle.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/bundle.py
"""
The mixed JTD ingest master.

`JtdIngestBundle` is the input-side analogue of `CompiledJtdPortfolio`: one
optional per-model authoring frame per JTD model, plus the shared long-format
multi-factor loadings relation. A "mixed portfolio" is this collection of
tightly-typed per-model masters — never a single wide union frame. The per-model
field order matches `CompiledJtdPortfolio`'s block band order so input and output
stay aligned.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "JtdIngestBundle",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
from typing import Final

import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import model_field_order

# Canonical (model -> bundle attribute) pairing, in compiled-portfolio band
# order. Single source = the model registry; the self-validation guards below
# confirm the derived attrs exist on this dataclass.
_MODEL_FIELD_ORDER: Final[tuple[tuple[JtdModel, str], ...]] = model_field_order()


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class JtdIngestBundle:
    """Mixed JTD ingest master: optional per-model frames + shared loadings."""

    forced_default_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    independent_bernoulli_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    beta_binomial_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    stress_regime_bernoulli_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    sf_gaussian_copula_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    sf_students_t_copula_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    mf_gaussian_copula_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None
    mf_students_t_copula_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None

    # Shared long-format relation (obligor_uid, factor_name, factor_loading),
    # consumed by every multi-factor model. NOT a per-model frame.
    mf_loadings_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None

    def frames_by_model(self) -> dict[JtdModel, pd.DataFrame]:
        """Populated per-model frames keyed by model, in band order.

        Excludes `mf_loadings_df` (a relation, not a per-model frame).
        """
        out: dict[JtdModel, pd.DataFrame] = {}
        for model, attr in _MODEL_FIELD_ORDER:
            frame = getattr(self, attr)
            if frame is not None:
                out[model] = frame
        return out

    def is_empty(self) -> bool:
        """True if no per-model frame is populated (loadings alone is empty)."""
        return not self.frames_by_model()

    @classmethod
    def from_instrument_frame(
        cls,
        frame: pd.DataFrame,
        *,
        instrument_col: str = "instrument",
        mf_loadings_df: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
    ) -> JtdIngestBundle:
        """Authoring convenience: split a denormalized frame that carries an
        ``instrument`` column (a ``JtdModel`` member name per row) into the
        per-model slots, dropping the now-redundant ``instrument`` column.

        This is NOT a compile path — ``compile_jtd_portfolio`` only accepts a
        bundle. It exists for callers (demos, tests, programmatically generated
        mixed portfolios) that find it natural to emit one frame and normalize
        it here. The canonical authoring form is still the per-model slots.
        Unknown instrument names fail fast.
        """
        if instrument_col not in frame.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"from_instrument_frame: frame has no '{instrument_col}' column."
            )
        name_to_attr = {model.name: attr for model, attr in _MODEL_FIELD_ORDER}
        present = set(frame[instrument_col].astype(str).unique())
        unknown = present - set(name_to_attr)
        if unknown:
            raise ValueError(
                f"from_instrument_frame: unknown instrument(s) {sorted(unknown)}."
            )
        kwargs: dict[str, pd.DataFrame] = {}
        for name, attr in name_to_attr.items():
            sub = frame[frame[instrument_col] == name]
            if not sub.empty:
                kwargs[attr] = sub.drop(columns=[instrument_col]).reset_index(drop=True)
        return cls(mf_loadings_df=mf_loadings_df, **kwargs)


# Guard: every JTD model must have a bundle slot (mirrors the contract guard).
_covered = {m for m, _ in _MODEL_FIELD_ORDER}
_missing = [m.name for m in JtdModel if m not in _covered]
if _missing:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"JtdIngestBundle has no frame slot for JTD model(s): {_missing}. "
        "Add them to _MODEL_FIELD_ORDER in credit/jtd/ingest/bundle.py."
    )

# Guard: the per-model attribute names actually exist on the dataclass.
_attrs = {f.name for f in fields(JtdIngestBundle)}
_bad = [attr for _, attr in _MODEL_FIELD_ORDER if attr not in _attrs]
if _bad:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"_MODEL_FIELD_ORDER references unknown JtdIngestBundle field(s): {_bad}."
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/compile.py
"""
JTD ingest orchestration: a `JtdIngestBundle` (per-model masters + the shared
multi-factor loadings relation) + a validated `CompiledCreditMasters`
aggregate -> `CompiledJtdPortfolio`.

The compile is a thin pipeline:

    structural validate  (validate.py: shape, dtypes, uid invariants)
        -> semantic validate (validate.py: factor kinds, distinctness, ranges,
                              chi/nu reconciliation, sum-beta^2)
        -> per-model builders (builders/: resolve + construct; input assumed valid)
        -> assemble CompiledJtdPortfolio

PD and LGD are never on the ingest frames — they are joined from the obligor
master (the single source of truth) inside the builders. Factor names die here,
resolved to int32 UIX against the factor universe.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "compile_jtd_portfolio",
]

from typing import Any

from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.validate import (
    validate_ingest_bundle,
    validate_ingest_bundle_semantics,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import JTD_MODEL_SPECS
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import CompiledCreditMasters


def compile_jtd_portfolio(
    *,
    bundle: JtdIngestBundle,
    masters: CompiledCreditMasters,
) -> CompiledJtdPortfolio:
    """Compile a validated JTD ingest bundle into a ``CompiledJtdPortfolio``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bundle
        The mixed JTD ingest master: one optional per-model frame per
        ``JtdModel`` plus the shared long-format ``mf_loadings`` relation. Each
        per-model frame is one row per obligor and carries only that model's
        contract columns (``instrument`` is implicit in the frame's slot). PD/LGD
        are NOT carried — they are joined from the obligor master.
    masters
        Validated credit master aggregate. Holding it is proof its constituent
        masters cross-validate, so the cross-master guarantee is a precondition
        of compilation rather than the caller's discipline. The obligor master
        is the single source of truth for PD/LGD; factor names resolve to int32
        UIX against the factor universe, whose contract also supplies the chi
        degrees of freedom that reconcile obligor ``nu``.
    """
    obligor_master = masters.obligor_master
    factor_universe = masters.factor_universe
    # 1. Structural validation (shape / dtypes / uid invariants / loadings shape).
    validate_ingest_bundle(bundle, obligor_master)

    # 2. Semantic / wiring validation (factor kinds, distinctness, ranges, chi
    #    reconciliation, sum-beta^2). After this, builders assume valid input.
    validate_ingest_bundle_semantics(bundle, factor_universe)

    factor_name_to_uix = factor_universe.factor_name_to_uix
    frames = bundle.frames_by_model()
    mf_loadings_df = bundle.mf_loadings_df

    # Registry-driven dispatch: one pass over the specs, build each populated
    # model's block via its (signature-tagged) builder, key it by its compiled
    # slot. No per-model branch here — adding a model is one registry entry.
    blocks_by_slot: dict[str, Any] = {}
    for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS:
        frame = frames.get(spec.model)
        if frame is None:
            continue
        args: list[object] = [frame, obligor_master]
        if spec.needs_factor_uix:
            args.append(factor_name_to_uix)
        if spec.needs_mf_loadings:
            if mf_loadings_df is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"compile_jtd_portfolio: {spec.model.name} requires a "
                    f"long-format loadings frame, but bundle.mf_loadings_df is "
                    f"None."
                )
            args.append(mf_loadings_df)
        blocks_by_slot[spec.compiled_slot] = spec.builder(*args)

    return CompiledJtdPortfolio(**blocks_by_slot)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/contracts.py
"""
JTD ingest contracts: the single source of truth for what each model's master
frame must contain.

Every JTD model is authored as its own tightly-typed per-model master (one row
per obligor, no nullable union columns). This module declares, per model, the
exact column contract — which columns are required, which are factor-NAME
references (resolved to UIX downstream), which are float-valued, and whether the
model consumes the shared multi-factor loadings relation. Both the structural
validators (`validate.py`) and any generated documentation read from here, so
there is exactly one place that answers "what does it take to run model X".

The obligor key on every per-model frame is `OBLIGOR_KEY` ("uid"); PD and LGD are
never carried here — they are joined from the obligor master. The `instrument`
column is intentionally NOT part of any contract: a per-model frame's identity
(its slot in `JtdIngestBundle`) already declares the model, so an inline
`instrument` column would be redundant state.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Final

from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.masters.enums.credit_factor_kind import CreditFactorKind

# Obligor key column on every per-model master frame.
OBLIGOR_KEY: Final[str] = "uid"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class JtdModelContract:
    """Column contract for one JTD model's per-obligor master frame.

    `required_columns` always includes `OBLIGOR_KEY`. `factor_name_columns` and
    `float_columns` are subsets of `required_columns`, partitioning the
    model-specific columns by how they are validated and resolved downstream.
    `uses_mf_loadings` flags models whose systemic loadings live in the separate
    long-format relation rather than on this frame.
    """

    model: JtdModel
    required_columns: frozenset[str]
    factor_name_columns: frozenset[str] = field(default_factory=frozenset)
    float_columns: frozenset[str] = field(default_factory=frozenset)
    distinct_columns: frozenset[str] = field(default_factory=frozenset)
    uses_mf_loadings: bool = False

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if OBLIGOR_KEY not in self.required_columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{self.model.name} contract must require the obligor key "
                f"'{OBLIGOR_KEY}'."
            )
        extra = (self.factor_name_columns | self.float_columns) - self.required_columns
        if extra:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{self.model.name} contract: factor_name/float columns "
                f"{sorted(extra)} are not in required_columns."
            )
        overlap = self.factor_name_columns & self.float_columns
        if overlap:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{self.model.name} contract: column(s) {sorted(overlap)} cannot "
                "be both factor-name and float."
            )
        bad_distinct = self.distinct_columns - self.factor_name_columns
        if bad_distinct:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{self.model.name} contract: distinct_columns {sorted(bad_distinct)} "
                "must be factor-name columns."
            )


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MfLoadingsContract:
    """Column contract for the shared long-format multi-factor loadings relation
    (one row per obligor x systemic factor)."""

    required_columns: frozenset[str]
    obligor_key: str = "obligor_uid"
    factor_name_column: str = "factor_name"
    loading_column: str = "factor_loading"


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-model contracts
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------

JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS: Final[dict[JtdModel, JtdModelContract]] = {
    JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT,
        required_columns=frozenset({OBLIGOR_KEY}),
    ),
    JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI,
        required_columns=frozenset({OBLIGOR_KEY, "uniform_factor"}),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor"}),
        distinct_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor"}),
    ),
    JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL,
        required_columns=frozenset({OBLIGOR_KEY, "uniform_factor", "beta_factor"}),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor", "beta_factor"}),
        distinct_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor"}),
    ),
    JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI,
        required_columns=frozenset(
            {
                OBLIGOR_KEY,
                "uniform_factor",
                "stress_regime_factor",
                "stress_pd_multiplier",
            }
        ),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor", "stress_regime_factor"}),
        float_columns=frozenset({"stress_pd_multiplier"}),
        distinct_columns=frozenset({"uniform_factor"}),
    ),
    JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN,
        required_columns=frozenset(
            {OBLIGOR_KEY, "factor_loading", "systemic_factor", "idiosyncratic_factor"}
        ),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"systemic_factor", "idiosyncratic_factor"}),
        float_columns=frozenset({"factor_loading"}),
    ),
    JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T,
        required_columns=frozenset(
            {
                OBLIGOR_KEY,
                "factor_loading",
                "systemic_factor",
                "idiosyncratic_factor",
                "chi_squared_factor",
                "nu",
            }
        ),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset(
            {"systemic_factor", "idiosyncratic_factor", "chi_squared_factor"}
        ),
        float_columns=frozenset({"factor_loading", "nu"}),
    ),
    JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN,
        required_columns=frozenset({OBLIGOR_KEY, "idiosyncratic_factor"}),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"idiosyncratic_factor"}),
        uses_mf_loadings=True,
    ),
    JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T: JtdModelContract(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T,
        required_columns=frozenset(
            {OBLIGOR_KEY, "idiosyncratic_factor", "chi_squared_factor", "nu"}
        ),
        factor_name_columns=frozenset({"idiosyncratic_factor", "chi_squared_factor"}),
        float_columns=frozenset({"nu"}),
        uses_mf_loadings=True,
    ),
}

MF_LOADINGS_CONTRACT: Final[MfLoadingsContract] = MfLoadingsContract(
    required_columns=frozenset({"obligor_uid", "factor_name", "factor_loading"}),
)


# Role (factor-name column) -> required CreditFactorKind. Model-independent: a
# `uniform_factor` is always UNIFORM, a `systemic_factor` always SYSTEMIC, etc.
# Drives the semantic kind-validation loop so each role's kind is asserted in
# exactly one place.
FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND: Final[dict[str, CreditFactorKind]] = {
    "systemic_factor": CreditFactorKind.SYSTEMIC,
    "idiosyncratic_factor": CreditFactorKind.IDIOSYNCRATIC,
    "chi_squared_factor": CreditFactorKind.CHI,
    "uniform_factor": CreditFactorKind.UNIFORM,
    "beta_factor": CreditFactorKind.BETA_PD,
    "stress_regime_factor": CreditFactorKind.STRESS_INDICATOR,
}


def contract_for(model: JtdModel) -> JtdModelContract:
    return JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[model]


# Fail loud at import if a model ships without a contract (mirrors the engine's
# evaluator-registration guard).
_missing = [m.name for m in JtdModel if m not in JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS]
if _missing:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No ingest contract registered for JTD model(s): {_missing}. "
        "Add them to JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS in credit/jtd/ingest/contracts.py."
    )


_uncovered = sorted(
    {c for ct in JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS.values() for c in ct.factor_name_columns}
    - set(FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND)
)
if _uncovered:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"factor-name column(s) {_uncovered} have no FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND entry."
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\ingest\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest/validate.py
"""
Structural validation of the JTD ingest bundle.

This is the contract-driven layer: it enforces the shape of each per-model
master and of the shared multi-factor loadings relation BEFORE any block is
built — required columns present, no nulls in required columns, dtype families
correct, obligor key unique within a frame and a subset of the obligor master,
and (critically) an obligor appears in at most one per-model frame. Semantic /
wiring validation (factor kinds, distinct uniforms, sum-beta^2 <= 1, chi-nu
homogeneity) is model-specific and lands separately; this layer answers only
"is the master shaped the way its contract demands".

Every check fails fast with a specific, actionable message. After this passes,
the builders may treat their input as structurally valid.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.contracts import (
    JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS,
    MF_LOADINGS_CONTRACT,
    OBLIGOR_KEY,
    JtdModelContract,
    MfLoadingsContract,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.enums.credit_factor_kind import CreditFactorKind

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit

_PREVIEW = 10


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-frame structural predicates
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------


def validate_required_columns(
    df: pd.DataFrame, contract: JtdModelContract, *, ctx: str
) -> None:
    missing = sorted(contract.required_columns - set(df.columns))
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: master for {contract.model.name} is missing required "
            f"column(s) {missing}. Required: {sorted(contract.required_columns)}."
        )


def validate_no_required_nulls(
    df: pd.DataFrame, contract: JtdModelContract, *, ctx: str
) -> None:
    for col in sorted(contract.required_columns):
        if df[col].isna().any():
            n = int(df[col].isna().sum())
            raise ValueError(
                f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} column '{col}' has {n} null "
                "value(s); per-model masters are dense by contract."
            )


def validate_dtypes(df: pd.DataFrame, contract: JtdModelContract, *, ctx: str) -> None:
    if not pd.api.types.is_integer_dtype(df[OBLIGOR_KEY]):
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} '{OBLIGOR_KEY}' must be an integer "
            f"dtype, got {df[OBLIGOR_KEY].dtype}."
        )
    for col in sorted(contract.float_columns):
        if not pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(df[col]):
            raise ValueError(
                f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} '{col}' must be numeric, got "
                f"{df[col].dtype}."
            )
    for col in sorted(contract.factor_name_columns):
        if not (
            pd.api.types.is_object_dtype(df[col])
            or pd.api.types.is_string_dtype(df[col])
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} factor-name column '{col}' must be "
                f"string-like, got {df[col].dtype}."
            )


def validate_uid_unique_within_frame(
    df: pd.DataFrame, contract: JtdModelContract, *, ctx: str
) -> None:
    uids = df[OBLIGOR_KEY].to_numpy()
    vals, counts = np.unique(uids, return_counts=True)
    dups = vals[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
    if dups:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} has duplicate obligor uid(s) {dups}; "
            "each obligor must appear at most once per model frame."
        )


def validate_uids_in_master(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    contract: JtdModelContract,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    uids = df[OBLIGOR_KEY].to_numpy()
    known = np.isin(uids, obligor_master.uid)
    if not known.all():
        unknown = np.unique(uids[~known])[:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {contract.model.name} references obligor uid(s) {unknown} "
            "absent from the obligor master (the single source of truth for "
            "PD/LGD)."
        )


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cross-frame structural predicates
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------


def validate_uid_disjoint_across_bundle(bundle: JtdIngestBundle, *, ctx: str) -> None:
    """An obligor carries exactly one JTD model, so its uid may appear in at most
    one per-model frame. The wide union frame enforced this implicitly (one row
    per uid); per-model frames must enforce it explicitly."""
    seen: dict[int, str] = {}
    for model, frame in bundle.frames_by_model().items():
        for uid in frame[OBLIGOR_KEY].tolist():
            uid_i = int(uid)
            if uid_i in seen:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{ctx}: obligor uid {uid_i} appears in both "
                    f"{seen[uid_i]} and {model.name}. Each obligor must be routed "
                    "to exactly one JTD model."
                )
            seen[uid_i] = model.name


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Multi-factor loadings relation
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------


def validate_mf_loadings_structural(
    mf_loadings_df: pd.DataFrame, contract: MfLoadingsContract, *, ctx: str
) -> None:
    missing = sorted(contract.required_columns - set(mf_loadings_df.columns))
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx}: mf_loadings missing required column(s) {missing}.")

    key = contract.obligor_key
    if mf_loadings_df[key].isna().any():
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{key}' has null value(s).")
    if not pd.api.types.is_integer_dtype(mf_loadings_df[key]):
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{key}' must be an integer dtype, got "
            f"{mf_loadings_df[key].dtype}."
        )

    name_col = contract.factor_name_column
    if mf_loadings_df[name_col].isna().any():
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{name_col}' has null value(s).")
    if not (
        pd.api.types.is_object_dtype(mf_loadings_df[name_col])
        or pd.api.types.is_string_dtype(mf_loadings_df[name_col])
    ):
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{name_col}' must be string-like, got "
            f"{mf_loadings_df[name_col].dtype}."
        )

    load_col = contract.loading_column
    if not pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(mf_loadings_df[load_col]):
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{load_col}' must be numeric, got "
            f"{mf_loadings_df[load_col].dtype}."
        )
    loadings = mf_loadings_df[load_col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(loadings)):
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx}: mf_loadings '{load_col}' contains NaN/inf.")


def validate_mf_loadings_routable(
    bundle: JtdIngestBundle, contract: MfLoadingsContract, *, ctx: str
) -> None:
    """Every loading row must attach to an obligor that actually appears in a
    multi-factor model frame. Guards the silent-drop class (a typo'd or
    mis-routed obligor_uid would otherwise vanish in the per-block filter)."""
    if bundle.mf_loadings_df is None:
        return

    mf_uids: set[int] = set()
    for model, frame in bundle.frames_by_model().items():
        if JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[model].uses_mf_loadings:
            mf_uids.update(int(u) for u in frame[OBLIGOR_KEY].tolist())

    if not mf_uids:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: mf_loadings supplied but the bundle has no multi-factor "
            "obligors; the loadings are unroutable."
        )

    key = contract.obligor_key
    stray = sorted({int(u) for u in bundle.mf_loadings_df[key].tolist()} - mf_uids)
    if stray:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: mf_loadings references obligor_uid(s) {stray[:_PREVIEW]} that "
            "are not multi-factor obligors in any bundle frame (silent-drop "
            "guard)."
        )


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Entry point
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------


def validate_ingest_bundle(
    bundle: JtdIngestBundle,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> None:
    """Run the full structural validation pass over a JTD ingest bundle.

    Fails fast on the first violated invariant. After this returns, every
    populated frame satisfies its column contract, uids are unique-per-frame,
    master-resident, and disjoint across frames, and the loadings relation is
    well-shaped and routable.
    """
    if bundle.is_empty():
        raise ValueError(
            "JtdIngestBundle is empty: no per-model master frame is populated."
        )

    for model, frame in bundle.frames_by_model().items():
        contract = JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[model]
        ctx = f"jtd_ingest[{model.name}]"
        validate_required_columns(frame, contract, ctx=ctx)
        validate_no_required_nulls(frame, contract, ctx=ctx)
        validate_dtypes(frame, contract, ctx=ctx)
        validate_uid_unique_within_frame(frame, contract, ctx=ctx)
        validate_uids_in_master(frame, obligor_master, contract, ctx=ctx)

    validate_uid_disjoint_across_bundle(bundle, ctx="jtd_ingest")

    # Multi-factor loadings: required iff any MF model frame is present.
    mf_present = any(
        JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[m].uses_mf_loadings for m in bundle.frames_by_model()
    )
    if mf_present and bundle.mf_loadings_df is None:
        raise ValueError(
            "jtd_ingest: a multi-factor model frame is present but mf_loadings "
            "is missing."
        )
    if bundle.mf_loadings_df is not None:
        validate_mf_loadings_structural(
            bundle.mf_loadings_df, MF_LOADINGS_CONTRACT, ctx="jtd_ingest[mf_loadings]"
        )
        validate_mf_loadings_routable(
            bundle, MF_LOADINGS_CONTRACT, ctx="jtd_ingest[mf_loadings]"
        )


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Semantic / wiring validators (model-specific correctness)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Beyond shape: factor-role kinds, conditional independence of uniforms, loading
# ranges, and the Student-t chi/nu reconciliation. They operate on RESOLVED uix
# (the builders resolve names -> uix via _shared._resolve_factor_uix, then call
# these). chi reconciliation additionally reads the universe's chi_nu_by_uix so
# the obligor frame cannot disagree with the generator about degrees of freedom.


def assert_factor_kind(
    uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    expected: CreditFactorKind,
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Every resolved UIX must be a factor of the kind its role requires.

    Catches mis-wiring the loose name→UIX dict cannot: e.g. a beta-binomial
    obligor pointing ``beta_factor`` at a UNIFORM column, or a Bernoulli obligor
    pointing ``uniform_factor`` at a systemic normal. The kind comes from the
    universe master (``factor_kind`` is UIX-indexed)."""
    actual = factor_kind[uix]
    bad = actual != np.int8(expected)
    if bad.any():
        offending = np.unique(uix[bad])[:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        got = {int(k): CreditFactorKind(int(k)).name for k in np.unique(actual[bad])}
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: factor(s) at UIX {offending} must be kind "
            f"{expected.name}, but resolve to kind(s) {got}."
        )


def assert_uniforms_distinct(
    uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Each obligor needs its OWN uniform row. Sharing a uniform column makes
    the conditionally-independent default draws comonotonic (rank-identical),
    silently breaking the independence the Bernoulli-family models assume."""
    vals, counts = np.unique(uix, return_counts=True)
    shared = vals[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
    if shared:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: uniform factor UIX {shared} are shared across obligors. "
            "Each obligor requires a distinct uniform factor for conditional "
            "independence (a shared uniform makes defaults comonotonic)."
        )


def validate_factor_loading_range(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64], factor_loading: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    bad = ~((factor_loading >= -1.0) & (factor_loading <= 1.0))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_jtd_portfolio: factor_loading outside [-1, 1] for obligor(s) "
            f"{obligor_uids[bad][:_PREVIEW].tolist()}."
        )


def validate_nu_positive(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64], nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    bad = ~(nu > 0.0)  # also catches NaN
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_jtd_portfolio: nu must be > 0 (finite) for obligor(s) "
            f"{obligor_uids[bad][:_PREVIEW].tolist()}."
        )


def validate_chi_group_nu_homogeneity(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    chi_squared_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    """Within a chi group, nu must be constant.

    Obligors sharing a ``chi_squared_uix`` share one mixing draw
    ``W ~ chi^2(nu)`` per scenario — the grouped t-copula construction (Daul,
    De Giorgi, Lindskog & McNeil, 2003). A single W cannot simultaneously be
    chi-square of two different dof, so every obligor on a given chi column
    must declare the same nu. The kernel's ``sqrt(nu_i / W)`` transform assumes
    this silently: a mismatch produces a scaled-t with the wrong dof and a
    miscalibrated marginal PD, with no runtime error. Fail fast here.

    The check is the group invariant only; it does not (cannot, from the frame
    alone) verify that the shared nu equals the dof the chi column was actually
    drawn with in the generator — that cross-layer guarantee comes from
    deriving the generator's ``nu_by_chi`` from these same blocks.
    """
    if chi_squared_uix.shape[0] == 0:
        return

    # Group by chi column via a stable sort; group nu-range == 0 iff constant.
    order = np.argsort(chi_squared_uix, kind="stable")
    cu = chi_squared_uix[order]
    nv = nu[order]

    starts = np.concatenate(([0], np.flatnonzero(np.diff(cu)) + 1))
    bad_groups = np.flatnonzero(
        np.minimum.reduceat(nv, starts) != np.maximum.reduceat(nv, starts)
    )
    if bad_groups.size == 0:
        return

    uix_to_name = {uix: name for name, uix in factor_name_to_uix.items()}
    uu = obligor_uids[order]
    ends = np.concatenate((starts[1:], [cu.shape[0]]))

    details = []
    for g in bad_groups[:_PREVIEW]:
        s, e = int(starts[g]), int(ends[g])
        col = int(cu[s])
        name = uix_to_name.get(col, f"<uix {col}>")
        nus = np.unique(nv[s:e]).tolist()
        uids = uu[s:e][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        details.append(f"{name!r}: nu={nus} across obligor(s) {uids}")

    raise ValueError(
        "COPULA_SF_T: obligors sharing a chi_squared_factor must declare the "
        "same nu (one W ~ chi^2(nu) per scenario cannot match multiple dof). "
        "Offending chi group(s): " + "; ".join(details) + "."
    )


def validate_chi_nu_against_reference(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    chi_squared_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    chi_nu_by_uix: Mapping[int, float],
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    """Reconcile obligor nu against the dof its chi column is actually drawn with.

    The kernel forms ``T_i = sqrt(nu_i / W) * A_i``; the marginal is Student-t
    with ``nu_i`` dof ONLY if the chi column obligor i references is drawn as
    ``W ~ chi^2(nu_i)``. ``nu_i`` lives on the obligor frame; the draw dof lives
    on the generator (``nu_by_chi``). They are independent inputs and nothing
    else reconciles them, so two silent failure modes exist:

    - **mismatch** — obligor declares ``nu_i`` but the generator draws that chi
      column at a different dof ``m``: ``sqrt(nu_i / W)`` with ``W ~ chi^2(m)``
      is a scaled-t with ``m`` dof, and ``F_t^{-1}(PD; nu_i)`` no longer gives
      ``P(T_i < t_i) = PD_i``.
    - **absent** — obligor references a column the generator does not draw as
      chi at all: that column carries a standard normal, not ``1/sqrt(W)``, so
      the latent variable is nonsense.

    Both produce plausible-looking losses with no runtime error. Fail fast.

    ``chi_nu_by_uix`` maps chi factor UIX -> dof and must come from the SAME
    generator that produces the factor matrix (``generator.chi_nu_by_uix()``),
    never a parallel registry that could itself have drifted.
    """
    if chi_squared_uix.shape[0] == 0:
        return

    # Vectorized membership + lookup against the (small) chi map via sorted
    # searchsorted — same primitive shape as the package's uid joins, no
    # per-obligor dict loop.
    keys = np.fromiter(chi_nu_by_uix.keys(), dtype=np.int64, count=len(chi_nu_by_uix))
    vals = np.fromiter(
        chi_nu_by_uix.values(), dtype=np.float64, count=len(chi_nu_by_uix)
    )
    sorter = np.argsort(keys, kind="stable")
    skeys = keys[sorter]
    svals = vals[sorter]

    chi64 = chi_squared_uix.astype(np.int64)
    n_keys = skeys.shape[0]
    if n_keys == 0:
        # No chi columns declared by the generator at all: every t obligor
        # references a column that will be drawn as a standard normal.
        present = np.zeros(chi64.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)
        ref_nu = np.full(chi64.shape[0], np.nan, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        pos = np.searchsorted(skeys, chi64)
        pos_clipped = np.clip(pos, 0, n_keys - 1)
        present = (pos < n_keys) & (skeys[pos_clipped] == chi64)
        ref_nu = np.where(present, svals[pos_clipped], np.nan)

    uix_to_name = {uix: name for name, uix in factor_name_to_uix.items()}

    absent = ~present
    if absent.any():
        bad_cols = sorted({int(c) for c in chi_squared_uix[absent].tolist()})
        names = [uix_to_name.get(c, f"<uix {c}>") for c in bad_cols]
        uids = obligor_uids[absent][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            "COPULA_SF_T: chi_squared_factor(s) "
            f"{names} are referenced by obligor(s) {uids} but the generator "
            "does not draw them as chi columns (chi_nu_by_uix has no entry). "
            "The generator would emit a standard normal there, not 1/sqrt(W)."
        )

    mismatch = ref_nu != nu  # present everywhere here; NaN already excluded
    if mismatch.any():
        idx = np.flatnonzero(mismatch)[:_PREVIEW]
        details = [
            f"uid {int(obligor_uids[i])} "
            f"({uix_to_name.get(int(chi_squared_uix[i]), '<uix>')}): "
            f"declared nu={float(nu[i])}, generator draws nu={float(ref_nu[i])}"
            for i in idx
        ]
        raise ValueError(
            "COPULA_SF_T: obligor nu disagrees with the dof its chi column is "
            "drawn with (the t-marginal would be miscalibrated). "
            + "; ".join(details)
            + "."
        )


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Semantic pre-build pass (the orchestration over the predicates above)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Runs AFTER structural validation and BEFORE any builder. Resolves each frame's
# factor-name columns to UIX and asserts wiring correctness, so the per-model
# builders can construct from input they may treat as fully valid. Resolution
# here is ingest-time and vectorized; the builders re-resolve for construction
# (cheap, and keeps validation and construction decoupled).

from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest._shared import _resolve_factor_uix  # noqa: E402
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.contracts import FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND  # noqa: E402
from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import JTD_MODEL_SPECS  # noqa: E402


def _resolve(frame: pd.DataFrame, col: str, factor_name_to_uix, *, ctx: str):
    return _resolve_factor_uix(
        frame[col].to_numpy(copy=False), factor_name_to_uix, ctx=f"{ctx}.{col}"
    )


def _assert_column_kinds(frame, contract, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, *, ctx):
    """Every factor-name column must resolve to the kind its role requires."""
    for col in sorted(contract.factor_name_columns):
        uix = _resolve(frame, col, factor_name_to_uix, ctx=ctx)
        assert_factor_kind(
            uix, FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND[col], factor_kind, ctx=f"{ctx}.{col}"
        )


def _assert_distinct_columns(frame, contract, factor_name_to_uix, *, ctx):
    for col in sorted(contract.distinct_columns):
        uix = _resolve(frame, col, factor_name_to_uix, ctx=ctx)
        assert_uniforms_distinct(uix, ctx=f"{ctx}.{col}")


def _semantic_sf_gaussian(
    frame, *, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, chi_nu_by_uix, bundle, ctx
):
    validate_factor_loading_range(
        frame["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64),
        frame["factor_loading"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64),
    )


def _semantic_sf_students_t(
    frame, *, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, chi_nu_by_uix, bundle, ctx
):
    uids = frame["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
    validate_factor_loading_range(
        uids, frame["factor_loading"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    )
    nu = frame["nu"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    validate_nu_positive(uids, nu)
    chi_uix = _resolve(frame, "chi_squared_factor", factor_name_to_uix, ctx=ctx)
    validate_chi_group_nu_homogeneity(uids, chi_uix, nu, factor_name_to_uix)
    validate_chi_nu_against_reference(
        uids, chi_uix, nu, chi_nu_by_uix, factor_name_to_uix
    )


def _semantic_stress(
    frame, *, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, chi_nu_by_uix, bundle, ctx
):
    stress_uix = _resolve(frame, "stress_regime_factor", factor_name_to_uix, ctx=ctx)
    if not np.all(stress_uix == stress_uix[0]):
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: stress_regime_factor must be a single global factor "
            "(all obligors share one stress indicator)."
        )


def _semantic_mf_gaussian(
    frame, *, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, chi_nu_by_uix, bundle, ctx
):
    """Systemic-kind, coverage, no-duplicate, and sum-beta^2 <= 1 — all checked
    on the long-format loadings relation (the builder then packs CSR)."""
    loadings = bundle.mf_loadings_df  # structural validation guarantees presence
    sub = loadings[loadings["obligor_uid"].isin(set(frame["uid"].tolist()))]

    sys_uix = _resolve_factor_uix(
        sub["factor_name"].to_numpy(copy=False),
        factor_name_to_uix,
        ctx=f"{ctx}.systemic_factor",
    )
    assert_factor_kind(
        sys_uix,
        FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND["systemic_factor"],
        factor_kind,
        ctx=f"{ctx}.systemic_factor",
    )

    # Coverage: every MF obligor needs >= 1 loading row.
    have = set(int(u) for u in sub["obligor_uid"].tolist())
    missing = sorted(set(int(u) for u in frame["uid"].tolist()) - have)
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: obligor(s) {missing[:_PREVIEW]} have no systemic loadings."
        )

    # No factor referenced twice for one obligor.
    if sub.duplicated(subset=["obligor_uid", "factor_name"]).any():
        dups = (
            sub.loc[
                sub.duplicated(subset=["obligor_uid", "factor_name"]), "obligor_uid"
            ]
            .unique()
            .tolist()[:_PREVIEW]
        )
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: obligor(s) {dups} load on the same systemic factor twice."
        )

    # sum beta^2 <= 1 (room for a real idiosyncratic component).
    sum_sq = sub["factor_loading"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64) ** 2
    by = pd.Series(sum_sq).groupby(sub["obligor_uid"].to_numpy()).sum()
    over = by[by > 1.0 + 1e-9].index.tolist()[:_PREVIEW]
    if over:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: sum of squared loadings exceeds 1 for obligor(s) {over}."
        )


def _semantic_mf_students_t(
    frame, *, factor_name_to_uix, factor_kind, chi_nu_by_uix, bundle, ctx
):
    """MF systemic loadings checks (coverage / no-dup / sum-beta^2 / systemic
    kind) plus the Student-t chi/nu reconciliation. The systemic side lives on
    the shared loadings relation; nu and the chi factor live on this frame."""
    _semantic_mf_gaussian(
        frame,
        factor_name_to_uix=factor_name_to_uix,
        factor_kind=factor_kind,
        chi_nu_by_uix=chi_nu_by_uix,
        bundle=bundle,
        ctx=ctx,
    )
    uids = frame["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
    nu = frame["nu"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    validate_nu_positive(uids, nu)
    chi_uix = _resolve(frame, "chi_squared_factor", factor_name_to_uix, ctx=ctx)
    validate_chi_group_nu_homogeneity(uids, chi_uix, nu, factor_name_to_uix)
    validate_chi_nu_against_reference(
        uids, chi_uix, nu, chi_nu_by_uix, factor_name_to_uix
    )


# Per-model semantic-validator dispatch, DERIVED from the model registry: each
# spec names its validator (`semantic_validator_attr`); the function itself lives
# here, co-located with the predicates it calls. Adding a model means declaring
# the name in its spec — no edit to a parallel dict here. A declared-but-missing
# name fails fast at import.
def _resolve_semantic_extra() -> dict:
    table: dict = {}
    for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS:
        attr = spec.semantic_validator_attr
        if attr is None:
            continue
        fn = globals().get(attr)
        if fn is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"{spec.model.name}: semantic_validator_attr '{attr}' is not "
                f"defined in validate.py."
            )
        table[spec.model] = fn
    return table


_SEMANTIC_EXTRA = _resolve_semantic_extra()


def validate_ingest_bundle_semantics(bundle, factor_universe) -> None:
    """Semantic / wiring validation over a structurally-valid bundle.

    Resolves factor-name columns to UIX and asserts: every role resolves to its
    required kind, Bernoulli-family uniforms are distinct, loadings lie in
    range, Student-t nu is positive and chi-consistent with the generator, the
    stress indicator is global, and multi-factor loadings are covered, unique,
    and satisfy sum-beta^2 <= 1. Fails fast on the first violation.
    """
    fmap = factor_universe.factor_name_to_uix
    fkind = factor_universe.factor_kind
    chi_nu = factor_universe.chi_nu_by_uix

    for model, frame in bundle.frames_by_model().items():
        contract = JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[model]
        ctx = f"jtd_semantic[{model.name}]"
        _assert_column_kinds(frame, contract, fmap, fkind, ctx=ctx)
        _assert_distinct_columns(frame, contract, fmap, ctx=ctx)
        extra = _SEMANTIC_EXTRA.get(model)
        if extra is not None:
            extra(
                frame,
                factor_name_to_uix=fmap,
                factor_kind=fkind,
                chi_nu_by_uix=chi_nu,
                bundle=bundle,
                ctx=ctx,
            )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/beta_binomial.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors
        _F64_2D,  # ead
        _F64_1D,  # lgd
        _I32_1D,  # uniform_uix
        _I32_1D,  # beta_uix
    )
)
def kernel_beta_binomial_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    beta_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> None:

    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        lgd_i = lgd[i]

        u_row = latent_factors[uniform_uix[i]]

        beta_row = latent_factors[beta_uix[i]]

        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            defaulted = u_row[s] < beta_row[s]

            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i * defaulted
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/forced_default.py
"""
Forced-default JTD loss kernel.

Scenario-independent: default is certain, so loss is `ead * lgd` with the
per-obligor `lgd` scalar broadcast across the scenario axis. The latent-factor
matrix is never read. Worst-case stress is simply `S = 1`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import _F64_1D, _F64_2D, parallel_njit


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,
        _F64_2D,
        _F64_1D,
    )
)
def kernel_forced_default_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        lgd_i = lgd[i]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/independent_bernoulli.py


from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors
        _F64_2D,  # ead
        _F64_1D,  # lgd
        _F64_1D,  # pd
        _I32_1D,  # uniform_uix
    )
)
def kernel_independent_bernoulli_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
):
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        pd_i = pd[i]
        lgd_i = lgd[i]

        u_row = latent_factors[uniform_uix[i]]

        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            defaulted = u_row[s] < pd_i

            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i * defaulted
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/mf_copula_gaussian.py
"""
Bare-metal multi-factor Gaussian copula JTD loss kernel.

This kernel evaluates Jump-to-Default (JTD) losses under a multi-factor
Gaussian copula model.

Each obligor is exposed to an arbitrary sparse set of systematic credit
factors and one idiosyncratic factor. The systematic exposures are stored in
compressed sparse row (CSR) form to avoid materializing a dense
(obligors × factors) loading matrix.

===============================================================================
MATHEMATICAL MODEL
===============================================================================

For obligor i and scenario s:

    A_i,s
        =
        Σ_j β_i,j Z_j,s
        +
        w_i ε_i,s

where

    A_i,s
        latent asset value

    β_i,j
        loading of obligor i to systematic factor j

    Z_j,s
        realization of systematic factor j in scenario s

    ε_i,s
        realization of the obligor-specific idiosyncratic factor

    w_i
        idiosyncratic loading

The sum runs only over the obligor's non-zero factor exposures.

To preserve unit variance:

    w_i = sqrt(1 - Σ_j β_i,j²)

which is precomputed during ingest and supplied as `idio_weight`.

Default occurs when the latent asset value falls below the obligor's
precomputed threshold:

    default_i,s = 1(A_i,s < threshold_i)

where

    threshold_i = Φ⁻¹(PD_i)

and Φ⁻¹ is the inverse standard-normal CDF.

Realized JTD loss is:

    loss_i,s
        =
        EAD_i,s × LGD_i × default_i,s

===============================================================================
SPARSE FACTOR REPRESENTATION
===============================================================================

Systematic loadings are stored in CSR form:

    loading_indptr
        row pointers for each obligor

    loading_values
        non-zero β values

    systemic_uix
        factor UIX associated with each loading

For obligor i:

    start = loading_indptr[i]
    end   = loading_indptr[i + 1]

the factor exposures are

    loading_values[start:end]

to factors

    systemic_uix[start:end]

This permits O(nnz) storage and computation instead of
O(n_obligors × n_factors).

===============================================================================
KERNEL ALGORITHM
===============================================================================

For each obligor:

1. Initialize the latent accumulator with the idiosyncratic component:

       A_i,s = w_i ε_i,s

2. Accumulate all systematic factor contributions:

       A_i,s += β_i,j Z_j,s

3. Compare against the default threshold:

       default_i,s = (A_i,s < threshold_i)

4. Convert default indicators into realized losses:

       loss_i,s = EAD_i,s × LGD_i × default_i,s

===============================================================================
MEMORY DESIGN
===============================================================================

`out` serves dual purpose:

    Phase 1:
        latent-value accumulator A_i,s

    Phase 2:
        overwritten in-place with realized JTD loss

This avoids allocating a separate latent-value matrix of shape:

    (n_obligors, n_scenarios)

and keeps peak memory usage minimal.

All scenario loops operate over contiguous memory, allowing efficient
vectorized memory access and parallelization across obligors.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    _I64_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out (M, S)
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors (F, S)
        _F64_2D,  # ead (M, S)
        _F64_1D,  # lgd (M,)
        _I64_1D,  # loading_indptr (M + 1,)
        _F64_1D,  # loading_values (nnz,)
        _I32_1D,  # systemic_uix (nnz,)
        _I32_1D,  # idiosyncratic_uix (M,)
        _F64_1D,  # idio_weight (M,)
        _F64_1D,  # thresholds (M,)
    )
)
def kernel_mf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    loading_indptr: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    loading_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    systemic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    idiosyncratic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    idio_weight: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Multi-factor Gaussian copula JTD loss kernel."""
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        out_row = out[i]

        # Seed the latent accumulator with the idiosyncratic component.
        idio_w = idio_weight[i]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idiosyncratic_uix[i]]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = idio_w * eps_row[s]

        # Accumulate systemic contributions factor-outer: each Z row is read
        # unit-stride and folded into the (unit-stride) accumulator.
        start = loading_indptr[i]
        end = loading_indptr[i + 1]
        for j in range(start, end):
            load_j = loading_values[j]
            z_row = latent_factors[systemic_uix[j]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] += load_j * z_row[s]

        # Finalize: latent -> realized loss, in place.
        threshold_i = thresholds[i]
        loss_scale = lgd[i]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * loss_scale * (out_row[s] < threshold_i)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\kernels\mf_copula_students_t.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/mf_copula_students_t.py
"""
Bare-metal multi-factor Student-t copula JTD loss kernel.

Combines the multi-factor Gaussian latent accumulation (sparse CSR systemic
loadings + idiosyncratic, per `kernel_mf_gaussian_copula_jtd`) with the Student-t
chi-mixing of the single-factor t kernel.

For obligor i and scenario s:

    A_gaussian = w_i * eps_i,s + Σ_j β_i,j Z_j,s        (CSR over the obligor)
    A_t        = sqrt(ν_i) * inv_sqrt_w_s * A_gaussian
    default    = 1(A_t < threshold_i)
    loss       = EAD_i,s * LGD_i * default

The rows referenced by `chi_uix` contain ``inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)`` (W ~ χ²),
precomputed in factor generation, so the hot path stays multiply/add/compare with
no square root. `threshold_i` is the Student-t quantile of PD_i at ν_i, set at
ingest. W is shared across obligors in a scenario, producing tail dependence.

`out` is the dual-purpose accumulator: phase 1 holds the Gaussian latent, phase 2
overwrites it in place with realized loss — no separate latent matrix.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    _I64_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out (M, S)
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors (F, S)
        _F64_2D,  # ead (M, S)
        _F64_1D,  # lgd (M,)
        _I64_1D,  # loading_indptr (M + 1,)
        _F64_1D,  # loading_values (nnz,)
        _I32_1D,  # systemic_uix (nnz,)
        _I32_1D,  # idiosyncratic_uix (M,)
        _F64_1D,  # idio_weight (M,)
        _F64_1D,  # thresholds (M,)
        _F64_1D,  # sqrt_nu (M,)
        _I32_1D,  # chi_squared_uix (M,)
    )
)
def kernel_mf_students_t_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    loading_indptr: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    loading_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    systemic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    idiosyncratic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    idio_weight: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    sqrt_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    chi_squared_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> None:
    """Multi-factor Student-t copula JTD loss kernel."""
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        out_row = out[i]

        # Phase 1a: seed the latent accumulator with the idiosyncratic component.
        idio_w = idio_weight[i]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idiosyncratic_uix[i]]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = idio_w * eps_row[s]

        # Phase 1b: accumulate systemic contributions (CSR slice for obligor i).
        start = loading_indptr[i]
        end = loading_indptr[i + 1]
        for j in range(start, end):
            load_j = loading_values[j]
            z_row = latent_factors[systemic_uix[j]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] += load_j * z_row[s]

        # Phase 2: t-scale by sqrt(ν) * inv_sqrt_w, threshold, realize loss.
        sqrt_nu_i = sqrt_nu[i]
        inv_sqrt_w_row = latent_factors[chi_squared_uix[i]]
        threshold_i = thresholds[i]
        loss_scale = lgd[i]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            latent = sqrt_nu_i * inv_sqrt_w_row[s] * out_row[s]
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * loss_scale * (latent < threshold_i)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/sf_copula_gaussian.py
"""
Bare-metal copula JTD loss kernels.

Overview
--------
These kernels realize jump-to-default (JTD) losses for Gaussian and
Student-t copula credit models.

Design goals
------------
- Zero allocation.
- Parallel across obligors.
- Contiguous scenario access.
- Hoist invariant work out of hot loops.
- Scenario-dependent EAD flows directly through loss realization.
- Minimize expensive operations inside the scenario loop.
- Keep the hottest path limited to loads, multiplies, adds, comparisons,
  and stores.

Array layouts
-------------
out             : (M, S) C-contiguous
ead             : (M, S) C-contiguous
latent_factors  : (F, S) C-contiguous

The scenario axis (S) is contiguous everywhere so the inner loop walks
unit-stride memory.

Factor pool conventions
-----------------------
Gaussian kernels
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Rows referenced by:

    sys_uix
    idio_uix

contain standard-normal draws.

Student-t kernels
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Rows referenced by:

    chi_uix

DO NOT contain raw chi-square draws.

Instead they contain:

    inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)

where:

    W ~ χ²

This transformation is performed once during factor generation and stored
directly in the factor pool.

Student-t latent variable
-------------------------
The Student-t latent variable is:

    T = sqrt(ν / W) * Z

where:

    ν = obligor-specific degrees of freedom
    W = scenario-specific chi-square draw
    Z = Gaussian latent variable

The kernel evaluates this as:

    T = sqrt(ν) * (1 / sqrt(W)) * Z

Why precompute 1 / sqrt(W)?
---------------------------
If raw chi-square draws were stored, the kernel would need to compute:

    sqrt(ν) * Z / sqrt(W)

for every obligor-scenario pair.

Large Monte Carlo runs can require hundreds of millions of such
evaluations.

Precomputing:

    1 / sqrt(W)

during factor generation removes a square root from the hottest loop and
reduces the Student-t path to simple multiplication.

Obligor-specific ν
------------------
The degrees-of-freedom parameter is obligor-specific:

    nu.shape == (M,)

Therefore:

    sqrt(ν / W)

cannot be stored directly in the shared factor pool because ν varies by
obligor.

Only:

    1 / sqrt(W)

is precomputed and shared across obligors.

The kernel combines:

    sqrt(ν_i)

with:

    1 / sqrt(W_s)

at runtime.

Loss realization
----------------
For both copulas the realized loss is:

    loss = EAD * LGD * default_indicator

where:

    default_indicator = 1(latent < threshold)

This allows scenario-dependent EAD paths to flow directly through the
simulation without any static loss precomputation.

Performance notes
-----------------
- Parallelized across obligors via Numba prange.
- Scenario axis is contiguous for all arrays.
- All obligor-invariant quantities are hoisted outside the scenario loop.
- Student-t scaling uses precomputed 1/sqrt(W) factors.
- No heap allocation occurs inside either kernel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors
        _F64_2D,  # ead
        _F64_1D,  # lgd
        _F64_1D,  # factor_loading
        _F64_1D,  # thresholds
        _I32_1D,  # sys_uix
        _I32_1D,  # idio_uix
    )
)
def kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (F, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    factor_loading: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    sys_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    idio_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    """
    Single-factor Gaussian copula JTD loss kernel.
    """
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        factor_loading_i = factor_loading[i]
        threshold_i = thresholds[i]
        loss_scale = lgd[i]

        sqrt_1mb = np.sqrt(1.0 - factor_loading_i * factor_loading_i)

        z_row = latent_factors[sys_uix[i]]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idio_uix[i]]

        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            latent = factor_loading_i * z_row[s] + sqrt_1mb * eps_row[s]

            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * loss_scale * (latent < threshold_i)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/copula.py
"""
Bare-metal copula JTD loss kernels.

Overview
--------
These kernels realize jump-to-default (JTD) losses for Gaussian and
Student-t copula credit models.

Design goals
------------
- Zero allocation.
- Parallel across obligors.
- Contiguous scenario access.
- Hoist invariant work out of hot loops.
- Scenario-dependent EAD flows directly through loss realization.
- Minimize expensive operations inside the scenario loop.
- Keep the hottest path limited to loads, multiplies, adds, comparisons,
  and stores.

Array layouts
-------------
out             : (M, S) C-contiguous
ead             : (M, S) C-contiguous
latent_factors  : (F, S) C-contiguous

The scenario axis (S) is contiguous everywhere so the inner loop walks
unit-stride memory.

Factor pool conventions
-----------------------
Gaussian kernels
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Rows referenced by:

    sys_uix
    idio_uix

contain standard-normal draws.

Student-t kernels
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Rows referenced by:

    chi_uix

DO NOT contain raw chi-square draws.

Instead they contain:

    inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)

where:

    W ~ χ²

This transformation is performed once during factor generation and stored
directly in the factor pool.

Student-t latent variable
-------------------------
The Student-t latent variable is:

    T = sqrt(ν / W) * Z

where:

    ν = obligor-specific degrees of freedom
    W = scenario-specific chi-square draw
    Z = Gaussian latent variable

The kernel evaluates this as:

    T = sqrt(ν) * (1 / sqrt(W)) * Z

Why precompute 1 / sqrt(W)?
---------------------------
If raw chi-square draws were stored, the kernel would need to compute:

    sqrt(ν) * Z / sqrt(W)

for every obligor-scenario pair.

Large Monte Carlo runs can require hundreds of millions of such
evaluations.

Precomputing:

    1 / sqrt(W)

during factor generation removes a square root from the hottest loop and
reduces the Student-t path to simple multiplication.

Obligor-specific ν
------------------
The degrees-of-freedom parameter is obligor-specific:

    nu.shape == (M,)

Therefore:

    sqrt(ν / W)

cannot be stored directly in the shared factor pool because ν varies by
obligor.

Only:

    1 / sqrt(W)

is precomputed and shared across obligors.

The kernel combines:

    sqrt(ν_i)

with:

    1 / sqrt(W_s)

at runtime.

Loss realization
----------------
For both copulas the realized loss is:

    loss = EAD * LGD * default_indicator

where:

    default_indicator = 1(latent < threshold)

This allows scenario-dependent EAD paths to flow directly through the
simulation without any static loss precomputation.

Performance notes
-----------------
- Parallelized across obligors via Numba prange.
- Scenario axis is contiguous for all arrays.
- All obligor-invariant quantities are hoisted outside the scenario loop.
- Student-t scaling uses precomputed 1/sqrt(W) factors.
- No heap allocation occurs inside either kernel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors
        _F64_2D,  # ead
        _F64_1D,  # lgd
        _F64_1D,  # factor_loading
        _F64_1D,  # thresholds
        _F64_1D,  # nu
        _I32_1D,  # sys_uix
        _I32_1D,  # idio_uix
        _I32_1D,  # chi_uix
    )
)
def kernel_sf_students_t_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (F, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    factor_loading: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    sqrt_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,) precomputed sqrt(degrees of freedom)
    sys_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    idio_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    chi_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    """
    Single-factor Student-t copula JTD loss kernel.

    Notes
    -----
    The rows referenced by ``chi_uix`` are expected to contain:

        inv_sqrt_w = 1 / sqrt(W)

    and NOT raw chi-square draws.

    This allows the kernel to evaluate:

        sqrt(ν/W) * Z

    as:

        sqrt(ν) * inv_sqrt_w * Z

    eliminating a square-root operation from the hot path.
    """
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        factor_loading_i = factor_loading[i]
        threshold_i = thresholds[i]
        loss_scale = lgd[i]

        sqrt_1m_factor_loading = np.sqrt(1.0 - factor_loading_i * factor_loading_i)

        # Precomputed sqrt(ν) for obligor[i].
        sqrt_nu_i = sqrt_nu[i]

        z_row = latent_factors[sys_uix[i]]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idio_uix[i]]

        # Contains: 1 / sqrt(W)
        inv_sqrt_w_row = latent_factors[chi_uix[i]]

        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            latent_gaussian = (
                factor_loading_i * z_row[s] + sqrt_1m_factor_loading * eps_row[s]
            )

            latent = sqrt_nu_i * inv_sqrt_w_row[s] * latent_gaussian

            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * loss_scale * (latent < threshold_i)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/stress_regime_bernoulli.py


from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D,  # out (N, S)
        _F64_2D,  # latent_factors (N, S)
        _F64_1D,  # stress_regime_indicator (S,) * (stress regime indicator for each scenario s - 1 if stressed, 0 if not)
        _F64_2D,  # ead  (N, S)
        _F64_1D,  # lgd (N,)
        _F64_1D,  # pd (N,)
        _I32_1D,  # uniform_uix (N,)
        _F64_1D,  # stress_pd_multiplier (N,)
    )
)
def kernel_stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N, S)
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N, S)
    stress_regime_indicator: npt.NDArray[
        np.float64
    ],  # (S,) stress_regime_indicator ∈ {0, 1}
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N,)
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N,)
    uniform_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (N,)
    stress_pd_multiplier: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N,)
):
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):  # parallel over obligors
        pd_i = pd[i]
        lgd_i = lgd[i]
        stress_pd_mult_i = stress_pd_multiplier[i]

        u_row = latent_factors[uniform_uix[i]]

        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        stressed_pd_i = min(pd_i * stress_pd_mult_i, 1.0)

        for s in range(s_scenarios):  # scenarios
            effective_pd = stressed_pd_i if stress_regime_indicator[s] > 0.5 else pd_i

            defaulted = u_row[s] < effective_pd  # Bernoulli draw against effective PD

            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i * defaulted
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/models.py
"""
JTD model dispatch identifiers.

`JtdModel` is the credit-layer analogue of `ExposureModel`: a compact integer
identifier used to dispatch into `JTD_EVALUATORS`.

Design principles
-----------------
- Values are contiguous in [0, N-1] → safe for direct indexing.
- Enum contains ONLY implemented models.
- Ordering reflects increasing model complexity:
    deterministic → single-factor → multi-factor
  (NOT production priority).
- The enum is the single source of truth for model IDs.

Any addition MUST:
    1. Append at the end (preserve ordering + contiguity)
    2. Provide a corresponding evaluator
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "JtdModel",
]

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final


class JtdModel(IntEnum):
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Forced default model
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    FORCED_DEFAULT = 0  # scenario-independent worst-case JTD

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # INDEPENDENT DEFAULT MODELS
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI = 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # BETA BINOMIAL MODEL
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------

    BETA_BINOMIAL = 2

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # STRESS REGIME BERNOULLI MODEL
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI = 3

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Single-factor copula models
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN = 4  # single-factor Gaussian copula
    COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T = 5  # single-factor Student-t copula

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Multi-factor copula models
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN = 6  # multi-factor Gaussian copula
    COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T = 7  # multi-factor Student-t copula


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Derived constants (single source of truth = enum)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------

NUM_JTD_MODELS: Final[int] = len(JtdModel)

VALID_JTD_MODEL_NAMES: Final[set[str]] = set(item.name for item in JtdModel)
VALID_JTD_MODEL_IDS: Final[set[int]] = set(item.value for item in JtdModel)

# Multi-factor copula models consume the shared long-format loadings frame.
# Extend as further MF copulas land (e.g. COPULA_MF_T).
MF_COPULA_MODELS: Final[frozenset[JtdModel]] = frozenset(
    {JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN, JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T}
)
MF_COPULA_MODEL_NAMES: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    m.name for m in MF_COPULA_MODELS
)

# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fast-path integer aliases (optional, performance-oriented)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# These exist to avoid enum dereferencing in tight loops / NumPy code.
# Safe because they derive directly from the enum.

# Forced default model
FORCED_DEFAULT_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT)

# Independent bernoulli model
INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI)

# Beta binomial model
BETA_BINOMIAL_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL)

# Stress regime bernoulli model
STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI)

# Single-factor copula models
COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN)
COPULA_SF_T_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T)

# Multi-factor copula models
COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN)
COPULA_MF_T_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T)
```
</file>
