<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\registry.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/registry.py
"""
JTD model registry — the active "vending machine" for model wiring.

A single `JtdModelSpec` per model captures everything needed to wire that model:
its contract, builder, compiled block type, evaluator (by lazy import
coordinates), and the attribute names of its compiled-portfolio slot and ingest
bundle field. `JTD_MODEL_SPECS` is the ONE ordered list; every dispatch table is
derived from it:

    * `evaluator_table()`   -> engine's model-id -> evaluator dispatch
    * `model_field_order()` -> bundle's per-model frame slots
    * `model_contracts()`   -> ingest contracts by model

Layering note (why evaluators are referenced by import coordinates, not imported
at module top): the ingest edge (compile / bundle / contracts) is Numba-free by
design — strings die at ingest, kernels live in the core. Evaluators import
kernels, which import Numba. If the registry imported evaluators eagerly, then
routing the ingest path through the registry would drag Numba into the ingest
edge. So evaluators are resolved lazily in `evaluator_table()` (called at engine
import time, where Numba is required anyway), keeping the registry itself — and
therefore `compile.py` and `bundle.py` — Numba-free.

Adding a model: write its block/kernel/evaluator/builder, append one
`JtdModelSpec`, append one `JtdModel` enum value. The import-time guards reject
any drift between the enum, the specs, and the compiled/bundle slot names.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib
from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass
from types import MappingProxyType
from typing import Any, Final

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.beta_binomial import BetaBinomialJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.forced_default import ForcedDefaultJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.independent_bernoulli import (
    IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_gaussian import MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.mf_copula_students_t import (
    MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_gaussian import SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.sf_copula_students_t import SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.stress_regime_bernoulli import (
    StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.type_alias import JtdBlockTypeUnion
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.base import JtdEvaluator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.beta_binomial import build_beta_binomial_block
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.forced_default import (
    build_forced_default_block,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.independent_bernoulli import (
    build_independent_bernoulli_block,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.mf_gaussian import build_mf_gaussian_block
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.mf_students_t import (
    build_mf_students_t_block,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.sf_gaussian import build_sf_gaussian_block
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.sf_students_t import build_sf_students_t_block
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.builders.stress_regime_bernoulli import (
    build_stress_regime_bernoulli_block,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.contracts import JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS, JtdModelContract
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import NUM_JTD_MODELS, JtdModel

_EVALUATOR_PKG: Final[str] = "riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class JtdModelSpec:
    """Everything needed to wire one JTD model into the pipeline.

    The evaluator is referenced by ``(evaluator_module, evaluator_attr)`` and
    resolved lazily (see module docstring); the builder is held directly because
    builders are Numba-free. `needs_factor_uix` / `needs_mf_loadings` let the
    registry dispatch the non-uniform builder signatures positionally.
    """

    model: JtdModel
    contract: JtdModelContract
    block_type: type[_JtdBlock]
    builder: Callable[..., JtdBlockTypeUnion]
    evaluator_module: str  # module under riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators
    evaluator_attr: str  # evaluator callable name within that module
    compiled_slot: str  # attribute on CompiledJtdPortfolio
    bundle_field: str  # attribute on JtdIngestBundle
    needs_factor_uix: bool  # builder takes factor_name_to_uix
    needs_mf_loadings: bool  # builder takes the shared mf_loadings frame
    # Name of this model's per-model semantic validator in ingest/validate.py,
    # resolved there (validators co-locate with the predicates they call); None
    # if the model needs no semantic checks beyond the contract-driven defaults.
    semantic_validator_attr: str | None = None

    def resolve_evaluator(self) -> JtdEvaluator[Any]:
        """Import and return the evaluator (pulls Numba via the kernel)."""
        module = importlib.import_module(f"{_EVALUATOR_PKG}.{self.evaluator_module}")
        evaluator = getattr(module, self.evaluator_attr)
        if not isinstance(evaluator, JtdEvaluator):
            raise TypeError(
                f"{self.model.name}: {self.evaluator_module}.{self.evaluator_attr} "
                f"does not satisfy the JtdEvaluator protocol: {evaluator!r}"
            )
        return evaluator


# The ONE ordered registration list. Order is JtdModel band order.
JTD_MODEL_SPECS: Final[tuple[JtdModelSpec, ...]] = (
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT],
        block_type=ForcedDefaultJtdBlock,
        builder=build_forced_default_block,
        evaluator_module="forced_default",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_forced_default_jtd",
        compiled_slot="forced_default_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="forced_default_df",
        needs_factor_uix=False,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI],
        block_type=IndependentBernoulliJtdBlock,
        builder=build_independent_bernoulli_block,
        evaluator_module="independent_bernoulli",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_independent_bernoulli_jtd",
        compiled_slot="independent_bernoulli_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="independent_bernoulli_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL],
        block_type=BetaBinomialJtdBlock,
        builder=build_beta_binomial_block,
        evaluator_module="beta_binomial",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_beta_binomial_jtd",
        compiled_slot="beta_binomial_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="beta_binomial_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI],
        block_type=StressRegimeBernoulliJtdBlock,
        builder=build_stress_regime_bernoulli_block,
        evaluator_module="stress_regime_bernoulli",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd",
        compiled_slot="stress_regime_bernoulli_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="stress_regime_bernoulli_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
        semantic_validator_attr="_semantic_stress",
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN],
        block_type=SfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
        builder=build_sf_gaussian_block,
        evaluator_module="sf_copula_gaussian",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd",
        compiled_slot="sf_gaussian_copula_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="sf_gaussian_copula_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
        semantic_validator_attr="_semantic_sf_gaussian",
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T],
        block_type=SfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock,
        builder=build_sf_students_t_block,
        evaluator_module="sf_copula_students_t",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_sf_students_t_copula_jtd",
        compiled_slot="sf_students_t_copula_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="sf_students_t_copula_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=False,
        semantic_validator_attr="_semantic_sf_students_t",
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN],
        block_type=MfGaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
        builder=build_mf_gaussian_block,
        evaluator_module="mf_copula_gaussian",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_mf_gaussian_copula_jtd",
        compiled_slot="mf_gaussian_copula_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="mf_gaussian_copula_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=True,
        semantic_validator_attr="_semantic_mf_gaussian",
    ),
    JtdModelSpec(
        model=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T,
        contract=JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS[JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T],
        block_type=MfStudentsTCopulaJtdBlock,
        builder=build_mf_students_t_block,
        evaluator_module="mf_copula_students_t",
        evaluator_attr="evaluate_mf_students_t_copula_jtd",
        compiled_slot="mf_students_t_copula_jtd_block",
        bundle_field="mf_students_t_copula_df",
        needs_factor_uix=True,
        needs_mf_loadings=True,
        semantic_validator_attr="_semantic_mf_students_t",
    ),
)

JTD_MODEL_REGISTRY: Final[MappingProxyType[JtdModel, JtdModelSpec]] = MappingProxyType(
    {spec.model: spec for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS}
)


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Import-time validation (all Numba-free — evaluators are NOT resolved here).
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
if len(JTD_MODEL_REGISTRY) != len(JTD_MODEL_SPECS):
    raise RuntimeError("JTD_MODEL_SPECS contains duplicate models.")

_missing = [m.name for m in JtdModel if m not in JTD_MODEL_REGISTRY]
if _missing:
    raise RuntimeError(f"JTD models with no registry spec: {_missing}.")

if tuple(spec.model for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS) != tuple(JtdModel):
    raise RuntimeError("JTD_MODEL_SPECS order must match JtdModel band order exactly.")

for _spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS:
    if _spec.contract.model is not _spec.model:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"{_spec.model.name}: spec.contract is for {_spec.contract.model.name}."
        )
# Slot/field name existence is validated by the consumers that own those
# dataclasses (compiled.py at construction, bundle.py at import), avoiding a
# registry -> compiled/bundle import cycle.


def evaluator_table() -> tuple[JtdEvaluator[Any], ...]:
    """Model-id-indexed evaluator dispatch — the engine's `JTD_EVALUATORS`.

    Resolves (and Numba-imports) every evaluator. Completeness + contiguity are
    guaranteed at import, so entry ``i`` is the evaluator for ``JtdModel(i)``.
    """
    table: list[JtdEvaluator[Any] | None] = [None] * NUM_JTD_MODELS
    for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS:
        table[int(spec.model)] = spec.resolve_evaluator()
    return tuple(table)  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # completeness validated above


def model_field_order() -> tuple[tuple[JtdModel, str], ...]:
    """Per-model bundle frame slots (single source for bundle._MODEL_FIELD_ORDER)."""
    return tuple((spec.model, spec.bundle_field) for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS)


def model_contracts() -> MappingProxyType[JtdModel, JtdModelContract]:
    """Ingest contracts by model (single source for JTD_MODEL_CONTRACTS)."""
    return MappingProxyType({spec.model: spec.contract for spec in JTD_MODEL_SPECS})
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/results.py
"""Resolved JTD-loss result container."""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "JtdLossResult",
    "StreamingJtdResult",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class JtdLossResult:
    """
    Jump-to-default loss, resolved per obligor.

    Row i of `loss` is the obligor identified by `uid[i]`.
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,)    obligor identifier
    loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N, S)  default loss per obligor, per scenario


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class StreamingJtdResult:
    """
    Accumulated JTD loss from a streaming run — the per-scenario PORTFOLIO total,
    never the per-obligor ``(N, S)`` matrix.

    `portfolio_loss` is the ``(S,)`` total the aggregation layer consumes
    directly. `model_loss`, when collected, is ``(n_models, S)`` with rows in
    `model_order` (JtdModel ids); each row is that model's per-scenario loss, and
    the rows sum to `portfolio_loss`. Per-obligor attribution is intentionally not
    retained — that is the memory trade the streaming path makes.
    """

    portfolio_loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (S,)
    total_obligors: int
    n_scenarios: int
    model_loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64] | None = None  # (n_models, S) | None
    model_order: tuple[int, ...] = ()  # JtdModel ids aligned to model_loss rows
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\streaming.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/streaming.py
"""
Streaming (batched) JTD execution.

`run_jtd` materialises the full per-obligor ``(N, S)`` loss; at institutional
obligor x scenario counts that — and the full ``(F, S)`` factor matrix feeding it
— is the memory ceiling. `run_jtd_streaming` removes both: it drives the loop off
the factor generator's scenario batches and, per batch, runs the SAME evaluator
dispatch into one reused ``(N, batch)`` scratch buffer, then collapses that batch
to its per-scenario totals. The only state that persists across batches is the
``(S,)`` portfolio loss vector (plus, optionally, an ``(n_models, S)`` per-model
breakdown) — never ``(N, S)`` or ``(F, S)``.

That ``(S,)`` total is exactly what `riskquant.credit.aggregate` was built to
consume, so the streaming path produces a `StreamingJtdResult` that the
aggregation layer turns into the loss distribution and risk metrics.

The per-scenario *portfolio* vector is the largest object kept (S floats), which
is cheap even at very large S; what streaming gives up is per-obligor attribution
(e.g. top contributors in the VaR scenario), since obligor-level loss is never
retained. Use `run_jtd` when that attribution is needed and the portfolio fits.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "run_jtd_streaming",
    "run_jtd_streaming_from_generator",
    "constant_ead_provider",
]

from collections.abc import Callable, Iterable
from typing import Any, Final

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)
from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.base import JtdEvaluator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.registry import evaluator_table
from riskquant.credit.jtd.results import StreamingJtdResult

JTD_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[JtdEvaluator[Any], ...]] = evaluator_table()

# (scenario_offset, batch_size) -> (n_obligors, batch_size) EAD, rows aligned to
# jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids. The caller owns EAD generation so it, too, streams
# per batch rather than materialising (N, S).
EadProvider = Callable[[int, int], npt.NDArray[np.float64]]


def run_jtd_streaming(
    *,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    factor_batches: Iterable[CreditFactorBatch],
    ead_provider: EadProvider,
    total_scenarios: int,
    collect_by_model: bool = False,
) -> StreamingJtdResult:
    """
    Resolve JTD loss over scenario batches, accumulating the portfolio total.

    Parameters
    ----------
    jtd_portfolio
        Compiled obligor blocks; band order / `obligor_uids` defines the EAD row
        order the `ead_provider` must match.
    factor_batches
        Scenario batches from a credit-factor generator
        (`generator.iter_batches(batch_size=...)`). Each batch carries
        ``(batch, F)`` scenario-major latent draws and its scenario offset; the
        batches must partition ``[0, total_scenarios)``.
    ead_provider
        ``(scenario_offset, batch_size) -> (n_obligors, batch_size)`` EAD for that
        scenario slice, rows aligned to `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`.
    total_scenarios
        Total scenario count S; sizes the persistent accumulator(s).
    collect_by_model
        If True, also accumulate an ``(n_models, S)`` per-model breakdown (rows
        sum to the portfolio total). Costs one ``(S,)`` row per distinct model.
    """
    n_obligors = jtd_portfolio.total_obligors
    blocks = jtd_portfolio.blocks
    offsets = jtd_portfolio.block_offsets
    n_blocks = jtd_portfolio.total_blocks
    evaluators_table = JTD_EVALUATORS

    # Persistent accumulators: (S,) portfolio total, written once per scenario as
    # the batches partition the scenario axis.
    portfolio_loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(total_scenarios, np.float64)

    model_loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64] | None = None
    model_order: tuple[int, ...] = ()
    block_model_row: list[int] = []
    if collect_by_model:
        # One row per block (one block per model in band order).
        model_order = tuple(int(blocks[i].JTD_MODEL_ID) for i in range(n_blocks))
        block_model_row = list(range(n_blocks))
        model_loss = np.empty((n_blocks, total_scenarios), np.float64)

    scratch: npt.NDArray[np.float64] | None = None  # (N, batch) reused across batches

    for batch in factor_batches:
        offset = batch.scenario_offset
        batch_size = batch.latent_factors.shape[0]
        end_s = offset + batch_size

        # (batch, F) scenario-major -> (F, batch) factor-major for the kernels.
        factors = CreditFactorMatrix(
            latent_factors=np.ascontiguousarray(batch.latent_factors.T)
        )
        ead = ead_provider(offset, batch_size)
        if ead.shape != (n_obligors, batch_size):
            raise ValueError(
                f"ead_provider returned {ead.shape}, expected "
                f"{(n_obligors, batch_size)} at scenario offset {offset}."
            )
        # Kernels are typed for C-contiguous EAD (float64[:, ::1]); a provider
        # returning a strided view (e.g. a column slice) would otherwise fail
        # kernel dispatch. No-op when the provider already returns C-contiguous.
        ead = np.ascontiguousarray(ead)

        if scratch is None or scratch.shape[1] != batch_size:
            scratch = np.empty((n_obligors, batch_size), dtype=np.float64, order="C")

        for i in range(n_blocks):
            block = blocks[i]
            start = int(offsets[i])
            stop = start + block.num_obligors
            evaluators_table[block.JTD_MODEL_ID](
                block=block,
                ead=ead[start:stop, :],
                credit_factors=factors,
                out=scratch[start:stop, :],
            )

        # Collapse this batch to per-scenario totals into the persistent (S,).
        portfolio_loss[offset:end_s] = scratch.sum(axis=0)
        if model_loss is not None:
            for i in range(n_blocks):
                start = int(offsets[i])
                stop = start + blocks[i].num_obligors
                model_loss[block_model_row[i], offset:end_s] = scratch[
                    start:stop, :
                ].sum(axis=0)

    return StreamingJtdResult(
        portfolio_loss=portfolio_loss,
        total_obligors=n_obligors,
        n_scenarios=total_scenarios,
        model_loss=model_loss,
        model_order=model_order,
    )


def run_jtd_streaming_from_generator(
    *,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    factor_generator: CreditFactorGenerator,
    ead_provider: EadProvider,
    batch_size: int,
    collect_by_model: bool = False,
) -> StreamingJtdResult:
    """Drive `run_jtd_streaming` straight off a credit-factor generator.

    Derives the scenario batches (`generator.iter_batches(batch_size=...)`) and the
    total scenario count from the generator, so the caller supplies only the
    portfolio, an EAD provider, and a batch size — no hand-wiring of the batch
    stream or `total_scenarios`.
    """
    return run_jtd_streaming(
        jtd_portfolio=jtd_portfolio,
        factor_batches=factor_generator.iter_batches(batch_size=batch_size),
        ead_provider=ead_provider,
        total_scenarios=factor_generator.total_scenarios,
        collect_by_model=collect_by_model,
    )


def constant_ead_provider(
    ead_by_obligor: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> EadProvider:
    """Build an `EadProvider` for scenario-INDEPENDENT EAD.

    `ead_by_obligor` is the ``(N,)`` per-obligor exposure (rows aligned to
    `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`); the returned provider broadcasts it across each
    batch's scenarios as a read-only ``(N, batch)`` view — zero per-batch
    allocation. Use this when EAD does not vary by scenario; supply your own
    provider when it does (e.g. exposure simulated jointly with the factors).
    """
    base = np.ascontiguousarray(ead_by_obligor, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1, 1)

    def _provider(scenario_offset: int, batch_size: int) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        return np.broadcast_to(base, (base.shape[0], batch_size))

    return _provider
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/cross_master/compiled.py
"""
The validated credit master aggregate.

`CompiledCreditMasters` is the single value that represents "a set of credit
masters that are mutually consistent". It does NOT compile anything — each master
is compiled independently by its own ingest edge (`compile_obligor_master`,
`compile_factor_universe`, `compile_netting_set_master`) and handed in already
built, so every master remains separately inspectable. What this type adds is the
cross-master invariant: it runs `validate_cross_masters` at construction and
fails fast, so an inconsistent set of masters cannot be represented at all.

This is deliberately NOT a pipeline/orchestrator. It owns exactly one
responsibility — the layered foreign-key relationships between already-compiled
masters — and leaves master compilation and engine construction explicit in the
caller. Downstream consumers (the JTD compile, a future engine builder) bind
against this aggregate instead of threading the individual masters and trusting
the caller to have remembered the cross-checks.

The netting-set master is optional: a pure default/loss workflow (obligors +
factor universe + externally supplied EADs) needs no netting sets, and the only
cross-master invariant defined today — the netting-set -> obligor foreign key —
simply does not apply when it is absent.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledCreditMasters",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.validate import validate_cross_masters
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledCreditMasters:
    """
    A mutually-consistent set of compiled credit masters.

    Construction validates every defined cross-master invariant and raises on the
    first violation, so holding an instance is proof the masters agree. The
    constituent masters stay directly accessible as fields for inspection and for
    passing on to the model-compilation edges.

    Parameters
    ----------
    obligor_master
        Compiled obligor master; the single source of truth for PD/LGD and the
        obligor universe every other master and model resolves against.
    factor_universe
        Compiled credit factor-universe master; the resolution target for factor
        names at JTD compile time.
    netting_set_master
        Optional compiled netting-set master. When supplied, every netting set's
        owning obligor must exist on the obligor master.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any cross-master invariant fails (e.g. a netting set references an
        obligor uid absent from the obligor master).
    """

    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit
    factor_universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # Cross-master invariants. Only the netting-set -> obligor foreign key is
        # defined today; it is vacuous (and skipped) when no netting-set master is
        # present. Future layered checks (rating/sector taxonomy joins, parent
        # closure) extend `validate_cross_masters` and are picked up here for free.
        if self.netting_set_master is not None:
            validate_cross_masters(
                obligor_master=self.obligor_master,
                netting_set_master=self.netting_set_master,
            )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/cross_master/validate.py
"""
Layered cross-master validators for the credit package.

Single-master validators (`obligors/validate.py`, `netting_set/validate.py`)
enforce intra-table invariants — dtype, range, uniqueness, sortedness,
self-references. They are intentionally unaware of other masters: an
obligor master that imports the netting-set master, or vice versa, would
couple two edges that should compile independently.

Layered invariants — facts that hold *across* tables — live here. The
canonical layered invariant is the foreign-key relationship from
`CompiledNettingSetMaster.obligor_uid` into `CompiledObligorCredit.uid`:
every netting-set row must point at an obligor that exists on the master.

This package is consumed by the top-level pipeline orchestrator (a future
`compile_credit_pipeline(...)` entry point) and by any caller that holds
both masters and wants to fail fast before they reach a downstream join.
Pure vectorized predicates on already-compiled SoA — no row loops, no
pandas, no string lookups.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_membership
from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit

_PREVIEW: Final[int] = 10


def validate_netting_set_obligor_uids_on_master(
    *,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> None:
    """
    Check that every netting set's owning obligor exists on the obligor master.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any `netting_set_master.obligor_uid` value is absent from
        `obligor_master.uid`. The error message previews up to ten
        offending (ns_uid, obligor_uid) pairs.
    """
    matched = searchsorted_membership(
        universe=obligor_master.uid,
        raw=netting_set_master.obligor_uid,
    )
    if matched.all():
        return

    bad_pairs = list(
        zip(
            netting_set_master.uid[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist(),
            netting_set_master.obligor_uid[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist(),
            strict=True,
        )
    )
    n_bad = int((~matched).sum())
    raise ValueError(
        f"cross_master: {n_bad} netting set(s) reference obligor uid(s) "
        f"absent from the obligor master. First {len(bad_pairs)} "
        f"(ns_uid, obligor_uid) pair(s): {bad_pairs}"
    )


def validate_cross_masters(
    *,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> None:
    """
    Single-call entry point: every cross-master invariant, fail-fast.

    Today: the netting-set -> obligor foreign key. Future layered checks
    (rating taxonomy join, sector taxonomy join, parent-obligor closure
    against an external corporate hierarchy) land here too.
    """
    validate_netting_set_obligor_uids_on_master(
        netting_set_master=netting_set_master,
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/enums/credit_factor_kind.py
"""
Credit factor taxonomy.

System-defined constants — not user configuration.

Users reference these string values in the credit factor universe
authoring table. The loader converts the strings into the corresponding
enum values during ingest.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CreditFactorKind",
]

from enum import IntEnum


class CreditFactorKind(IntEnum):
    SYSTEMIC = 0
    IDIOSYNCRATIC = 1
    CHI = 2
    UNIFORM = 3
    STRESS_INDICATOR = 4
    BETA_PD = 5


VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_NAMES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    e.name for e in CreditFactorKind
)
VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_IDS: frozenset[int] = frozenset(
    e.value for e in CreditFactorKind
)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/factor_universe/compiled.py
"""
Compiled credit-factor universe data.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledCreditFactorUniverse",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.masters.enums.credit_factor_kind import CreditFactorKind


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledCreditFactorUniverse:
    """
    Canonical credit-factor universe.

    UIX is implicit:

        factor_ids[i]
        factor_names[i]
        factor_kind[i]
        factor_chi_nu[i]
        factor_beta_alpha[i]
        factor_beta_beta[i]

    all describe factor UIX=i.

    Example
    -------
    The universe is stored in structure-of-arrays form. Conceptually it is
    equivalent to the following table:

    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | UIX | Factor ID | Factor Name       | Factor Kind      | Nu     | Alpha | Beta |
    +=====+===========+===================+==================+========+=======+======+
    | 0   | 1001      | GLOBAL_CREDIT     | SYSTEMIC         |   -    |   -   |   -  |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | 1   | 1002      | IDIO_OBLIGOR_001  | IDIOSYNCRATIC    |   -    |   -   |   -  |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | 2   | 1003      | CHI_GLOBAL        | CHI              |  4.0   |   -   |   -  |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | 3   | 1004      | U_OBLIGOR_001     | UNIFORM          |   -    |   -   |   -  |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | 4   | 1005      | RECESSION_FLAG    | STRESS_INDICATOR |   -    |   -   |   -  |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+
    | 5   | 1006      | PD_ENVIRONMENT    | BETA_PD          |   -    | 2.0   | 18.0 |
    +-----+-----------+-------------------+------------------+--------+-------+------+

    which is represented internally as:

        factor_ids
            = [1001, 1002, 1003, 1004, 1005, 1006]

        factor_names
            = [
                "GLOBAL_CREDIT",
                "IDIO_OBLIGOR_001",
                "CHI_GLOBAL",
                "U_OBLIGOR_001",
                "RECESSION_FLAG",
                "BETA_PD_ENVIRONMENT",
            ]

        factor_kind
            = [
                SYSTEMIC,
                IDIOSYNCRATIC,
                CHI,
                UNIFORM,
                STRESS_INDICATOR,
                BETA_PD,
            ]

    UIX is therefore simply the row coordinate into the factor matrix:

        credit_factor_matrix[0, :]  -> GLOBAL_CREDIT
        credit_factor_matrix[2, :]  -> CHI_GLOBAL
        credit_factor_matrix[5, :]  -> BETA_PD_ENVIRONMENT

    `factor_ids` is the stable identity axis (sorted ascending, unique); UIX is
    the engine-owned coordinate — the row index into the `(F, S)`
    `CreditFactorMatrix`.

    `factor_chi_nu` carries nu > 0 for CHI factors and the `NO_CHI_NU`
    sentinel (NOT NaN) elsewhere.

    `factor_beta_alpha` and `factor_beta_beta` carry the Beta(alpha, beta)
    shape parameters (both > 0) for BETA_PD factors and the
    `NO_BETA_PARAM` sentinel elsewhere.

    The derived accessors are plain ``property``: a slotted dataclass has no
    ``__dict__`` for ``functools.cached_property`` to write into, and these are
    setup-time projections (generator config, ingest maps), never hot-path.
    """

    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    factor_names: npt.NDArray[np.str_]
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    factor_chi_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    factor_beta_alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    factor_beta_beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    @property
    def n_factors(self) -> int:
        return int(self.factor_ids.size)

    @property
    def factor_id_to_uix(self) -> dict[int, int]:
        return {int(factor_id): uix for uix, factor_id in enumerate(self.factor_ids)}

    @property
    def factor_name_to_uix(self) -> dict[str, int]:
        return {str(name): uix for uix, name in enumerate(self.factor_names)}

    @property
    def chi_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.CHI).astype(np.int32)

    @property
    def systemic_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.SYSTEMIC).astype(
            np.int32
        )

    @property
    def idiosyncratic_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(
            self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.IDIOSYNCRATIC
        ).astype(np.int32)

    @property
    def uniform_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.UNIFORM).astype(
            np.int32
        )

    @property
    def stress_indicator_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(
            self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.STRESS_INDICATOR
        ).astype(np.int32)

    @property
    def chi_nu_by_uix(self) -> dict[int, float]:
        return {int(uix): float(self.factor_chi_nu[uix]) for uix in self.chi_uix}

    @property
    def chi_nu_vector(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        """
        nu values aligned to chi_uix.
        """
        return np.ascontiguousarray(
            self.factor_chi_nu[self.chi_uix],
            dtype=np.float64,
        )

    @property
    def beta_pd_uix(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        return np.flatnonzero(self.factor_kind == CreditFactorKind.BETA_PD).astype(
            np.int32
        )

    @property
    def beta_alpha(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        """alpha values aligned to beta_pd_uix."""
        return np.ascontiguousarray(
            self.factor_beta_alpha[self.beta_pd_uix],
            dtype=np.float64,
        )

    @property
    def beta_beta(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        """beta values aligned to beta_pd_uix."""
        return np.ascontiguousarray(
            self.factor_beta_beta[self.beta_pd_uix],
            dtype=np.float64,
        )

    @property
    def beta_params_by_uix(self) -> dict[int, tuple[float, float]]:
        return {
            int(uix): (
                float(self.factor_beta_alpha[uix]),
                float(self.factor_beta_beta[uix]),
            )
            for uix in self.beta_pd_uix
        }

    def get_uix_from_factor_id(self, factor_id: int) -> int:
        return self.factor_id_to_uix[factor_id]

    def get_uix_from_factor_name(self, factor_name: str) -> int:
        return self.factor_name_to_uix[factor_name]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\masters\factor_universe\ingest.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/factor_universe/ingest.py
"""
Credit factor master DataFrame -> `CompiledCreditFactorUniverse`.

The factor edge: a human-facing authoring table is cast to strict dtypes,
sorted by `factor_id` ascending, validated as one self-consistent universe,
and collapsed into a single immutable SoA container.

`factor_id` ascending sorted fixes the identity axis; UIX is the row position
after the sort and is owned by the engine. The same permutation is applied to
every column so the SoA stays aligned row-wise.

Authoring table
---------------
One row per factor. Columns:
    factor_id    : int   — stable identity, unique. Sorted ascending -> UIX.
    factor_name  : str   — human identity obligor ingest references, unique.
    factor_kind  : str   — a `CreditFactorKind` name (SYSTEMIC, CHI, ...).
    nu           : float — OPTIONAL column. Required and > 0 for CHI rows;
                           blank / NA elsewhere. Mapped to the NO_CHI_NU
                           sentinel for non-CHI factors.
    alpha, beta  : float — OPTIONAL columns. Required and > 0 for BETA_PD rows;
                           blank / NA elsewhere. Mapped to the NO_BETA_PARAM
                           sentinel for non-BETA_PD factors.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import warnings

__all__ = [
    "compile_credit_factor_universe",
]

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import NO_BETA_PARAM, NO_CHI_NU
from riskquant.credit.masters.enums.credit_factor_kind import (
    VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_NAMES,
    CreditFactorKind,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.validate import (
    validate_credit_factor_universe_arrays,
)

_REQUIRED_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "factor_id",
    "factor_name",
    "factor_kind",
)
_PREVIEW = 10

# Authored kind string -> int8 id (single source of truth = the enum).
_KIND_NAME_TO_ID: dict[str, np.int8] = {
    k.name: np.int8(k.value) for k in CreditFactorKind
}


def _check_required_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_credit_factor_universe: missing required column(s) {missing}."
        )


def _i32(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32))


def _names(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.str_]:
    # Force a fixed-width unicode SoA column; .astype(str) first so any
    # non-string authoring (e.g. ints) fails visibly rather than boxing.
    return np.ascontiguousarray(
        np.asarray(df["factor_name"].astype(str).to_numpy(), dtype=np.str_)
    )


def _kind_ids(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    """Map authored kind strings to int8 ids; fail-fast on unknown names."""
    raw = df["factor_kind"].astype(str).to_numpy()
    unknown = sorted(set(raw.tolist()) - VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_NAMES)
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_credit_factor_universe: unknown factor_kind(s) "
            f"{unknown[:_PREVIEW]}. Valid: {sorted(VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_NAMES)}."
        )
    return np.ascontiguousarray(
        np.array([_KIND_NAME_TO_ID[s] for s in raw], dtype=np.int8)
    )


def _chi_nu(
    df: pd.DataFrame, kind_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Map genuinely-missing nu to the NO_CHI_NU sentinel, preserving every
    authored value.

    Only NaN (truly absent) becomes the sentinel. A value authored on a non-CHI
    row is kept, NOT silently discarded, so `validate_chi_nu` can reject it as a
    likely kind-mislabel. A missing nu on a CHI row becomes the sentinel and is
    likewise rejected downstream (the sentinel is not > 0), with the offending
    factor id reported.
    """
    n = kind_ids.shape[0]
    if "nu" in df.columns:
        nu_raw = df["nu"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)  # NA -> NaN
    else:
        nu_raw = np.full(n, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)

    return np.ascontiguousarray(np.where(np.isnan(nu_raw), NO_CHI_NU, nu_raw))


def _require_param_columns_for_kinds(
    df: pd.DataFrame, factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
) -> None:
    """Fail fast if a kind that needs a param column is present but the column
    is absent. Per-row value validity is still enforced downstream by
    `validate_chi_nu` / `validate_beta_params`; this just turns a "forgot the
    column entirely" schema mistake into a crisp, specific error."""
    if np.any(factor_kind == np.int8(CreditFactorKind.CHI)) and "nu" not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            "compile_credit_factor_universe: universe has CHI factor(s) but no "
            "'nu' column. CHI factors require their degrees of freedom."
        )
    if np.any(factor_kind == np.int8(CreditFactorKind.BETA_PD)):
        missing = [c for c in ("alpha", "beta") if c not in df.columns]
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                "compile_credit_factor_universe: universe has BETA_PD factor(s) "
                f"but no {missing} column(s). BETA_PD factors require their Beta "
                "shape parameters."
            )


def _beta_param(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str, n: int) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Map a genuinely-missing Beta shape parameter to the NO_BETA_PARAM sentinel,
    preserving every authored value.

    Only NaN (truly absent) becomes the sentinel. A value authored on a
    non-BETA_PD row is kept, NOT silently discarded, so `validate_beta_params`
    can reject it as a likely kind-mislabel; a missing value on a BETA_PD row
    becomes the sentinel and is likewise rejected downstream.
    """
    if col in df.columns:
        raw = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)  # NA -> NaN
    else:
        raw = np.full(n, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)

    return np.ascontiguousarray(np.where(np.isnan(raw), NO_BETA_PARAM, raw))


def compile_credit_factor_universe(
    factor_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> CompiledCreditFactorUniverse:
    """
    Compile a validated credit factor-universe frame into
    `CompiledCreditFactorUniverse`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    factor_df
        One row per factor. Required columns: factor_id, factor_name,
        factor_kind. Optional `nu` column carries the chi degrees of freedom
        (required for CHI rows, blank otherwise).

    Returns
    -------
    CompiledCreditFactorUniverse
        `factor_ids` sorted ascending; all other columns reordered by the same
        permutation. UIX == row position.
    """
    if factor_df.empty:
        raise ValueError(
            "compile_credit_factor_universe: cannot compile an empty frame."
        )

    _check_required_columns(factor_df)

    # One stable permutation by the identity axis applied to every column.
    df = factor_df.sort_values("factor_id", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)

    factor_ids = _i32(df, "factor_id")
    factor_names = _names(df)
    factor_kind = _kind_ids(df)

    # Param columns stay OPTIONAL, but a column is REQUIRED the moment a factor
    # of its kind appears: a CHI factor without a 'nu' column, or a BETA_PD
    # factor without 'alpha'/'beta', is a schema mistake worth a crisp error
    # here rather than the value-level "missing/non-positive" downstream. The
    # structural requirement follows from the content, so simple universes
    # (no chi / no beta) need carry no param columns at all.
    _require_param_columns_for_kinds(df, factor_kind)

    factor_chi_nu = _chi_nu(df, factor_kind)
    n = factor_kind.shape[0]
    factor_beta_alpha = _beta_param(df, "alpha", n)
    factor_beta_beta = _beta_param(df, "beta", n)

    validate_credit_factor_universe_arrays(
        factor_ids=factor_ids,
        factor_names=factor_names,
        factor_kind=factor_kind,
        factor_chi_nu=factor_chi_nu,
        factor_beta_alpha=factor_beta_alpha,
        factor_beta_beta=factor_beta_beta,
    )

    return CompiledCreditFactorUniverse(
        factor_ids=factor_ids,
        factor_names=factor_names,
        factor_kind=factor_kind,
        factor_chi_nu=factor_chi_nu,
        factor_beta_alpha=factor_beta_alpha,
        factor_beta_beta=factor_beta_beta,
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\masters\factor_universe\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/factor_universe/validate.py
"""
Credit-factor master validators.

Pure vectorized predicates on the post-ingest SoA arrays. Each function
either passes silently or raises `ValueError` with the (bounded) list of
offending factor ids. No row loops, no pandas, no string lookups beyond the
identity axis — these run once at ingest time on arrays already cast to their
compiled dtypes.

Split per invariant (id uniqueness, name uniqueness, kind domain, chi/nu
consistency) so any concrete failure points at exactly one rule.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import NO_BETA_PARAM, NO_CHI_NU
from riskquant.credit.masters.enums.credit_factor_kind import (
    VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_IDS,
    CreditFactorKind,
)

_PREVIEW = 10


def _bad_ids(
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32], mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]
) -> list[int]:
    return factor_ids[mask][:_PREVIEW].tolist()


def validate_lengths_aligned(
    *,
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    factor_chi_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_beta_alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_beta_beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """All SoA columns share one length F > 0."""
    n = factor_ids.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        raise ValueError("credit factor universe: empty factor table.")
    lengths = {
        "factor_names": factor_names.shape[0],
        "factor_kind": factor_kind.shape[0],
        "factor_chi_nu": factor_chi_nu.shape[0],
        "factor_beta_alpha": factor_beta_alpha.shape[0],
        "factor_beta_beta": factor_beta_beta.shape[0],
    }
    misaligned = {k: v for k, v in lengths.items() if v != n}
    if misaligned:
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: column length mismatch vs factor_ids "
            f"({n}): {misaligned}."
        )


def validate_factor_id_sorted_unique(factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]) -> None:
    """`factor_id` must be strictly ascending — uniqueness + sorted in one check."""
    if not (np.diff(factor_ids) > 0).all():
        unique, counts = np.unique(factor_ids, return_counts=True)
        dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        if dups:
            raise ValueError(f"credit factor universe: duplicate factor_id(s) {dups}.")
        raise ValueError("credit factor universe: factor_id is not sorted ascending.")


def validate_factor_names(
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
) -> None:
    """Factor names must be non-empty and unique."""
    empty = np.char.str_len(factor_names) == 0
    if empty.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: empty factor_name for factor_id(s) "
            f"{_bad_ids(factor_ids, empty)}."
        )
    unique, counts = np.unique(factor_names, return_counts=True)
    dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
    if dups:
        raise ValueError(f"credit factor universe: duplicate factor_name(s) {dups}.")


def validate_factor_kind(
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
) -> None:
    """Every `factor_kind` is a defined `CreditFactorKind` value."""
    valid = np.array(sorted(VALID_CREDIT_FACTOR_KIND_IDS), dtype=np.int8)
    bad = ~np.isin(factor_kind, valid)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: unknown factor_kind id(s) "
            f"{np.unique(factor_kind[bad])[:_PREVIEW].tolist()} for factor_id(s) "
            f"{_bad_ids(factor_ids, bad)}."
        )


def validate_chi_nu(
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    factor_chi_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """
    nu consistency with kind.

    - CHI factors: nu must be finite and strictly positive (it is the dof the
      generator draws ``W ~ chi^2(nu)`` with; a missing or non-positive value
      breaks the t-marginal).
    - Non-CHI factors: nu must be the `NO_CHI_NU` sentinel (never NaN, never a
      stray value that a future consumer might mistake for a real dof).
    """
    is_chi = factor_kind == np.int8(CreditFactorKind.CHI)

    bad_chi = is_chi & ~(np.isfinite(factor_chi_nu) & (factor_chi_nu > 0.0))
    if bad_chi.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: CHI factor(s) with missing or non-positive "
            f"nu for factor_id(s) {_bad_ids(factor_ids, bad_chi)}."
        )

    bad_non_chi = (~is_chi) & (factor_chi_nu != NO_CHI_NU)
    if bad_non_chi.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: non-CHI factor(s) carry a nu value "
            f"(must be NO_CHI_NU={float(NO_CHI_NU)}) for factor_id(s) "
            f"{_bad_ids(factor_ids, bad_non_chi)}."
        )


def validate_beta_params(
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    factor_beta_alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_beta_beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """
    Beta(alpha, beta) parameter consistency with kind.

    - BETA_PD factors: both alpha and beta must be finite and strictly positive
      (they are the shape parameters the generator draws ``P_s ~ Beta`` with; a
      missing or non-positive value is an invalid Beta).
    - Non-BETA_PD factors: both must be the `NO_BETA_PARAM` sentinel (never NaN,
      never a stray value a future consumer might mistake for a real parameter).
    """
    is_beta = factor_kind == np.int8(CreditFactorKind.BETA_PD)
    is_good_beta = (
        np.isfinite(factor_beta_alpha)
        & (factor_beta_alpha > 0.0)
        & np.isfinite(factor_beta_beta)
        & (factor_beta_beta > 0.0)
    )

    is_bad_beta = is_beta & ~is_good_beta
    if is_bad_beta.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: BETA_PD factor(s) with missing or "
            f"non-positive alpha/beta for factor_id(s) "
            f"{_bad_ids(factor_ids, is_bad_beta)}."
        )

    bad_non_beta = (~is_beta) & (
        (factor_beta_alpha != NO_BETA_PARAM) | (factor_beta_beta != NO_BETA_PARAM)
    )
    if bad_non_beta.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"credit factor universe: non-BETA_PD factor(s) carry an alpha/beta "
            f"value (must be NO_BETA_PARAM={float(NO_BETA_PARAM)}) for "
            f"factor_id(s) {_bad_ids(factor_ids, bad_non_beta)}."
        )


def validate_credit_factor_universe_arrays(
    *,
    factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    factor_names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    factor_kind: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    factor_chi_nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_beta_alpha: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    factor_beta_beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Single-call entry point: every validator, fail-fast on first violation."""
    validate_lengths_aligned(
        factor_ids=factor_ids,
        factor_names=factor_names,
        factor_kind=factor_kind,
        factor_chi_nu=factor_chi_nu,
        factor_beta_alpha=factor_beta_alpha,
        factor_beta_beta=factor_beta_beta,
    )
    validate_factor_id_sorted_unique(factor_ids)
    validate_factor_names(factor_ids, factor_names)
    validate_factor_kind(factor_ids, factor_kind)
    validate_chi_nu(factor_ids, factor_kind, factor_chi_nu)
    validate_beta_params(factor_ids, factor_kind, factor_beta_alpha, factor_beta_beta)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/netting_set/compiled.py
from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledNettingSetMaster",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledNettingSetMaster:
    """
    Compiled netting-set master data.

    This only contains information relevant to netting-set-level collateral, which is
    all that's needed for exposure calculation. The master is keyed by `uid`, which
    is the netting-set id ingested from the batch and joined onto the portfolio's
    `trade_uids` to align collateral to priced trades.
    """

    # --- NETTING SET UID ---
    # ingest column netting_set_id; sorted ascending, unique
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # --- OBLIGOR UID ---
    # Obligor uid of the netting set; aligned to `uid`
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # --- COLLATERAL PER NETTING SET ---
    # net collateral per netting set; aligned to `uid`
    net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/netting_set/ingest.py
"""
Netting-set master DataFrame -> `CompiledNettingSetMaster`.

The netting-set edge: a human-facing master frame is cast to strict dtypes,
sorted by netting-set uid ascending, validated, and collapsed into a single
immutable SoA container. The ascending uid invariant is what
`compile_exposure._resolve_collateral` relies on for its `searchsorted`
join from `netting_set_uids` -> `(obligor_uid, net_collateral)`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import warnings

__all__ = [
    "compile_netting_set_master",
]

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster
from riskquant.credit.masters.netting_set.validate import (
    validate_netting_set_master_arrays,
)

_REQUIRED_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = ("uid", "obligor_uid", "net_collateral")


def _check_required_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_netting_set_master: missing required column(s) {missing}."
        )


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if np.isnan(arr).any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_netting_set_master: NaN in non-nullable column '{col}'."
        )
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def compile_netting_set_master(
    netting_set_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> CompiledNettingSetMaster:
    """
    Compile a validated netting-set master frame into `CompiledNettingSetMaster`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    netting_set_df
        One row per effective netting set. Required columns:
        - `uid` (int64): netting-set identifier; matches the
          `netting_set_id` column on the portfolio ingest frame.
        - `obligor_uid` (int64): obligor that owns the netting set; the
          join key from the exposure result into the obligor space.
        - `net_collateral` (float64): haircut-adjusted collateral, >= 0.

    Returns
    -------
    CompiledNettingSetMaster
        `uid` sorted ascending; other columns reordered by the same
        permutation to stay row-aligned.
    """
    if netting_set_df.empty:
        raise ValueError("compile_netting_set_master: cannot compile an empty frame.")

    _check_required_columns(netting_set_df)

    df = netting_set_df.sort_values("uid", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)

    uid = _i64(df, "uid")
    obligor_uid = _i64(df, "obligor_uid")
    net_collateral = _f64(df, "net_collateral")

    validate_netting_set_master_arrays(
        uid=uid,
        obligor_uid=obligor_uid,
        net_collateral=net_collateral,
    )

    return CompiledNettingSetMaster(
        uid=uid,
        obligor_uid=obligor_uid,
        net_collateral=net_collateral,
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\masters\netting_set\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/netting_set/validate.py
"""
Netting-set master validators.

Mirrors `obligors/validate.py`: vectorized predicates on post-ingest SoA,
no row loops, no pandas. Cross-master validation (obligor uids present on
the obligor master) is deliberately out of scope here — it's a layered
check that belongs at the top-level pipeline orchestrator, where both
masters are visible.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.exceptions import ConfigurationError

_PREVIEW = 10


def _bad_uids(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]) -> list[int]:
    return uid[mask][:_PREVIEW].tolist()


def validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]) -> None:
    """`uid` (netting-set id) must be strictly ascending."""
    if uid.shape[0] == 0:
        raise ValueError("netting set master: empty uid array.")
    if not (np.diff(uid) > 0).all():
        unique, counts = np.unique(uid, return_counts=True)
        dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        if dups:
            raise ValueError(
                f"netting set master: duplicate netting-set uid(s) {dups}."
            )
        raise ValueError("netting set master: uid is not sorted ascending.")


def validate_obligor_uid_nonnegative(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    """Every netting set must point at a non-negative obligor uid (no sentinel)."""
    bad = obligor_uid < 0
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"netting set master: negative obligor_uid for netting set(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_net_collateral(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    """
    Validates that net collateral values are finite.
    Negative values are mathematically valid and represent net posted collateral.
    """
    # =====================================================================
    # DOD CONTRACT: Net Collateral = Received - Posted.
    # Negative values (posted) correctly increase exposure via max(V - C, 0)
    # =====================================================================
    bad = ~np.isfinite(net_collateral)

    if bad.any():
        bad_uids = uid[bad][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        raise ConfigurationError(
            f"NETTING_SET_MASTER: net_collateral must be finite. Invalid at UIDs: {bad_uids}"
        )


def validate_netting_set_master_arrays(
    *,
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Single-call entry point: every validator, fail-fast."""
    validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid)
    validate_obligor_uid_nonnegative(uid, obligor_uid)
    validate_net_collateral(uid, net_collateral)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/obligors/compiled.py
from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CompiledObligorCredit",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledObligorCredit:
    """
    Universal obligor credit data.
    Consumed by ALL credit kernels (Deterministic JTD, Expected Loss, Copula).
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # obligor ID; sorted ascending, unique
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8]  # int enum from config/enums/credit_ratings
    sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # int enum, future
    country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # int enum, future
    parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR sentinel for ultimates
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/obligors/ingest.py
"""
Obligor master DataFrame -> `CompiledObligorCredit`.

The obligor edge: a human-facing master frame is cast to strict dtypes,
sorted by uid ascending, validated as one self-consistent universe, and
collapsed into a single immutable SoA container.

`uid` ascending sorted is a structural invariant the downstream layers
rely on for vectorized joins (`searchsorted` in `compile_jtd_portfolio_from_df`,
`aggregate_ead_to_obligor`, and `_resolve_collateral`). The same ordering
permutation is applied to every column so the SoA stays aligned row-wise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "compile_obligor_master",
]

from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import (
    NO_COUNTRY_ID,
    NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR,
    NO_RATING_ID,
    NO_SECTOR_ID,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.validate import validate_obligor_master_arrays

_REQUIRED_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "uid",
    "pd",
    "lgd",
    # "rating_id",
    # "sector_id",
    # "country_id",
    # "parent_uid",
)


def _check_required_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_obligor_master: missing required column(s) {missing}."
        )


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if np.isnan(arr).any():
        raise ValueError(f"compile_obligor_master: NaN in non-nullable column '{col}'.")
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def _i8(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int8))


def _i32(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32))


def _rating_id(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    """NA / None -> NO_RATING_ID; numeric values pass through as int8."""
    if "rating_id" not in df.columns:
        # Fill missing column with default value.
        return np.full(len(df), NO_RATING_ID, dtype=np.int8)
    filled = (
        df["rating_id"]
        .astype("Int8")  # pandas nullable integer
        .fillna(NO_RATING_ID)
    )
    return cast(
        npt.NDArray[np.int8],
        np.ascontiguousarray(filled.to_numpy(dtype=np.int8)),
    )


def _sector_id(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """NA / None -> NO_SECTOR_ID; numeric values pass through as int32."""
    if "sector_id" not in df.columns:
        # Fill missing column with default value.
        return np.full(len(df), NO_SECTOR_ID, dtype=np.int32)
    filled = (
        df["sector_id"]
        .astype("Int32")  # pandas nullable integer
        .fillna(NO_SECTOR_ID)
    )
    return cast(
        npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        np.ascontiguousarray(filled.to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)),
    )


def _country_id(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """NA / None -> NO_COUNTRY_ID; numeric values pass through as int32."""
    if "country_id" not in df.columns:
        # Fill missing column with default value.
        return np.full(len(df), NO_COUNTRY_ID, dtype=np.int32)
    filled = (
        df["country_id"]
        .astype("Int32")  # pandas nullable integer
        .fillna(NO_COUNTRY_ID)
    )
    return cast(
        npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        np.ascontiguousarray(filled.to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)),
    )


def _parent_uid(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """NA / None -> NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR; numeric values pass through as int64."""
    if "parent_uid" not in df.columns:
        # Fill missing column with default value.
        return np.full(len(df), NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR, dtype=np.int64)
    filled = (
        df["parent_uid"]
        .astype("Int64")  # pandas nullable integer
        .fillna(NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR)
    )
    return cast(
        npt.NDArray[np.int64],
        np.ascontiguousarray(filled.to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)),
    )


def compile_obligor_master(
    obligor_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> CompiledObligorCredit:
    """
    Compile a validated obligor-master frame into `CompiledObligorCredit`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    obligor_df
        One row per obligor. Required columns: uid, pd, lgd, rating_id,
        sector_id, country_id, parent_uid. `parent_uid` is nullable —
        NA/None marks an ultimate (no parent) and is mapped to
        `NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR`.

    Returns
    -------
    CompiledObligorCredit
        `uid` sorted ascending; all other columns reordered by the same
        permutation to stay row-aligned.
    """
    if obligor_df.empty:
        raise ValueError("compile_obligor_master: cannot compile an empty frame.")

    _check_required_columns(obligor_df)

    # Sort once by uid; one stable permutation applied to every column. Sorting
    # the frame (not the arrays) keeps the parent_uid fillna semantics intact.
    df = obligor_df.sort_values("uid", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)

    # Required columns are cast to strict dtypes and converted to C-contiguous arrays.
    uid = _i64(df, "uid")
    pd_ = _f64(df, "pd")
    lgd = _f64(df, "lgd")

    # Optional columns are also cast to strict dtypes, but missing columns are filled with defaults.
    rating_id = _rating_id(df)
    sector_id = _sector_id(df)
    country_id = _country_id(df)
    parent_uid = _parent_uid(df)

    validate_obligor_master_arrays(
        uid=uid,
        pd=pd_,
        lgd=lgd,
        rating_id=rating_id,
        sector_id=sector_id,
        country_id=country_id,
        parent_uid=parent_uid,
    )

    return CompiledObligorCredit(
        uid=uid,
        pd=pd_,
        lgd=lgd,
        rating_id=rating_id,
        sector_id=sector_id,
        country_id=country_id,
        parent_uid=parent_uid,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/masters/obligors/validate.py
"""
Obligor master validators.

Pure vectorized predicates on the post-ingest SoA arrays. Each function
either passes silently or raises `ValueError` with the (bounded) list of
offending uids. No row loops, no pandas, no string lookups — these run
once at ingest time, on float64/int64/int32/int8 arrays already cast to
their compiled dtypes.

The validators are split (uid uniqueness, range checks, sentinel checks)
rather than fused so any concrete failure points at exactly one invariant.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import (
    NO_COUNTRY_ID,
    NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR,
    NO_RATING_ID,
    NO_SECTOR_ID,
)

_PREVIEW = 10
_PD_LO = 0.0
_PD_HI = 1.0
_LGD_LO = 0.0
_LGD_HI = 1.0


def _bad_uids(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]) -> list[int]:
    return uid[mask][:_PREVIEW].tolist()


def validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]) -> None:
    """`uid` must be strictly ascending — uniqueness + sorted in one check."""
    if uid.shape[0] == 0:
        raise ValueError("obligor master: empty uid array.")
    if not (np.diff(uid) > 0).all():
        # Surface duplicates first (the common bug); sortedness second.
        unique, counts = np.unique(uid, return_counts=True)
        dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        if dups:
            raise ValueError(f"obligor master: duplicate uid(s) {dups}.")
        raise ValueError("obligor master: uid is not sorted ascending.")


def validate_pd_range(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]) -> None:
    """PD in [0, 1]; NaN is also rejected (np.isnan short-circuits the compare)."""
    bad = ~((pd >= _PD_LO) & (pd <= _PD_HI))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: PD outside [{_PD_LO}, {_PD_HI}] for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_lgd_range(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    """LGD in [0, 1]; NaN is also rejected."""
    bad = ~((lgd >= _LGD_LO) & (lgd <= _LGD_HI))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: LGD outside [{_LGD_LO}, {_LGD_HI}] for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_rating_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
) -> None:
    """
    `rating_id` must be either the unknown sentinel (`NO_RATING_ID == 0`) or
    a positive int8. The full rating-id taxonomy lives in
    `config/enums/credit_ratings`; this validator only enforces the dtype-
    level invariant. The taxonomy check is deferred until the rating enum
    is promoted to an IntEnum and a canonical {AAA..D} -> int8 mapping
    lands.
    """
    bad = (rating_id < np.int8(0)) & (rating_id != NO_RATING_ID)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: rating_id < 0 (must be NO_RATING_ID={NO_RATING_ID} "
            f"or a positive int8) for uid(s) {_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_sector_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
) -> None:
    bad = (sector_id < np.int32(0)) & (sector_id != NO_SECTOR_ID)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: sector_id < 0 and not NO_SECTOR_ID for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_country_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
) -> None:
    bad = (country_id < np.int32(0)) & (country_id != NO_COUNTRY_ID)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: country_id < 0 and not NO_COUNTRY_ID for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_parent_uid(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    """
    Validate obligor hierarchy.

    Rules
    -----
    1. Every parent_uid is either NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR or an obligor uid
       present in this master.
    2. An obligor may not list itself as its parent.
    3. The parent hierarchy must be acyclic.
    """

    n = uid.shape[0]

    needs_lookup = parent_uid != NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR

    # Parent row index for each obligor (-1 = no parent).
    parent_idx = np.full(n, -1, dtype=np.int64)

    if needs_lookup.any():
        targets = parent_uid[needs_lookup]

        pos = np.searchsorted(uid, targets)

        matched = (pos < n) & (uid[np.minimum(pos, n - 1)] == targets)

        if not matched.all():
            bad_children = uid[needs_lookup][~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
            bad_parents = targets[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist()

            raise ValueError(
                f"obligor master: parent_uid(s) {bad_parents} "
                f"(children {bad_children}) absent from the obligor master."
            )

        parent_idx[needs_lookup] = pos

        self_parent = uid[needs_lookup] == targets

        if self_parent.any():
            bad = uid[needs_lookup][self_parent][:_PREVIEW].tolist()

            raise ValueError(
                f"obligor master: obligor(s) {bad} list themselves as parent_uid."
            )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cycle detection.
    #
    # state:
    #   0 = unseen
    #   1 = processed
    #
    # walk_id:
    #   identifies the current traversal.
    #
    # If we revisit a node with the same walk_id before reaching a
    # processed node or root, we found a cycle.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    state = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    walk_id = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)

    current_walk = 0

    for start_idx in range(n):
        if state[start_idx]:
            continue

        current_walk += 1
        idx = start_idx

        while idx != -1:
            if state[idx]:
                break

            if walk_id[idx] == current_walk:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"obligor master: parent hierarchy cycle detected "
                    f"at obligor {int(uid[idx])}."
                )

            walk_id[idx] = current_walk
            idx = parent_idx[idx]

        idx = start_idx

        while idx != -1 and not state[idx]:
            state[idx] = 1
            idx = parent_idx[idx]


def validate_obligor_master_arrays(
    *,
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    """Single-call entry point: every validator, fail-fast on first violation."""
    validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid)
    validate_pd_range(uid, pd)
    validate_lgd_range(uid, lgd)
    validate_rating_id(uid, rating_id)
    validate_sector_id(uid, sector_id)
    validate_country_id(uid, country_id)
    validate_parent_uid(uid, parent_uid)
```
</file>

```python
# `riskquant.credit` — Stable Public API

This is the frozen public surface of the credit modelling core. Everything listed
here is supported and versioned: **changing a public name or signature is a
breaking change.** Everything not listed is internal — it may move, rename, or
change shape at any time without notice.

## Policy

- **Public** = listed below = enforced by that module's `__all__`. Stable
  signatures.
- **Internal** = everything else (no `__all__` membership). No stability promise.
- **Import convention.** `__init__.py` are empty by constitution; there is no
  re-export hub. Import public names from their owning module by full path
  (`from riskquant.credit.jtd.engine import run_jtd`). `__all__` on each public
  module is the machine-checkable record of that module's surface. This keeps the
  Numba-free ingest modules free of the kernel import tax.

## Public surface

### Masters — ingest (edge; pandas in)
- `masters.obligors.ingest.compile_obligor_master`
- `masters.netting_set.ingest.compile_netting_set_master`
- `masters.factor_universe.ingest.compile_credit_factor_universe`

### Masters — compiled values (SoA; inspectable)
- `masters.obligors.compiled.CompiledObligorCredit`
- `masters.netting_set.compiled.CompiledNettingSetMaster`
- `masters.factor_universe.compiled.CompiledCreditFactorUniverse`
- `masters.cross_master.compiled.CompiledCreditMasters`

### Factor simulation
- `factors.simulate.base.build_credit_factor_matrix`
- `factors.simulate.mixed.MixedCreditFactorGenerator` (`.from_universe`)
- `factors.state.CreditFactorMatrix` (the latent-factor value `run_jtd` consumes)

### JTD ingest + execution
- `jtd.ingest.bundle.JtdIngestBundle` (incl. `from_instrument_frame`)
- `jtd.ingest.compile.compile_jtd_portfolio`
- `jtd.compiled.CompiledJtdPortfolio`
- `jtd.engine.run_jtd`
- `jtd.results.JtdLossResult`

### Aggregation / risk metrics
- `aggregate.summary`: `build_portfolio_loss_distribution`, `summarize_jtd_loss`,
  `JtdRiskSummary`, `model_tail_var_es`
- `aggregate.loss_metrics`: `expected_loss`, `loss_var_es`, `value_at_risk`,
  `expected_shortfall`, `tail_var_es_by_group`, `loss_quantiles`,
  `PortfolioLossDistribution`

### Taxonomy (stable integer enums)
- `jtd.models.JtdModel`
- `masters.eums.credit_factor_kind.CreditFactorKind`

## Deprecated (removed next release)

Importable but warn on call; not in `__all__`. Migrate to the new name.

None.

## Explicitly internal (NOT part of the contract)

`jtd.registry` (model wiring; the supported way to *extend*, not a stability
promise), `jtd.ingest.builders.*`, `jtd.ingest._shared`, `jtd.ingest.contracts`
(`JtdModelContract`, `FACTOR_COLUMN_KIND`), `jtd.ingest.validate` predicates, all
`jtd.kernels.*` and `jtd.evaluators.*`, block classes / constructors
(`jtd.blocks.*`), the derived constants in `jtd.models` (`NUM_JTD_MODELS`,
`*_ID`, `MF_COPULA_MODELS`), and `scenario_metrics`
(a separate shared tail-stats package with its own surface).

## Resolved decisions
```

```python
# riskquant/exceptions.py


class PrettyKeyError(KeyError):
    """
    KeyError variant that prints the message using str(...) instead of repr(...),
    allowing multi-line, human-readable diagnostics while preserving KeyError
    semantics.
    """

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return str(self.args[0])


class RiskQuantError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all custom riskquant errors."""

    pass


class ConfigurationError(RiskQuantError):
    """
    Raised when static configuration, ID namespaces, or
    market data mappings violate the engine's strict contracts.
    """

    pass
```

```python
# src/riskquant/ingest_fixtures/constants.py

"""
Constants used across the ingest fixtures codebase.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Final

import numpy as np

# ==============================================================================
# RANDOM NUMBER GENERATION
# ==============================================================================

# Seed for RNG in ingest fixtures. This is a constant to ensure reproducibility of
# any random data generated for testing purposes.
CANONICAL_RNG_SEED: Final[int] = 49

# ==============================================================================
# BUSINESS DAY CALCULATIONS
# ==============================================================================

# Business days per year for Brownian-motion scaling.
BUSINESS_DAYS_PER_YEAR: Final[int] = 252

# Number of business days of history to synthesize.
CANONICAL_HISTORY_BUSINESS_DAYS: Final[int] = 260


# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL HISTORICAL SIMULATION CONSTANTS
# ==============================================================================


# Output dtype for the synthetic history. float32 matches the downstream
# RiskFactorMatrix default; float64 would be wasted precision.
HISTORY_DTYPE: Final[type[np.floating]] = np.float32


# ==============================================================================
# MARKET RISK FACTOR SIMULATION CONSTANTS
# ==============================================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _MarketRiskFactorParams:
    """Per-factor GBM/ABM parameters used to seed the synthetic history."""

    s0: float
    annualized_vol: float
    is_rate: bool  # True -> arithmetic BM; False -> geometric BM


# Defaults applied per asset class / unit type. Keep them in one block so
# tuning is obvious and no values are buried inside the universe loop.
EQUITY_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=185.50,
    annualized_vol=0.25,
    is_rate=False,
)
FX_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=0.9250,
    annualized_vol=0.08,
    is_rate=False,
)
RATE_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=4.00,
    annualized_vol=0.0075,
    is_rate=True,
)
GAS_COMMODITY_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=2.50,
    annualized_vol=0.35,
    is_rate=False,
)
OIL_COMMODITY_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=80.00,
    annualized_vol=0.35,
    is_rate=False,
)
VOL_OF_VOL_DEFAULTS: Final[_MarketRiskFactorParams] = _MarketRiskFactorParams(
    s0=0.35,
    annualized_vol=0.50,
    is_rate=False,
)
```
