<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\scenario_metrics\api\var_es.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/scenario_metrics/api/var_es.py

from __future__ import annotations

from riskquant.scenario_metrics._computer import (
    _compute_lower_tail_parameters,
    _compute_tail_var_es,
)
from riskquant.scenario_metrics._result import VarEsResult
from riskquant.scenario_metrics._types import Float64Matrix
from riskquant.scenario_metrics._validate import (
    _validate_confidence_level,
    _validate_group_value_matrix,
)


def compute_var_es(
    group_value_matrix: Float64Matrix,
    confidence_level: float,
) -> VarEsResult:
    """
    Compute lower-tail VaR and ES for each group.

    Parameters
    ----------
    group_value_matrix : Float64Matrix
        Shape (n_groups, n_scenarios).

    confidence_level : float
        Example: 0.99.

    Returns
    -------
    var_es_result : VarEsResult
        Contains the VaR and ES for each group.
    """

    _validate_group_value_matrix(
        group_value_matrix,
    )

    _validate_confidence_level(
        confidence_level,
    )

    kth_index, tail_count = _compute_lower_tail_parameters(
        scenario_count=group_value_matrix.shape[1],
        confidence_level=confidence_level,
    )

    return _compute_tail_var_es(
        group_value_matrix=group_value_matrix,
        kth_index=kth_index,
        tail_count=tail_count,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/solutions/credit_var/api.py
"""
Public API for the credit-VaR solution.

This module is THE entry surface for the solution: every symbol a consumer
(notebook, Emissary, the xlwings tool) is meant to use is re-exported here, and
nothing else in `riskquant.solutions.credit_var.*` should be imported from
outside the package. The other modules (`run`, `schemas`, `catalog`, `recipes`)
are supportive internals — they may change shape freely as long as this surface
holds.

Why a module and not the package `__init__`: in `src/riskquant`, every
`__init__.py` is empty, without exception. A solution that wants a single import
line gets it from a named `api` module instead, so the "one stable surface" need
is met without weakening the empty-init rule.

Import as::

    from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.api import run_credit_var, CreditVarRequest
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    # entry point
    "run_credit_var",
    # request / result contract
    "CreditVarRequest",
    "CreditVarResult",
    "ObligorRow",
    "LoadingRow",
    "RiskSummary",
    "ModelTail",
    "RunProvenance",
    # model catalog (the front-end menu + param specs)
    "MODEL_CATALOG",
    "ModelEntry",
    "ParamSpec",
    "model_entry",
    "supported_model_keys",
]

from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.catalog import (
    MODEL_CATALOG,
    ModelEntry,
    ParamSpec,
    model_entry,
    supported_model_keys,
)
from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.run import run_credit_var
from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.schemas import (
    CreditVarRequest,
    CreditVarResult,
    LoadingRow,
    ModelTail,
    ObligorRow,
    RiskSummary,
    RunProvenance,
)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/solutions/credit_var/catalog.py
"""
Credit-VaR model catalog (solution-tier).

The menu the front-end renders and the source of per-model parameter rules the
request schema validates against. This is deliberately solution-side: it carries
human display labels and the authoring contract, which the core engine has no
business knowing. It lists only models this solution can run; the underlying
construction lives in `riskquant.solutions.credit_var.recipe`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "ParamSpec",
    "ModelEntry",
    "MODEL_CATALOG",
    "supported_model_keys",
    "model_entry",
]

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.recipes import SUPPORTED_RECIPES


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ParamSpec:
    """One scalar knob a model exposes to the author."""

    key: str
    label: str
    default: float
    min_value: float
    max_value: float
    min_inclusive: bool = True
    max_inclusive: bool = False

    def contains(self, value: float) -> bool:
        lo = value >= self.min_value if self.min_inclusive else value > self.min_value
        hi = value <= self.max_value if self.max_inclusive else value < self.max_value
        return bool(lo and hi)

    @property
    def interval_repr(self) -> str:
        lb = "[" if self.min_inclusive else "("
        rb = "]" if self.max_inclusive else ")"
        return f"{lb}{self.min_value}, {self.max_value}{rb}"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ModelEntry:
    key: str
    display_name: str
    description: str
    params: tuple[ParamSpec, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    requires_loadings: bool = False  # multi-factor: needs a systemic-loadings sheet


_RHO = ParamSpec(
    key="rho",
    label="Asset correlation (rho)",
    default=0.15,
    min_value=0.0,
    max_value=1.0,
    min_inclusive=True,
    max_inclusive=False,
)
_NU = ParamSpec(
    key="nu",
    label="Degrees of freedom (nu)",
    default=8.0,
    min_value=2.0,
    max_value=250.0,
    min_inclusive=True,
    max_inclusive=True,
)
_BETA_ALPHA = ParamSpec(
    key="alpha",
    label="Beta shape alpha",
    default=1.0,
    min_value=0.0,
    max_value=1000.0,
    min_inclusive=False,
    max_inclusive=True,
)
_BETA_BETA = ParamSpec(
    key="beta",
    label="Beta shape beta",
    default=24.0,
    min_value=0.0,
    max_value=1000.0,
    min_inclusive=False,
    max_inclusive=True,
)
_STRESS_PROB = ParamSpec(
    key="stress_prob",
    label="Stress-regime probability",
    default=0.05,
    min_value=0.0,
    max_value=1.0,
    min_inclusive=True,
    max_inclusive=True,
)
_STRESS_MULT = ParamSpec(
    key="stress_pd_multiplier",
    label="Stressed PD multiplier",
    default=3.0,
    min_value=1.0,
    max_value=50.0,
    min_inclusive=True,
    max_inclusive=True,
)


MODEL_CATALOG: dict[str, ModelEntry] = {
    "forced_default": ModelEntry(
        key="forced_default",
        display_name="Forced Default",
        description="Every obligor defaults in every scenario; loss = EAD x LGD. "
        "A deterministic worst-case bound (PD is ignored).",
        params=(),
    ),
    "beta_binomial": ModelEntry(
        key="beta_binomial",
        display_name="Beta-Binomial",
        description="Conditionally-independent defaults driven by a shared per-"
        "scenario PD drawn from Beta(alpha, beta). Mean PD = alpha/(alpha+beta); "
        "the obligor PD column is not used.",
        params=(_BETA_ALPHA, _BETA_BETA),
    ),
    "stress_regime_bernoulli": ModelEntry(
        key="stress_regime_bernoulli",
        display_name="Stress-Regime Bernoulli",
        description="Independent-Bernoulli defaults with a shared stress regime "
        "that fires with probability stress_prob and multiplies every PD by "
        "stress_pd_multiplier (capped at 1).",
        params=(_STRESS_PROB, _STRESS_MULT),
    ),
    "sf_gaussian": ModelEntry(
        key="sf_gaussian",
        display_name="Single-Factor Gaussian Copula",
        description="One systemic factor with asset correlation rho (Vasicek / "
        "Basel ASRF). The standard desk credit-VaR model.",
        params=(_RHO,),
    ),
    "independent_bernoulli": ModelEntry(
        key="independent_bernoulli",
        display_name="Independent Bernoulli",
        description="Each obligor defaults independently with probability PD. "
        "No correlation; the diversified baseline.",
        params=(),
    ),
    "sf_students_t": ModelEntry(
        key="sf_students_t",
        display_name="Single-Factor Student-t Copula",
        description="Single systemic factor with asset correlation rho plus a "
        "shared chi-square mixing variable at nu degrees of freedom (tail "
        "dependence / fatter joint tails than Gaussian at the same rho).",
        params=(_RHO, _NU),
    ),
    "mf_gaussian": ModelEntry(
        key="mf_gaussian",
        display_name="Multi-Factor Gaussian Copula",
        description="Per-obligor systemic loadings across several factors (e.g. a "
        "global factor plus sector factors), supplied as a loadings sheet. "
        "Idiosyncratic weight is the residual sqrt(1 - sum beta^2).",
        params=(),
        requires_loadings=True,
    ),
    "mf_students_t": ModelEntry(
        key="mf_students_t",
        display_name="Multi-Factor Student-t Copula",
        description="Multi-factor Gaussian loadings plus a shared chi-square "
        "mixing variable at nu degrees of freedom (multi-factor structure with "
        "tail dependence). Needs a loadings sheet.",
        params=(_NU,),
        requires_loadings=True,
    ),
}

# No-drift guard: the catalog must not advertise a model the core can't assemble.
_missing = set(MODEL_CATALOG) - set(SUPPORTED_RECIPES)
if _missing:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"credit_var.catalog advertises model(s) with no core recipe: {sorted(_missing)}."
    )


def supported_model_keys() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    return tuple(MODEL_CATALOG)


def model_entry(key: str) -> ModelEntry:
    entry = MODEL_CATALOG.get(key)
    if entry is None:
        raise KeyError(f"unknown model '{key}'. Supported: {sorted(MODEL_CATALOG)}.")
    return entry
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\solutions\credit_var\recipes.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/solutions/credit_var/recipes.py
"""
Model recipes: flat obligor table + scalar params -> compiled engine inputs.

The authoring layer of the credit-VaR solution: it turns a desk's flat sheet plus
a handful of scalar knobs into the factor universe + per-model JTD frame the engine
needs, which the author never sees. Single-factor Gaussian: rho becomes a systemic
factor, per-obligor idiosyncratic factors, and a sqrt(rho) loading. Beta-binomial:
(alpha, beta) become a shared BETA_PD factor. Student-t: adds a shared CHI factor
at nu. Stress-regime: a shared STRESS_INDICATOR plus a per-run stress probability.

This is solution-tier, not core: the core engine takes a fully-authored universe +
bundle; "construct them from a flat table" is presentation/authoring sugar that
belongs with the solution. Plain DataFrame + params in, engine objects out,
fail-fast. Adding a flat-sheet model is one assembler here plus a catalog entry.

Multi-factor copulas are deliberately absent: their systemic loadings are a
relational input (a loadings sheet), not a scalar, and are handled by a separate
relational recipe path.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "AssembledInputs",
    "SUPPORTED_RECIPES",
    "assemble_inputs",
]

import math
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.compiled import CompiledObligorEad
from riskquant.credit.exposure.obligor_ead.ingest import compile_obligor_ead
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.bundle import JtdIngestBundle
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.masters.cross_master.compiled import CompiledCreditMasters
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.compiled import (
    CompiledCreditFactorUniverse,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.factor_universe.ingest import (
    compile_credit_factor_universe,
)
from riskquant.credit.masters.obligors.ingest import compile_obligor_master

_SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL = "SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL"
_BETA_PD_GLOBAL = "BETA_PD_GLOBAL"
_STRESS_GLOBAL = "STRESS_GLOBAL"
_CHI_GLOBAL = "CHI_GLOBAL"
_FORCED_DUMMY = (
    "FORCED_DUMMY"  # forced-default reads no factor; the generator needs >=1
)

_DEFAULT_RHO = 0.15
_DEFAULT_NU = 8.0
_DEFAULT_BETA_ALPHA = 1.0
_DEFAULT_BETA_BETA = 24.0  # mean PD = alpha/(alpha+beta) = 0.04
_DEFAULT_STRESS_PROB = 0.05
_DEFAULT_STRESS_MULT = 3.0


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class AssembledInputs:
    """Compiled engine inputs for one run.

    `stress_p_by_uix` carries the per-run STRESS_INDICATOR probabilities the
    generator needs (the one knob kept off the static universe); empty for models
    with no stress factor.
    """

    masters: CompiledCreditMasters
    bundle: JtdIngestBundle
    obligor_ead: CompiledObligorEad
    stress_p_by_uix: dict[int, float] = field(default_factory=dict)


# A universe row: factor name + kind, with optional marginal params the kind needs
# (nu for CHI, alpha/beta for BETA_PD).
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _FactorRow:
    name: str
    kind: str
    nu: float | None = None
    alpha: float | None = None
    beta: float | None = None


def _uids(obligor_table: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_table["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))


def _universe(rows: list[_FactorRow]) -> CompiledCreditFactorUniverse:
    """Compile factor rows into a universe, emitting the nu / alpha / beta columns
    only when a kind that needs them is present (the ingest maps NaN elsewhere to
    its sentinels)."""
    data: dict[str, object] = {
        "factor_id": np.arange(len(rows), dtype=np.int64),
        "factor_name": [r.name for r in rows],
        "factor_kind": [r.kind for r in rows],
    }
    if any(r.nu is not None for r in rows):
        data["nu"] = [r.nu if r.nu is not None else np.nan for r in rows]
    if any(r.alpha is not None for r in rows):
        data["alpha"] = [r.alpha if r.alpha is not None else np.nan for r in rows]
    if any(r.beta is not None for r in rows):
        data["beta"] = [r.beta if r.beta is not None else np.nan for r in rows]
    return compile_credit_factor_universe(pd.DataFrame(data))


def _masters(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame, universe: CompiledCreditFactorUniverse
) -> CompiledCreditMasters:
    # compile_obligor_master reads uid/pd/lgd and ignores the rest (e.g. ead).
    return CompiledCreditMasters(
        obligor_master=compile_obligor_master(obligor_table),
        factor_universe=universe,
    )


def _ead(obligor_table: pd.DataFrame) -> CompiledObligorEad:
    return compile_obligor_ead(obligor_table)


def _require(params: Mapping[str, float], key: str, default: float) -> float:
    return float(params.get(key, default))


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-model assemblers
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _assemble_forced_default(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    # Forced default reads no factor, but the generator needs a non-empty universe.
    universe = _universe([_FactorRow(name=_FORCED_DUMMY, kind="UNIFORM")])
    frame = pd.DataFrame({"uid": uids, "instrument": JtdModel.FORCED_DEFAULT.name})
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _assemble_independent_bernoulli(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    uniform_names = [f"U_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    universe = _universe([_FactorRow(name=n, kind="UNIFORM") for n in uniform_names])
    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.INDEPENDENT_BERNOULLI.name,
            "uniform_factor": uniform_names,
        }
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _assemble_beta_binomial(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    alpha = _require(params, "alpha", _DEFAULT_BETA_ALPHA)
    beta = _require(params, "beta", _DEFAULT_BETA_BETA)
    if alpha <= 0.0 or beta <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"beta_binomial: alpha={alpha}, beta={beta} must be > 0.")

    uniform_names = [f"U_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    universe = _universe(
        [_FactorRow(name=n, kind="UNIFORM") for n in uniform_names]
        + [_FactorRow(name=_BETA_PD_GLOBAL, kind="BETA_PD", alpha=alpha, beta=beta)]
    )
    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.BETA_BINOMIAL.name,
            "uniform_factor": uniform_names,
            "beta_factor": _BETA_PD_GLOBAL,
        }
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _assemble_stress_regime_bernoulli(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    stress_prob = _require(params, "stress_prob", _DEFAULT_STRESS_PROB)
    stress_mult = _require(params, "stress_pd_multiplier", _DEFAULT_STRESS_MULT)
    if not (0.0 <= stress_prob <= 1.0):
        raise ValueError(f"stress_regime: stress_prob={stress_prob} must be in [0, 1].")
    if stress_mult < 1.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"stress_regime: stress_pd_multiplier={stress_mult} must be >= 1."
        )

    uniform_names = [f"U_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    universe = _universe(
        [_FactorRow(name=n, kind="UNIFORM") for n in uniform_names]
        + [_FactorRow(name=_STRESS_GLOBAL, kind="STRESS_INDICATOR")]
    )
    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.STRESS_REGIME_BERNOULLI.name,
            "uniform_factor": uniform_names,
            "stress_regime_factor": _STRESS_GLOBAL,
            "stress_pd_multiplier": np.full(uids.shape[0], stress_mult, np.float64),
        }
    )
    stress_p_by_uix = {int(u): stress_prob for u in universe.stress_indicator_uix}
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
        stress_p_by_uix=stress_p_by_uix,
    )


def _assemble_sf_gaussian(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    rho = _require(params, "rho", _DEFAULT_RHO)
    if not (0.0 <= rho < 1.0):
        raise ValueError(f"sf_gaussian: rho={rho} must be in [0, 1).")
    beta = math.sqrt(rho)  # systemic loading; idiosyncratic weight = sqrt(1 - rho)

    idio_names = [f"IDIO_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    universe = _universe(
        [_FactorRow(name=_SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL, kind="SYSTEMIC")]
        + [_FactorRow(name=n, kind="IDIOSYNCRATIC") for n in idio_names]
    )
    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN.name,
            "factor_loading": np.full(uids.shape[0], beta, dtype=np.float64),
            "systemic_factor": _SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL,
            "idiosyncratic_factor": idio_names,
        }
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _assemble_sf_students_t(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    rho = _require(params, "rho", _DEFAULT_RHO)
    nu = _require(params, "nu", _DEFAULT_NU)
    if not (0.0 <= rho < 1.0):
        raise ValueError(f"sf_students_t: rho={rho} must be in [0, 1).")
    if nu <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"sf_students_t: nu={nu} must be > 0.")
    beta = math.sqrt(rho)

    idio_names = [f"IDIO_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    universe = _universe(
        [_FactorRow(name=_SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL, kind="SYSTEMIC")]
        + [_FactorRow(name=n, kind="IDIOSYNCRATIC") for n in idio_names]
        + [_FactorRow(name=_CHI_GLOBAL, kind="CHI", nu=nu)]
    )
    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": uids,
            "instrument": JtdModel.COPULA_SF_STUDENTS_T.name,
            "factor_loading": np.full(uids.shape[0], beta, dtype=np.float64),
            "systemic_factor": _SYSTEMIC_GLOBAL,
            "idiosyncratic_factor": idio_names,
            "chi_squared_factor": _CHI_GLOBAL,
            # nu on the frame must match the chi factor's nu (engine invariant).
            "nu": np.full(uids.shape[0], nu, dtype=np.float64),
        }
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(frame),
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _mf_universe_and_bundle(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None,
    *,
    instrument: str,
    with_chi: bool,
    nu: float | None = None,
) -> tuple[CompiledCreditFactorUniverse, JtdIngestBundle]:
    """Shared MF build: distinct systemic factors from the loadings relation,
    per-obligor idiosyncratic factors, an optional shared CHI factor, the engine
    long-format loadings frame, and the per-obligor dispatch frame.

    Numeric validation (coverage, no-duplicate, sum-beta^2 <= 1, loading range,
    chi/nu reconciliation) is the engine's at compile; this only shapes inputs.
    """
    if loadings_table is None or len(loadings_table) == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{instrument}: a multi-factor model requires a non-empty loadings table."
        )
    uids = _uids(obligor_table)
    idio_names = [f"IDIO_{int(u)}" for u in uids]
    # Distinct systemic factors, authoring order preserved.
    systemic_names = list(dict.fromkeys(loadings_table["factor"].astype(str)))

    rows = [_FactorRow(name=s, kind="SYSTEMIC") for s in systemic_names]
    rows += [_FactorRow(name=n, kind="IDIOSYNCRATIC") for n in idio_names]
    if with_chi:
        rows.append(_FactorRow(name=_CHI_GLOBAL, kind="CHI", nu=nu))
    universe = _universe(rows)

    mf_loadings_df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "obligor_uid": loadings_table["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64),
            "factor_name": loadings_table["factor"].astype(str).to_numpy(),
            "factor_loading": loadings_table["loading"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64),
        }
    )

    frame_data: dict[str, object] = {
        "uid": uids,
        "instrument": instrument,
        "idiosyncratic_factor": idio_names,
    }
    if with_chi:
        frame_data["chi_squared_factor"] = _CHI_GLOBAL
        # frame nu must match the chi factor's nu (engine reconciliation).
        frame_data["nu"] = np.full(uids.shape[0], nu, dtype=np.float64)
    bundle = JtdIngestBundle.from_instrument_frame(
        pd.DataFrame(frame_data), mf_loadings_df=mf_loadings_df
    )
    return universe, bundle


def _assemble_mf_gaussian(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    universe, bundle = _mf_universe_and_bundle(
        obligor_table,
        loadings_table,
        instrument=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_GAUSSIAN.name,
        with_chi=False,
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=bundle,
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


def _assemble_mf_students_t(
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float],
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    nu = _require(params, "nu", _DEFAULT_NU)
    if nu <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"mf_students_t: nu={nu} must be > 0.")
    universe, bundle = _mf_universe_and_bundle(
        obligor_table,
        loadings_table,
        instrument=JtdModel.COPULA_MF_STUDENTS_T.name,
        with_chi=True,
        nu=nu,
    )
    return AssembledInputs(
        masters=_masters(obligor_table, universe),
        bundle=bundle,
        obligor_ead=_ead(obligor_table),
    )


# Recipe key -> assembler. The key is the stable model identifier the solution
# dispatches on; the catalog mirrors it (no-drift guard).
SUPPORTED_RECIPES: dict[str, object] = {
    "forced_default": _assemble_forced_default,
    "independent_bernoulli": _assemble_independent_bernoulli,
    "beta_binomial": _assemble_beta_binomial,
    "stress_regime_bernoulli": _assemble_stress_regime_bernoulli,
    "sf_gaussian": _assemble_sf_gaussian,
    "sf_students_t": _assemble_sf_students_t,
    "mf_gaussian": _assemble_mf_gaussian,
    "mf_students_t": _assemble_mf_students_t,
}


def assemble_inputs(
    *,
    model_key: str,
    obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
    params: Mapping[str, float] | None = None,
    loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
) -> AssembledInputs:
    """Build compiled engine inputs for `model_key` from the obligor table + params.

    Raises ValueError on an unknown/unsupported recipe; each assembler fail-fast
    validates its own params, and the obligor columns flow through the master / EAD
    compilers' validation.
    """
    assembler = SUPPORTED_RECIPES.get(model_key)
    if assembler is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"assemble_inputs: no recipe for model '{model_key}'. "
            f"Supported: {sorted(SUPPORTED_RECIPES)}."
        )
    return assembler(obligor_table, params or {}, loadings_table)  # type: ignore[operator]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\solutions\credit_var\run.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/solutions/credit_var/run.py
"""
`run_credit_var` — the credit-VaR solution entry point.

JSON/Pydantic in, JSON/Pydantic out; the core engine in between. This is the
notebook-facing convenience today and Emissary's integration target tomorrow:
the same call serves both because the contract is a validated payload, not a set
of engine objects. The function depends only on the frozen core API
(the solution recipe + the engine), never the other way round.

Notebook ergonomics: pass a `CreditVarRequest`, a plain ``dict`` (it is validated
into one), or keyword the request fields via `CreditVarRequest(...)`. The result
is a `CreditVarResult` whose `.model_dump()` / `.model_dump_json()` is wire-ready.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "run_credit_var",
]

import hashlib
import json
from datetime import datetime, timezone

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.aggregate.summary import (
    model_tail_var_es,
    summarize_jtd_loss,
)
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import build_credit_factor_matrix
from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.mixed import MixedCreditFactorGenerator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.engine import run_jtd_for_obligor_ead
from riskquant.credit.jtd.ingest.compile import compile_jtd_portfolio
from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.recipes import assemble_inputs
from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.schemas import (
    CreditVarRequest,
    CreditVarResult,
    ModelTail,
    RiskSummary,
    RunProvenance,
)

_PERCENTILE_GRID: tuple[float, ...] = (
    0.1,
    1.0,
    2.5,
    5.0,
    25.0,
    50.0,
    75.0,
    90.0,
    95.0,
    97.5,
    99.0,
    99.9,
)
_FACTOR_BATCH = 25_000


def _engine_version() -> str:
    try:
        from importlib.metadata import version

        return version("riskquant")
    except Exception:
        return "0+unknown"


def _obligor_frame(request: CreditVarRequest) -> pd.DataFrame:
    return pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": [o.uid for o in request.obligors],
            "ead": [o.ead for o in request.obligors],
            "pd": [o.pd for o in request.obligors],
            "lgd": [o.lgd for o in request.obligors],
        }
    )


def _loadings_frame(request: CreditVarRequest) -> pd.DataFrame | None:
    if not request.loadings:
        return None
    return pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "uid": [lo.uid for lo in request.loadings],
            "factor": [lo.factor for lo in request.loadings],
            "loading": [lo.loading for lo in request.loadings],
        }
    )


def _input_hash(request: CreditVarRequest) -> str:
    payload = json.dumps(request.model_dump(mode="json"), sort_keys=True).encode()
    return hashlib.sha256(payload).hexdigest()[:16]


def _alpha_key(alpha: float) -> str:
    # Stable, JSON-friendly key (no trailing-zero noise): 0.99 -> "0.99".
    return repr(float(alpha))


def run_credit_var(request: CreditVarRequest | dict) -> CreditVarResult:
    """Run one credit-VaR request end to end.

    Parameters
    ----------
    request
        A `CreditVarRequest`, or a dict validated into one (Pydantic raises
        `ValidationError` with cell-addressable `loc` on bad input).
    """
    req = (
        request
        if isinstance(request, CreditVarRequest)
        else CreditVarRequest.model_validate(request)
    )

    obligor_df = _obligor_frame(req)
    loadings_df = _loadings_frame(req)
    inputs = assemble_inputs(
        model_key=req.model,
        obligor_table=obligor_df,
        params=req.params,
        loadings_table=loadings_df,
    )
    portfolio = compile_jtd_portfolio(bundle=inputs.bundle, masters=inputs.masters)

    universe = inputs.masters.factor_universe
    generator = MixedCreditFactorGenerator.from_universe(
        universe,
        total_scenarios=req.n_scenarios,
        seed=req.seed,
        stress_p_by_uix=inputs.stress_p_by_uix,
    )
    credit_factors = build_credit_factor_matrix(
        generator.iter_batches(batch_size=min(req.n_scenarios, _FACTOR_BATCH)),
        num_factors=universe.n_factors,
        num_scenarios=req.n_scenarios,
    )

    result = run_jtd_for_obligor_ead(
        jtd_portfolio=portfolio,
        obligor_ead=inputs.obligor_ead,
        credit_factors=credit_factors,
    )

    alphas = tuple(req.alphas)
    summary = summarize_jtd_loss(result=result, jtd_portfolio=portfolio, alphas=alphas)
    headline_alpha = max(alphas)
    per_model = model_tail_var_es(
        result=result, jtd_portfolio=portfolio, alpha=headline_alpha
    )

    portfolio_losses = result.loss.sum(axis=0)
    pct = np.percentile(portfolio_losses, _PERCENTILE_GRID)

    return CreditVarResult(
        model=req.model,
        headline_alpha=headline_alpha,
        summary=RiskSummary(
            expected_loss=float(summary.expected_loss),
            var={_alpha_key(a): float(summary.var[a]) for a in alphas},
            es={_alpha_key(a): float(summary.es[a]) for a in alphas},
            expected_loss_by_model={
                k: float(v) for k, v in summary.expected_loss_by_model.items()
            },
        ),
        per_model_tail={
            name: ModelTail(var=float(v), es=float(e))
            for name, (v, e) in per_model.items()
        },
        loss_percentiles={
            repr(float(p)): float(v) for p, v in zip(_PERCENTILE_GRID, pct, strict=True)
        },
        provenance=RunProvenance(
            engine_version=_engine_version(),
            model=req.model,
            params=dict(req.params),
            n_obligors=int(portfolio.total_obligors),
            n_scenarios=req.n_scenarios,
            seed=req.seed,
            alphas=list(req.alphas),
            input_hash=_input_hash(req),
            generated_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        ),
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\solutions\credit_var\schemas.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/solutions/credit_var/schemas.py
"""
Pydantic schemas for the credit-VaR solution.

The wire contract for Emissary (and any JSON client): a request payload in, a
result payload out, both validated and JSON-native. Pydantic does the field- and
cross-field validation; its `ValidationError.errors()` already carries `loc`
paths (e.g. ``("obligors", 2, "ead")``) that a front-end maps straight to a cell,
so this doubles as the structured, cell-addressable validation layer.

Dict-keyed-by-confidence fields (`var`, `es`, `loss_percentiles`) use STRING keys
because JSON object keys are strings — e.g. ``{"0.99": 1234.5}`` — so the result
round-trips through JSON without surprise float-key coercion.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pandas as pd

__all__ = [
    "ObligorRow",
    "LoadingRow",
    "CreditVarRequest",
    "RiskSummary",
    "ModelTail",
    "RunProvenance",
    "CreditVarResult",
]

from pydantic import (
    BaseModel,
    ConfigDict,
    Field,
    field_validator,
    model_validator,
)

from riskquant.solutions.credit_var.catalog import MODEL_CATALOG, model_entry

_STRICT = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", protected_namespaces=())


class ObligorRow(BaseModel):
    """One obligor: the flat desk row."""

    model_config = _STRICT

    uid: int
    ead: float = Field(ge=0.0, description="Exposure at default (>= 0).")
    pd: float = Field(ge=0.0, le=1.0, description="Probability of default in [0, 1].")
    lgd: float = Field(ge=0.0, le=1.0, description="Loss given default in [0, 1].")


class LoadingRow(BaseModel):
    """One systemic-factor loading for a multi-factor model: obligor `uid` loads
    on systemic `factor` with `loading`. The set of distinct `factor` values
    defines the model's systemic factors; per-obligor idiosyncratic weight is the
    engine residual sqrt(1 - sum loading^2)."""

    model_config = _STRICT

    uid: int
    factor: str = Field(min_length=1)
    loading: float = Field(ge=-1.0, le=1.0)


class CreditVarRequest(BaseModel):
    """A single credit-VaR run, as a JSON payload."""

    model_config = _STRICT

    model: str = Field(description="Model key, e.g. 'sf_gaussian'.")
    obligors: list[ObligorRow] = Field(min_length=1)
    params: dict[str, float] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    loadings: list[LoadingRow] | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Systemic loadings for multi-factor models; omit otherwise.",
    )
    n_scenarios: int = Field(default=100_000, gt=0)
    alphas: list[float] = Field(default_factory=lambda: [0.95, 0.99, 0.999])
    seed: int = 12345

    @field_validator("alphas")
    @classmethod
    def _alphas_unit_interval(cls, v: list[float]) -> list[float]:
        if not v:
            raise ValueError("at least one confidence level is required.")
        for a in v:
            if not (0.0 < a < 1.0):
                raise ValueError(f"confidence level {a} must be in (0, 1).")
        return v

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _validate_model_and_params(self) -> CreditVarRequest:
        if self.model not in MODEL_CATALOG:
            raise ValueError(
                f"unknown model '{self.model}'. Supported: {sorted(MODEL_CATALOG)}."
            )
        entry = model_entry(self.model)
        known = {p.key for p in entry.params}
        unknown = set(self.params) - known
        if unknown:
            raise ValueError(
                f"model '{self.model}' does not accept param(s) {sorted(unknown)}. "
                f"Accepted: {sorted(known)}."
            )
        for p in entry.params:
            if p.key in self.params and not p.contains(self.params[p.key]):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"param '{p.key}' = {self.params[p.key]} out of range "
                    f"{p.interval_repr}."
                )
        return self

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _unique_uids(self) -> CreditVarRequest:
        uids = [o.uid for o in self.obligors]
        if len(uids) != len(set(uids)):
            raise ValueError("duplicate obligor uid(s).")
        return self

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _validate_loadings(self) -> CreditVarRequest:
        if self.model not in MODEL_CATALOG:
            return self  # unknown model already reported
        requires = model_entry(self.model).requires_loadings
        if not requires:
            if self.loadings:
                raise ValueError(f"model '{self.model}' does not use a loadings table.")
            return self
        if not self.loadings:
            raise ValueError(
                f"model '{self.model}' requires a non-empty 'loadings' table "
                "(one row per obligor x systemic factor)."
            )
        obligor_uids = {o.uid for o in self.obligors}
        stray = sorted({lo.uid for lo in self.loadings} - obligor_uids)
        if stray:
            raise ValueError(f"loadings reference uid(s) not in obligors: {stray}.")
        uncovered = sorted(obligor_uids - {lo.uid for lo in self.loadings})
        if uncovered:
            raise ValueError(f"obligor(s) have no systemic loadings: {uncovered}.")
        return self

    @classmethod
    def from_dataframe(
        cls,
        obligor_table: pd.DataFrame,
        *,
        model: str,
        loadings_table: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
        n_scenarios: int = 100_000,
        alphas: list[float] | None = None,
        seed: int = 12345,
        **params: float,
    ) -> CreditVarRequest:
        """Build a request from a flat obligor DataFrame (notebook ergonomics).

        The DataFrame needs `uid, ead, pd, lgd`; extra columns are ignored. Model
        params are passed as keywords, e.g.::

            CreditVarRequest.from_dataframe(df, model="sf_gaussian", rho=0.15)

        For multi-factor models, pass `loadings_table` with `uid, factor, loading`::

            CreditVarRequest.from_dataframe(df, model="mf_gaussian", loadings_table=ld)

        Validation is identical to the dict/JSON path — a bad cell still raises a
        `ValidationError` with a `loc` into `obligors` / `loadings`.
        """
        required = ("uid", "ead", "pd", "lgd")
        missing = [c for c in required if c not in obligor_table.columns]
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                f"from_dataframe: obligor_table missing column(s) {missing}."
            )
        obligors = [
            ObligorRow(
                uid=int(rec["uid"]),
                ead=float(rec["ead"]),
                pd=float(rec["pd"]),
                lgd=float(rec["lgd"]),
            )
            for rec in obligor_table.loc[:, list(required)].to_dict("records")
        ]
        kwargs: dict[str, object] = {
            "model": model,
            "obligors": obligors,
            "params": dict(params),
            "n_scenarios": n_scenarios,
            "seed": seed,
        }
        if loadings_table is not None:
            lcols = ("uid", "factor", "loading")
            lmissing = [c for c in lcols if c not in loadings_table.columns]
            if lmissing:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"from_dataframe: loadings_table missing column(s) {lmissing}."
                )
            kwargs["loadings"] = [
                LoadingRow(
                    uid=int(rec["uid"]),
                    factor=str(rec["factor"]),
                    loading=float(rec["loading"]),
                )
                for rec in loadings_table.loc[:, list(lcols)].to_dict("records")
            ]
        if alphas is not None:
            kwargs["alphas"] = list(alphas)
        return cls(**kwargs)


class RiskSummary(BaseModel):
    """Headline portfolio risk metrics."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    expected_loss: float
    var: dict[str, float]  # confidence (as string) -> VaR
    es: dict[str, float]  # confidence (as string) -> ES
    expected_loss_by_model: dict[str, float]


class ModelTail(BaseModel):
    """Standalone tail risk for one model at the headline confidence level."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    var: float
    es: float


class RunProvenance(BaseModel):
    """Audit stamp: enough to reproduce a number."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", protected_namespaces=())

    engine_version: str
    model: str
    params: dict[str, float]
    n_obligors: int
    n_scenarios: int
    seed: int
    alphas: list[float]
    input_hash: str
    generated_at: str


class CreditVarResult(BaseModel):
    """A completed credit-VaR run, JSON-native."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", protected_namespaces=())

    model: str
    headline_alpha: float
    summary: RiskSummary
    per_model_tail: dict[str, ModelTail]
    loss_percentiles: dict[str, float]  # percentile (as string) -> loss
    provenance: RunProvenance
```

```python
# src/riskquant/universe.py
"""
Run-level UIX universe contract.

`UixMapper` is the immutable single source of truth for global-ID -> UIX
translation across a run. It is deliberately a LEAF module: it depends only
on `_arrays` and `constants`, so any tier (portfolio, market_data, simlab)
can import the contract type at runtime without a layering inversion. The
PRODUCER, `build_uix_mapper(ingest_batches)`, stays in `portfolio.engine`
because it consumes portfolio internals (`IngestBatch`); only the contract
type lives here.

This module was extracted from `portfolio.engine` (item C2) specifically to
let `market_data.builder` / `market_data.assemble` drop their `TYPE_CHECKING`
deferral of `UixMapper` — the deferral existed only to dodge the
`market_data -> portfolio` import cycle, which no longer exists now that the
contract type is a leaf.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32
from riskquant.constants import INVALID_UIX


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class UixMapper:
    """
    Immutable execution-space translator.

    This is the single source of truth for global-ID → UIX translation.

    Holds the four global universes touched by the run plus the deduplicated
    FX-effective pair table. All universe arrays are sorted ascending and
    unique; the `__post_init__` validation runs once so the runtime `map_*`
    methods can stay allocation-light and branch-light.

    Field layout
    ------------
    market_id_universe          : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by
                                  prices / forwards / vols / fx-spots.
                                  Indexed by `price_uix`, `vol_uix`,
                                  `underlying_price_curve_uix`, etc.
    discount_curve_id_universe  : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by discount
                                  curves. Kept distinct from `market_id_universe`
                                  because the discount-curve ID range is
                                  disjoint and `MarketDataMatrix.discount_factor_grid`
                                  has its own axis-0 universe.
    date_ord32_universe         : sorted-unique int32 day-ordinals
                                  (days-since-epoch) touched by the run.
                                  Indexed by `*_date_uix` on blocks.
    fx_effective_pairs          : (K, 2) int32 deduplicated `(fx_rf_uix, invert)`
                                  table. `fx_rf_uix` is itself an index into
                                  `market_id_universe`; `invert` is 0 or 1.
                                  The market-data builder turns this into a
                                  `(K, S)` `fx_spots_effective` matrix.
    """

    market_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    discount_curve_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    date_ord32_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    fx_effective_pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # --- INVARIANTS (CHECKED ONCE) -----------------------------------------

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._validate_universe(self.market_id_universe, "market_id_universe")
        self._validate_universe(
            self.discount_curve_id_universe, "discount_curve_id_universe"
        )
        self._validate_universe(self.date_ord32_universe, "date_ord32_universe")
        self._validate_fx_effective_pairs(self.fx_effective_pairs)

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_universe(arr: npt.NDArray[np.int32], name: str) -> None:
        if arr.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"{name} must be int32; got {arr.dtype}.")
        if arr.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be 1-D; got ndim={arr.ndim}.")
        if not arr.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be C-contiguous.")
        if arr.size > 1 and np.any(arr[1:] <= arr[:-1]):
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be strictly increasing and unique.")

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_fx_effective_pairs(pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]) -> None:
        if pairs.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"fx_effective_pairs must be int32; got {pairs.dtype}.")
        if pairs.ndim != 2 or (pairs.size and pairs.shape[1] != 2):
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx_effective_pairs must have shape (K, 2); got {pairs.shape}"
            )
        if not pairs.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError("fx_effective_pairs must be C-contiguous.")

    # --- TRANSLATION API ---------------------------------------------------

    def map_market_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global market IDs into `market_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global discount-curve IDs into
        `discount_curve_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.discount_curve_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_dates_to_uix(
        self,
        ordinals: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global day-ordinals into `date_ord32_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`ordinals < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.date_ord32_universe,
            raw=ordinals,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        self,
        *,
        raw_fx_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        raw_fx_inv: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate per-trade `(raw_fx_id, raw_fx_inv)` into per-trade
        `fx_spots_effective_uix` indices.

        Steps (all vectorized, no Python loop):
          1. Translate `raw_fx_ids` into `market_id_universe` UIXs.
          2. Look the resulting `(fx_uix, invert)` pair up in
             `fx_effective_pairs` to obtain the effective UIX.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_fx_ids == NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        if raw_fx_ids.shape != raw_fx_inv.shape:
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx legs shape mismatch: ids={raw_fx_ids.shape}, "
                f"inv={raw_fx_inv.shape}"
            )

        # Stage 1: market-id universe lookup. Sentinels collapse to INVALID_UIX.
        fx_market_uix = searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_fx_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

        # Stage 2: dedup-pair lookup. We pack each (uix, inv) pair into a
        # single int64 key for a vectorized one-shot searchsorted into the
        # equivalently-packed effective-pairs table.
        n = raw_fx_ids.shape[0]
        out = np.full(n, INVALID_UIX, dtype=np.int32)

        if self.fx_effective_pairs.size == 0:
            return out

        needs_fx = fx_market_uix != INVALID_UIX
        if not needs_fx.any():
            return out

        needed_uix = fx_market_uix[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_inv = raw_fx_inv[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_key = (needed_uix << 1) | needed_inv

        pair_uix = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 0].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_inv = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 1].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_key = (pair_uix << 1) | pair_inv

        idx = np.searchsorted(pair_key, needed_key)
        # Every needed_key MUST exist in pair_key (it was deduplicated from
        # exactly this input). Validate to fail loud on contract drift.
        if idx.size and (
            idx.max() >= pair_key.size or np.any(pair_key[idx] != needed_key)
        ):
            raise RuntimeError(
                "FX-effective dedup contract violated: per-trade pair not "
                "found in fx_effective_pairs. Mapper was built from a "
                "different batch than this one."
            )

        out[needs_fx] = idx.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
        return out
```

```python
from pandas import DataFrame
from pandas.api.types import is_string_dtype


def validate_string_cols(df: DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...] | list[str]) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        if col not in df.columns:
            continue

        s = df[col]

        if not is_string_dtype(s):
            raise ValueError(f"Column '{col}' must be of string dtype.")

        non_na = s.dropna()
        if non_na.empty:
            continue

        # Length
        max_len = non_na.str.len().max()
        if max_len > 255:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains strings longer than 255 characters "
                f"(max={max_len})."
            )

        # Canonical format
        pattern = r"^[A-Z0-9]+(?:_[A-Z0-9]+)*(?:\[(?:[A-Z]+[+-]\d+|(?:ON|TN|SN|\d+[DWMY]))\])?$"
        if not non_na.str.match(pattern).all():
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains non-canonical strings. "
                f"Expected format: UPPERCASE tokens separated by underscores."
            )
```

```python
# riskquant/utils/dates.py
from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
from collections.abc import Iterable
from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import DATE_EPOCH

DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D: Final[np.datetime64] = np.datetime64(
    DATE_EPOCH.isoformat(),
    "D",
)


def coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(
    as_of_date: pd.Timestamp | dt.date | pd.Timestamp | str,
) -> pd.Timestamp:
    """
    Accepts: pd.Timestamp | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | str
    Returns: pd.Timestamp (tz-naive)
    """
    # If str, validate format.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, str):
        assert_date_str_is_iso_format(as_of_date)

    # As pd.Timestamp
    ts = pd.Timestamp(as_of_date)

    # If caller passed a date (no time), treat it as end-of-day inclusive
    # so 2026-01-02 includes all times on 2026-01-02.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, dt.date) and not isinstance(as_of_date, dt.datetime):
        ts = ts + pd.Timedelta(days=1) - pd.Timedelta(nanoseconds=1)  # type: ignore[operator]  # mypy doesn't know Timestamp supports this math

    # Normalize tz handling (if any). Adjust policy as you like.
    if ts.tzinfo is not None:
        ts = ts.tz_convert(None)

    return ts


def is_date_str_valid(date_str: str) -> bool:
    """Checks if date_str which must have form %Y-%m-%d is a valid calendar date."""
    if not isinstance(date_str, str):
        return False
    if len(date_str) != 10 or date_str[4] != "-" or date_str[7] != "-":
        return False
    if not date_str.replace("-", "").isdigit():
        return False

    try:
        pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise")
    except ValueError:
        return False

    return True


def assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str: str) -> None:
    """Assert that date_str is in ISO format 'YYYY-MM-DD'."""
    if not is_date_str_valid(date_str):
        raise ValueError(f"Date string '{date_str}' is not in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format.")


def date_str_to_date(date_str: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert a date string in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format to a datetime.date object."""
    assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str)
    return pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise").date()


def date_to_ord32(d: dt.date) -> np.int32:
    return np.int32((d - DATE_EPOCH).days)


def ordinal32_to_date(days: int) -> dt.date:
    return DATE_EPOCH + pd.Timedelta(days=int(days))  # or datetime.timedelta


def datetime64D_to_ord32(dts: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert numpy datetime64[D] array -> int32 days since DATE_EPOCH.
    Returns a view/copy depending on input; no pandas involvement.
    """
    dts = np.asarray(dts, dtype="datetime64[D]")
    return (dts - DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def ordinal32_to_datetime64D(days: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert int32 days since DATE_EPOCH -> numpy datetime64[D] array.
    """
    days = np.asarray(days, dtype=np.int32)
    # Cast to timedelta64[D] then add epoch
    return DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D + days.astype("timedelta64[D]", copy=False)


def ym_str_to_int_tuple(ym: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Convert 'YYYYMM' -> (YYYY, MM)."""
    if len(ym) != 6 or not ym.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    y, m = int(ym[:4]), int(ym[4:6])
    if not 1 <= m <= 12:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    return y, m


def ymd_str_to_date(ymd: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert 'YYYYMMDD' -> datetime.date."""
    if len(ymd) != 8 or not ymd.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YMD string: {ymd}")
    return dt.date(int(ymd[:4]), int(ymd[4:6]), int(ymd[6:8]))


def month_diff(
    *,
    anchor_ym: tuple[int, int],
    target_ym: tuple[int, int],
) -> int:
    """Difference in months: target - anchor."""
    ay, am = anchor_ym
    ty, tm = target_ym
    return (ty - ay) * 12 + (tm - am)


def next_half_year_boundary(
    t0: dt.date,
    ym_boundaries: Iterable[str] = ("0630", "1231"),
) -> dt.date:
    """
    Return the first boundary date >= t0 among {YYYY0630, YYYY1231} (or configured boundaries).
    If none remain in the year, return the first boundary in the next year.
    """
    ym_bounds = list(ym_boundaries)
    if not ym_bounds:
        raise ValueError("No half-year boundaries provided.")

    year = t0.year
    candidates = []
    for b in ym_bounds:
        if len(b) != 4 or not b.isdigit():
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid year-month boundary 'MMDD': {b}")
        m = int(b[:2])
        d = int(b[2:])
        candidates.append(dt.date(year, m, d))

    for d in sorted(candidates):
        if d >= t0:
            return d

    # none left this year -> first boundary next year (sorted)
    year += 1
    b0 = sorted(ym_bounds)[0]
    return dt.date(year, int(b0[:2]), int(b0[2:]))


def half_year_index(d: dt.date) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """
    Map boundary date to a half-year index:
      YYYY-06-30 -> (YYYY, 0)
      YYYY-12-31 -> (YYYY, 1)
    """
    if (d.month, d.day) == (6, 30):
        return d.year, 0
    if (d.month, d.day) == (12, 31):
        return d.year, 1
    raise ValueError(f"Not a recognized half-year boundary: {d.isoformat()}")


def half_year_diff(
    *,
    anchor: dt.date,
    target: dt.date,
) -> int:
    """Difference in half-years: target - anchor, where each boundary step counts as 1."""
    ay, ah = half_year_index(anchor)
    ty, th = half_year_index(target)
    return (ty - ay) * 2 + (th - ah)


def get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> str:
    # Ensure holidays is a list of strings.
    if holiday_dates is None:
        holiday_dates = []
    if isinstance(holiday_dates, tuple):
        holiday_dates = list(holiday_dates)
    if not isinstance(holiday_dates, list):
        raise TypeError("holiday_dates must be a list!")

    # Create a custom business day with the holidays.
    custom_business_day = pd.offsets.CustomBusinessDay(holidays=holiday_dates)

    # Start from today and apply the lag and one additional business day.
    start_date = dt.datetime.today().date()
    adjusted_date = start_date - custom_business_day * (lag_in_bus_days + 1)
    date_as_str = adjusted_date.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

    # Append the time string if provided, and return.
    if time_str_to_append_to_date is not None:
        return date_as_str + f" {time_str_to_append_to_date}"
    return date_as_str


def get_previous_business_date_as_date(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> dt.date:
    # Get as string.
    date_string = get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
        lag_in_bus_days=lag_in_bus_days,
        holiday_dates=holiday_dates,
        time_str_to_append_to_date=time_str_to_append_to_date,
    )

    # To date.
    return dt.date.fromisoformat(date_string)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\utils\printer.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/utils/printer.py
from __future__ import annotations

import math
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

# ==========================================
# DEFAULT STYLING & ANSI COLORS
# ==========================================
ANSI_CYAN = "\033[96m"
ANSI_GREEN = "\033[92m"
ANSI_FOREST_GREEN = "\033[38;5;28m"  # A rich, medium-dark green (#008700)
ANSI_DEEP_GREEN = "\033[38;5;22m"  # A very dark, subtle green (#005f00)
ANSI_YELLOW = "\033[93m"
ANSI_RED = "\033[91m"
ANSI_MAGENTA = "\033[95m"
ANSI_BOLD = "\033[1m"
ANSI_RESET = "\033[0m"
ANSI_BLUE = "\033[34m"  # Standard Dark/Deep Blue
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLUE = (
    "\033[94m"  # High-intensity Blue (matches the brightness of your other variables)
)
ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE = "\033[38;5;17m"  # Deepest dark blue (#00005f)
ANSI_NAVY_BLUE = "\033[38;5;18m"  # Slightly brighter navy (#000087)

ANSI_BLACK = "\033[30m"
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLACK = "\033[90m"  # Dark gray, great for subdued metadata
ANSI_BLACK_BG = "\033[40m"


DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR = ANSI_BLUE


def print_header(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╭{'─' * width}╮{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}│ {title_} │{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╰{'─' * width}╯{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┏{'━' * width}┓{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┃ {title_} ┃{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┗{'━' * width}┛{ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_subheader(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}  {title_}  {ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD} {'─' * width} {ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_text(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
) -> None:
    import textwrap

    if add_space_before:
        print()
    print(textwrap.fill(text, width=width))
    if add_space_after:
        print()


def as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> str:
    # Convert an iterable of items to a nicely formatted string with each
    # item on its own line, prefixed by "- ".
    items = (str(i) for i in x)  # generator avoids extra allocations
    return "- " + ",\n- ".join(items) + "."


def print_as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> None:
    print(as_list_str(x))


def print_wrapped_text_box(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 1,
    corner_char: str = "+",
    top_border: str = "-",
    bottom_border: str = "-",
    horiz_border: str = "|",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Word‑wrap `text` to `width` characters and display it in an ASCII box."""
    import textwrap

    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)

    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2
    horiz = corner_char + top_border * inner_width + corner_char

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    print(f"{c}{bold}{horiz}{reset}")
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
        )
    print(f"{c}{bold}{corner_char + bottom_border * inner_width + corner_char}{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_success_message(
    message: str | None = None,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    uppercase: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_GREEN,
) -> None:
    """
    Print a success message inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import textwrap

    s = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    if message:
        s += f" | {message}"
    else:
        s += " | SUCCESS! CODE RAN SUCCESSFULLY!"

    text = s.upper() if uppercase else s.capitalize()

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_calling_file(
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_BLACK,
) -> None:
    """
    Print the name of the Python file that called this function
    inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import inspect
    import os
    import textwrap

    frame = inspect.currentframe()
    try:
        caller_frame = frame.f_back if frame else None
        filename = caller_frame.f_code.co_filename if caller_frame else "<unknown>"
        filename = os.path.basename(filename)
    finally:
        # Explicitly delete frame references to avoid reference cycles
        del frame

    dt_str = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    text = f"{dt_str} | RUNNING FILE: {filename}"

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict(
    d: dict[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating]
    | Mapping[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    indent: int = 0,
    indent_step: int = 4,
    sort_keys: bool = False,
    limit: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Pretty-print a dictionary with aligned keys and nested support."""
    items = sorted(d.items()) if sort_keys else list(d.items())
    if limit is not None:
        items = items[:limit]

    try:
        max_key_len = max(len(str(k)) for k in d)
    except ValueError:
        max_key_len = 0

    pad = " " * indent

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    print(pad + "{")
    for key, value in items:
        key_str = str(key)
        key_pad = key_str.ljust(max_key_len)

        if isinstance(value, (Mapping, dict)):
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}:")
            print_dict(
                d=value,
                indent=indent + indent_step * 2,
                indent_step=indent_step,
                sort_keys=sort_keys,
                limit=None,
                add_space_before=False,
                add_space_after=False,
            )
        else:
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}: {value}")

    print(pad + "}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


class _FloatFormatter:
    __slots__ = ("_fmt", "_zero_tol")

    def __init__(self, spec: str, zero_tol: float = 0.0) -> None:
        if "_" in spec:
            clean_spec = spec.replace("_", ",")
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=clean_spec: f"{x:{s}}".replace(",", "_")
        else:
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=spec: f"{x:{s}}"
        self._zero_tol = float(zero_tol)

    def __call__(self, x: float) -> str:
        if x is None or (isinstance(x, float) and math.isnan(x)):
            # Handle NaN alignment for Numpy formats
            try:
                width = int(
                    self._fmt.__defaults__[0]
                    .split(".")[0]
                    .replace(">", "")
                    .replace("<", "")
                )
                return "nan".rjust(width)
            except (ValueError, TypeError, IndexError):
                return "nan"
        if self._zero_tol and abs(x) < self._zero_tol:
            x = 0.0
        try:
            return self._fmt(x)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            return str(x)


def print_df(
    obj: pd.Series | pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a Series/DataFrame with optional display controls."""
    if isinstance(obj, pd.Series):
        name = obj.name if obj.name is not None else "value"
        df = obj.to_frame(name=name)
    else:
        df = obj

    if float_format is not None:
        numeric_cols = df.select_dtypes(
            include=["int64", "int32", "float64", "float32"]
        ).columns
        df = df.copy()
        df[numeric_cols] = df[numeric_cols].astype(float)

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        df_attrs = f"(shape={df.shape})"
        print_header(
            title=f"DataFrame: {title} {df_attrs}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is None:
        print(df.to_string(index=index, max_rows=max_rows, max_cols=max_cols))
    else:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print(
            df.to_string(
                index=index,
                max_rows=max_rows,
                max_cols=max_cols,
                float_format=formatter,
            )
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict_of_dfs(
    d: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    cols_before_transpose: int = 20,
    title_color: str | None = ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE,
    subtitle_color: str | None = ANSI_BLUE,
) -> None:
    """Print a dictionary of DataFrames with optional display controls."""
    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        # Use a slightly different color for the main dictionary wrapper if desired,
        # but defaulting to standard title behavior.
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary of DataFrames: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
        )

    for key, df in d.items():
        if df.shape[1] > cols_before_transpose:
            df = df.T
            key += " (transposed)"
        print_df(
            df,
            title=key,
            index=index,
            max_rows=max_rows,
            max_cols=max_cols,
            float_format=float_format,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
            zero_tol=zero_tol,
            title_color=subtitle_color,
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_array(
    arr: np.ndarray,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = ">10.2f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a NumPy array with perfectly aligned tabular columns and ANSI colors."""
    _array_info = f"(shape={arr.shape}, dtype={arr.dtype})"

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        full_title = f"Array: {title} {_array_info}"
        print_header(
            title=full_title,
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is not None and np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.integer):
        arr_to_print = arr.astype(float)
    else:
        arr_to_print = arr

    print_opts = {
        "suppress": True,
        "linewidth": 200,
    }

    if max_rows is not None or max_cols is not None:
        r_edge = max_rows // 2 if max_rows is not None else 3
        c_edge = max_cols // 2 if max_cols is not None else 3
        print_opts["edgeitems"] = max(r_edge, c_edge)

        row_limit = max_rows if max_rows is not None else arr_to_print.shape[0]
        col_limit = (
            max_cols
            if max_cols is not None
            else (arr_to_print.shape[1] if arr_to_print.ndim > 1 else 1)
        )
        print_opts["threshold"] = row_limit * col_limit - 1

    if float_format is not None:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print_opts["formatter"] = {"float_kind": formatter}

    with np.printoptions(**print_opts):
        print(arr_to_print)

    if add_space_after:
        print()
```

```python
# riskquant/utils/tenors.py
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date
from typing import Optional, Tuple


def parse_bracket_token(tenor_token: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[int]]:
    """
    "[Y-2]" -> ("Y", -2)
    "[M+0]" -> ("M", 0)
    "[D+24]" -> ("D", 24)
    "[HY+1]" -> ("HY", 1)
    "[ON]" -> ("ON", None)   (LABEL)
    """
    # Error.
    if tenor_token is None:
        raise ValueError("tenor_token cannot be None!")

    # Strip white space.
    s = tenor_token.strip()

    # Must start with '[' and end with ']'.
    if s.startswith("[") and s.endswith("]"):
        s = s[1:-1].strip()

    # Find the plus or minus sign.
    plus = s.rfind("+")
    minus = s.rfind("-")
    sign_pos = max(plus, minus)

    # If no sign.
    if sign_pos <= 0:
        return s, None

    # Unit (Y, M, D, HY, etc.)
    unit = s[:sign_pos]

    # Signed offset.
    offset = int(s[sign_pos:])

    # Return.
    return unit, offset


def get_spot_year(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    roll_month: int,
    roll_day: int,
    post_roll_shift: int,
) -> int:
    """
    Defines which delivery year corresponds to Y+0 under ROLL_ON_MMDD.
    """

    # Get the roll date,
    roll_date = date(as_of_date.year, roll_month, roll_day)

    # Compare to as-of-date.
    # As-of-date is on or after the roll date.
    if as_of_date >= roll_date:
        return (
            as_of_date.year + post_roll_shift
        )  # Current year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).

    # As-of-date is before the roll date.
    # Previous year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).
    return (as_of_date.year - 1) + post_roll_shift


def add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01(
    *,
    d: date,
    months_to_add: int,
) -> date:
    """Add months to a month-start date (day=1)."""
    y = d.year + (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) // 12
    m = (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) % 12 + 1
    return date(y, m, 1)


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyy(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymm(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymmdd(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}{d.day:02d}"
```
